We have bowed down to her who is exceedingly gentle and exceedingly fierce; homage, homage to her. Homage to her who is the foundation of the world, to the Devī, to Kṛtyā33, homage, homage.34†

85.12

Sanskrit

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु विष्णुमायेति शब्दिता । नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu viṣṇumāyeti śabditā | namastasyai namastasyai namastasyai namo namaḥ ||

English

To the Devī who is called Viṣṇumāyā35 in all beings, homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

85.13

Sanskrit

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु चेतनेत्यभिधीयते । नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu cetanetyabhidhīyate | namastasyai namastasyai namastasyai namo namaḥ ||

English

To the Devī who is called consciousness (cetanā)36 in all beings, homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

85.14

Sanskrit

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु बुद्धिरूपेण संस्थिता । नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu buddhirūpeṇa saṃsthitā | namastasyai namastasyai namastasyai namo namaḥ ||

English

To the Devī who abides in all beings in the form of intellect (Buddhi)37, homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

85.15

Sanskrit

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु निद्रारूपेण संस्थिता । नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै मनो नमः ॥

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu nidrārūpeṇa saṃsthitā | namastasyai namastasyai namastasyai mano namaḥ ||

English

To the Devī who abides in all beings in the form of Sleep (Nidrā), homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, mano homage38.39†

85.16

Sanskrit

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु क्षुधारूपेण संस्थिता । नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu kṣudhārūpeṇa saṃsthitā | namastasyai namastasyai namastasyai namo namaḥ ||

English

To the Devī who abides in all beings in the form of hunger40, homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

85.17

Sanskrit

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु च्छायारूपेण संस्थिता । नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu cchāyārūpeṇa saṃsthitā | namastasyai namastasyai namastasyai namo namaḥ ||

English

To the Devī who abides in all beings in the form of shadow (chāyā)41, homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.42

85.18

Sanskrit

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु शक्तिरूपेण संस्थिता । नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu śaktirūpeṇa saṃsthitā | namastasyai namastasyai namastasyai namo namaḥ ||

English

To the Devī who abides in all beings in the form of power (Śakti), homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

85.19

Sanskrit

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु तृष्णारूपेण संस्थिता । नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu tṛṣṇārūpeṇa saṃsthitā | namastasyai namastasyai namastasyai namo namaḥ ||

English

To the Devī who abides in all beings in the form of thirst (tṛṣṇā)43, homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.44

85.20

Sanskrit

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु क्षान्तिरूपेण संस्थिता । नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu kṣāntirūpeṇa saṃsthitā | namastasyai namastasyai namastasyai namo namaḥ ||

English

To the Devī who abides in all beings in the form of forbearance (kṣānti45), homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

85.21

Sanskrit

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु जातिरूपेण संस्थिता । नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu jātirūpeṇa saṃsthitā | namastasyai namastasyai namastasyai namo namaḥ ||

English

To the Devī who abides in all beings in the form of jāti46, homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.47

85.22

Sanskrit

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु लज्जारूपेण संस्थिता । नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu lajjārūpeṇa saṃsthitā | namastasyai namastasyai namastasyai namo namaḥ ||

English

To the Devī who abides in all beings in the form of Lajjā48, homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

85.23

Sanskrit

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु शान्तिरूपेण संस्थिता । नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu śāntirūpeṇa saṃsthitā | namastasyai namastasyai namastasyai namo namaḥ ||

English

To the Devī who abides in all beings in the form of Śānti, homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

85.24

Sanskrit

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु श्रद्धारूपेण संस्थिता । नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu śraddhārūpeṇa saṃsthitā | namastasyai namastasyai namastasyai namo namaḥ ||

English

To the Devī who abides in all beings in the form of Śraddhā, homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

85.25

Sanskrit

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु कान्तिरूपेण संस्थिता । नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu kāntirūpeṇa saṃsthitā | namastasyai namastasyai namastasyai namo namaḥ ||

English

To the Devī who abides in all beings in the form of kānti49, homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.

85.26

Sanskrit

या देवी सर्वभूतेषु लक्ष्मीरूपेण संस्थिता । नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥

yā devī sarvabhūteṣu lakṣmīrūpeṇa saṃsthitā | namastasyai namastasyai namastasyai namo namaḥ ||

85.36

Sanskrit

इन्द्रियाणामधिष्ठात्री भूतानां चाखिलेषु या । भूतेषु सततं तस्यै व्याप्तिदेव्यै नमो नमः ॥

indriyāṇāmadhiṣṭhātrī bhūtānāṃ cākhileṣu yā | bhūteṣu satataṃ tasyai vyāptidevyai namo namaḥ ||

English

To her who is the presiding power62 over the senses of beings, and who constantly [pervades] all beings, to her, the Goddess of Pervasion63, homage, homage.64

85.37

Sanskrit

चितिरूपेण या कृत्स्त्रमेतद् व्याप्य स्थिता जगत् । नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमस्तस्यै नमो नमः ॥

citirūpeṇa yā kṛtstrametad vyāpya sthitā jagat | namastasyai namastasyai namastasyai namo namaḥ ||

English

To her who abides pervading this entire world in the form of citi65, homage to her, homage to her, homage to her, homage, homage.66

85.38

Sanskrit

स्तु ता सुरैः पूर्वमभीष्टसंश्रयात् तथासुरेन्द्रेण दिनेषु सेविता । करोतु सा नः शुभहेतुरीश्वरी शुभानि भद्राण्यभिहन्तु चापदः ॥

stu tā suraiḥ pūrvamabhīṣṭasaṃśrayāt tathāsurendreṇa dineṣu sevitā | karotu sā naḥ śubhaheturīśvarī śubhāni bhadrāṇyabhihantu cāpadaḥ ||

English

May she, the Īśvarī, the cause of good, who was praised before by the suras because they had taken refuge in her for what they desired, and who was likewise served day after day by Indra, the lord of the suras, bring about good and auspicious things for us, and strike down calamities.67†

85.39

Sanskrit

या साम्प्रतं चोद्धतदैत्यतापितैर् अस्माभिरीशा च सुरैर्नमस्यते । या च स्मृता तत्क्षणमेव हन्ति नः सर्वापदो भक्तिविनम्कमूर्तिभिः ॥

yā sāmprataṃ coddhatadaityatāpitair asmābhirīśā ca surairnamasyate | yā ca smṛtā tatkṣaṇameva hanti naḥ sarvāpado bhaktivinamkamūrtibhiḥ ||

English

She who is now worshipped as the Sovereign68 by us, the suras, tormented by the arrogant Daityas, with our bodies bowed in devotion, and who, when remembered, destroys all our calamities at that very moment.69†

Sanskrit

ऋषिरूवाच

ṛṣirūvāca

English

85.40The ṛṣi said: While the devas were thus engaged in praise and the rest70, Pārvatī71 came up to that place to bathe in the water of the Jāhnavī72, O delight of kings.73

85.40

Sanskrit

एवं स्तवादियुक्तानां देवानां तत्र पार्वती । स्त्रातुमभ्याययौ तोये जाह्नव्या नृपनन्दन ॥

evaṃ stavādiyuktānāṃ devānāṃ tatra pārvatī | strātumabhyāyayau toye jāhnavyā nṛpanandana ||

English

Its English is given with 85.40, above.

85.41

Sanskrit

साब्रवीत्तान् सुरान् सुभ्रूर्भवद्भिः स्तूयते ऽत्र का । शरीरकोशतश्चास्याः समुद्भूताब्रवीच्छिवा ॥

sābravīttān surān subhrūrbhavadbhiḥ stūyate 'tra kā | śarīrakośataścāsyāḥ samudbhūtābravīcchivā ||

English

She, the fair-browed one, said to those suras: “Who is it that is being praised here by you?” And Śivā74, who had arisen from the sheath of her body75, spoke.76

85.42

Sanskrit

स्तोत्रं ममैतत् क्रियते शुम्भदैत्यनिराकृतैः । देवैः समेतैः समरे निशुम्भेन पराजितैः ॥

stotraṃ mamaitat kriyate śumbhadaityanirākṛtaiḥ | devaiḥ sametaiḥ samare niśumbhena parājitaiḥ ||

English

"This hymn of praise is being made to me by the devas, who have gathered together, driven out by the Daitya Śumbha77 and defeated in battle by Niśumbha."78

85.43

Sanskrit

शरीरकोशाद्यत्तस्याः पार्वत्या निः सृताम्बिका । कौशिकीति समस्तेषु ततो लोकेषु गीयते ॥

śarīrakośādyattasyāḥ pārvatyā niḥ sṛtāmbikā | kauśikīti samasteṣu tato lokeṣu gīyate ||

English

Because Ambikā79 came forth from the sheath of the body80 of that Pārvatī, she is therefore sung of in all the worlds as Kauśikī81.82

85.44

Sanskrit

तस्यां विनिर्गतायां तु कृष्णाभूत् सापि पार्वती । कालिकेति समाख्याता हिमाचलकृताश्रया ॥

tasyāṃ vinirgatāyāṃ tu kṛṣṇābhūt sāpi pārvatī | kāliketi samākhyātā himācalakṛtāśrayā ||

English

When she had come forth, that Pārvatī herself became dark83 (kṛṣṇā). She is known as Kālikā84, and she has made her dwelling on the Himālaya85 mountain.86

85.45

Sanskrit

ततो ऽम्बिकां परं रूपं बिभ्राणां सुमनोहरम् । ददर्श चण्डो मुण्डश्च भृत्यौ शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः ॥

tato 'mbikāṃ paraṃ rūpaṃ bibhrāṇāṃ sumanoharam | dadarśa caṇḍo muṇḍaśca bhṛtyau śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ ||

English

Then Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa87, the two servants of Śumbha and Niśumbha, saw Ambikā88, who bore a supreme and most captivating form.

85.46

Sanskrit

ताभ्यां शुम्भाय चाख्याता अतीव सुमनोहरा । काप्यास्ते स्त्री महाराज भासयन्ती हिमाचलम् ॥

tābhyāṃ śumbhāya cākhyātā atīva sumanoharā | kāpyāste strī mahārāja bhāsayantī himācalam ||

English

She was reported89 by the two of them to Śumbha: "There lives a certain woman, most captivating, O great king90, lighting up the Himālaya mountain."91

85.47

Sanskrit

नैव तादृक् क्वचिद्रूपं दृष्टं केनचिदुत्तमम् । ज्ञायतां काप्यसौ देवी गृह्यतां चासुरेश्वर ॥

naiva tādṛk kvacidrūpaṃ dṛṣṭaṃ kenaciduttamam | jñāyatāṃ kāpyasau devī gṛhyatāṃ cāsureśvara ||

English

"Nowhere has anyone ever seen a form92 of such supreme excellence. Let it be learned who this goddess is, and let her be seized, O lord of the asuras93."94

85.48

Sanskrit

स्त्रीरत्नमतिचार्वङ्गी द्योतयन्ती दिशस्त्विषा । सा तु तिष्ठति दैत्येन्द्र तां भवान् द्रष्टुमर्हति ॥

strīratnamaticārvaṅgī dyotayantī diśastviṣā | sā tu tiṣṭhati daityendra tāṃ bhavān draṣṭumarhati ||

English

"She is a jewel of a woman, with exceedingly beautiful limbs, lighting up the quarters with her radiance. She stands there, O lord of the Daityas95; you ought to see her."96

85.49

Sanskrit

यानि रत्नानि मणयो गजाश्वादीनि वै प्रभो । त्रैलोक्ये तु समस्तानि साम्प्रतं भान्ति ते गृहे ॥

yāni ratnāni maṇayo gajāśvādīni vai prabho | trailokye tu samastāni sāmprataṃ bhānti te gṛhe ||

English

"Whatever jewels97 there are in the three worlds, gems, and elephants, horses and the like, O lord, all of them now shine in your house."98†

85.50

Sanskrit

ऐरावतः समानीतो गजरत्नं पुरन्दरात् । पारिजाततरुश्चायं तथैवोच्चैः श्रवा हयः ॥

airāvataḥ samānīto gajaratnaṃ purandarāt | pārijātataruścāyaṃ tathaivoccaiḥ śravā hayaḥ ||

English

Airāvata99, the elephant-jewel, has been brought from Purandara; and this Pārijāta tree100, and likewise the horse Uccaiḥśravas101, [have been brought as well].102

85.51

Sanskrit

विमानं हंससंयुक्तमेतत्तिष्ठति ते ऽङ्गणे । रत्नभूतमिहानीतं यदासीद्वेधसो ऽद्भुतम् ॥

vimānaṃ haṃsasaṃyuktametattiṣṭhati te 'ṅgaṇe | ratnabhūtamihānītaṃ yadāsīdvedhaso 'dbhutam ||

English

This vimāna103, yoked to swans, which was the wondrous [car] of Vedhas104, stands in your courtyard, brought here as a jewel.105

85.52

Sanskrit

निधिरेष महापद्मः समानीतो धनेश्वरात् । किञ्जल्किनीं ददौ चाब्धिर्मालामम्लानपङ्कजाम् ॥

nidhireṣa mahāpadmaḥ samānīto dhaneśvarāt | kiñjalkinīṃ dadau cābdhirmālāmamlānapaṅkajām ||

English

This treasure, the Mahāpadma106, has been brought from the lord of wealth107; and the ocean gave you a garland, rich in filaments108, whose lotuses never fade.109

85.53

Sanskrit

छत्रं ते वारुणं गेहे काञ्चनस्त्रावि तिष्ठति । तथायं स्यन्तनवरो यः पुरासीत् प्रजापतेः ॥

chatraṃ te vāruṇaṃ gehe kāñcanastrāvi tiṣṭhati | tathāyaṃ syantanavaro yaḥ purāsīt prajāpateḥ ||

English

In your house stands the umbrella of Varuṇa110, which streams gold111; and likewise this excellent chariot, which belonged of old to Prajāpati.112

85.54

Sanskrit

मृत्योरुत्क्रान्तिदा नाम शक्तिरीश त्वया हृता । पाशः सलिलराजस्य भ्रातुस्तव परिग्रहे ॥

mṛtyorutkrāntidā nāma śaktirīśa tvayā hṛtā | pāśaḥ salilarājasya bhrātustava parigrahe ||

English

The power of Death, named Utkrāntidā113, has been carried off by you, O lord; and the noose of the king of the waters114 is in the keeping of your brother115.116

85.55

Sanskrit

निशुम्भस्याब्धिजाताश्च समस्ता रत्नजातयः । वह्निरपि ददौ तुभ्यमग्निशौचे च वाससी ॥

niśumbhasyābdhijātāśca samastā ratnajātayaḥ | vahnirapi dadau tubhyamagniśauce ca vāsasī ||

English

All the kinds of jewels born of the ocean, too, [belong] to Niśumbha117. Fire also gave you two garments purified by fire118.119

85.56

Sanskrit

एवं दैत्येन्द्र रत्नानि समस्तान्याहृतानि ते । स्त्रीरत्नमेषा कल्याणी त्वया कस्मान्न गृह्यते ॥

evaṃ daityendra ratnāni samastānyāhṛtāni te | strīratnameṣā kalyāṇī tvayā kasmānna gṛhyate ||

English

Thus, O lord of the Daityas120, all the jewels have been brought to you. Why is this auspicious woman121, this jewel among women, not taken by you?

Sanskrit

ऋषिरुवाच

ṛṣiruvāca

English

85.57The ṛṣi said: Having heard this speech of Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa, Śumbha, that great asura, then sent Sugrīva122 as a messenger to the Goddess123.

85.57

Sanskrit

निशम्येति वचः शुम्भः स तदा चण्डमुण्डयोः । प्रेषयामास सुग्रीवं दूतं देव्या महासुरः ॥

niśamyeti vacaḥ śumbhaḥ sa tadā caṇḍamuṇḍayoḥ | preṣayāmāsa sugrīvaṃ dūtaṃ devyā mahāsuraḥ ||

English

Its English is given with 85.57, above.

Sanskrit

शुम्भ उवाच

śumbha uvāca

English

85.58Śumbha said: "Having gone, she is to be told 'thus' and 'thus'124 on my word; and you must quickly act in such a way that she comes to me gladly."125

85.58

Sanskrit

इति चेति च वक्तव्या सा गत्वा वचनान्मम । यथा चाभ्येति संप्रीत्या तथा कार्यं त्वया लघु ॥

iti ceti ca vaktavyā sā gatvā vacanānmama | yathā cābhyeti saṃprītyā tathā kāryaṃ tvayā laghu ||

English

Its English is given with 85.58, above.

85.59

Sanskrit

स तत्र गत्वा यत्रास्ते शैलोद्देशे ऽतिशोभने । तां च देवीं ततः प्राह श्लक्ष्णं मधुरया गिरा ॥

sa tatra gatvā yatrāste śailoddeśe 'tiśobhane | tāṃ ca devīṃ tataḥ prāha ślakṣṇaṃ madhurayā girā ||

English

He went there, to the very place where she stayed, a most beautiful spot in the mountains, and then spoke to the Goddess in a gentle, sweet voice.126

Sanskrit

दूत उवाच

dūta uvāca

85.60

Sanskrit

देवि दैत्येश्वरः शुम्भस्त्रैलोक्ये परमेश्वरः । दूतो ऽहं प्रेषितस्तेन त्वत्सकाशमिहागतः ॥

devi daityeśvaraḥ śumbhastrailokye parameśvaraḥ | dūto 'haṃ preṣitastena tvatsakāśamihāgataḥ ||

English

O Devī, Śumbha, lord of the Daityas127, is the supreme sovereign in the three worlds. I am his messenger; sent by him, I have come here into your presence.

85.61

Sanskrit

अव्याहताज्ञः सर्वासु यः सदा देवयोनिषु । निर्जिताखिलदैत्यारिः स यदाह शृणुष्व तत् ॥

avyāhatājñaḥ sarvāsu yaḥ sadā devayoniṣu | nirjitākhiladaityāriḥ sa yadāha śṛṇuṣva tat ||

English

Listen to what he has said, he whose command is always unobstructed among all the classes of gods128, and by whom all the enemies of the Daityas129 have been conquered.130†

85.62

Sanskrit

मम त्रैलोक्यमखिलं मम देवा वशानुगाः । यज्ञभागानहं सर्वानुपाश्नामि पृथक् पृथक् ॥

mama trailokyamakhilaṃ mama devā vaśānugāḥ | yajñabhāgānahaṃ sarvānupāśnāmi pṛthak pṛthak ||

English

"The whole of the three worlds is mine; the devas are mine, obedient to my will. I enjoy all the shares of the yajña131, each separately.132

85.63

Sanskrit

त्रैलोक्ये वररत्नानि मम वश्यान्यशेषतः । तथैव गजरत्नं च हृत्वा देवेन्द्रवाहनम् ॥

trailokye vararatnāni mama vaśyānyaśeṣataḥ | tathaiva gajaratnaṃ ca hṛtvā devendravāhanam ||

English

85.63–64In the three worlds the finest jewels are wholly under my control. Likewise, having taken the jewel of an elephant133 too, the mount of the lord of the devas134, [I hold it]; and the jewel of a horse, named Uccaiḥśravas135, born of the churning of the Ocean of Milk, was presented to me by the immortals, who bowed down.136

85.64

Sanskrit

क्षीरोदमथनोद्भूतमश्वरत्नं ममामरैः । उच्चैः श्रवससंज्ञं तत्प्रणिपत्य समर्पितम् ॥

kṣīrodamathanodbhūtamaśvaratnaṃ mamāmaraiḥ | uccaiḥ śravasasaṃjñaṃ tatpraṇipatya samarpitam ||

English

Its English is given with 85.63–64, above.

85.65

Sanskrit

यानि चान्यानि देवेषु गन्धर्वेषूरगेषु च । रत्नभूतानि भूतानि तानि मय्येव शोभने ॥

yāni cānyāni deveṣu gandharveṣūrageṣu ca | ratnabhūtāni bhūtāni tāni mayyeva śobhane ||

English

And whatever other things that are jewels137 among the devas, the Gandharvas and the serpents138, all those are in my possession alone, O beautiful lady.139†

85.66

Sanskrit

स्त्रीरत्नभूतां त्वां देवि लोके मन्यामहे वयम् । सा त्वमस्मानुपागच्छ यतो रत्नभुजो वयम् ॥

strīratnabhūtāṃ tvāṃ devi loke manyāmahe vayam | sā tvamasmānupāgaccha yato ratnabhujo vayam ||

English

We regard you, O Devī, as the jewel of a woman140 in the world. As such, come to us, since we are enjoyers of jewels.141

85.67

Sanskrit

मां वा ममानुजं वापि निशुम्भमुरुविक्रमम् । भज त्वं चञ्चलापाङ्गि रत्नभूतासि वै यतः ॥

māṃ vā mamānujaṃ vāpi niśumbhamuruvikramam | bhaja tvaṃ cañcalāpāṅgi ratnabhūtāsi vai yataḥ ||

English

Resort to either me or my younger brother Niśumbha, of mighty prowess, O you of restless glances142, since you are a jewel.143

85.68

Sanskrit

परमैश्वर्यमतुलं प्राप्स्यसे मत्परिग्रहात् । एतद् बुद्ध्या समालोच्य मत्परिग्रहतां व्रज ॥

paramaiśvaryamatulaṃ prāpsyase matparigrahāt | etad buddhyā samālocya matparigrahatāṃ vraja ||

English

You will obtain supreme, incomparable sovereignty by becoming my wife144. Having considered this with your intellect145, enter into the state of being my wife.146

Sanskrit

ऋषिरुवाच

ṛṣiruvāca

English

85.69The ṛṣi said: Spoken to in this way, the Devī then answered, smiling deeply within herself147: she, Durgā, the Blessed One, Bhadrā, by whom this world is upheld148.†

85.69

Sanskrit

इत्युक्ता सा तदा देवी गम्भीरान्तः स्मिता जगौ । दुर्गा भगवती भद्रा ययेदं धार्यते जगत् ॥

ityuktā sā tadā devī gambhīrāntaḥ smitā jagau | durgā bhagavatī bhadrā yayedaṃ dhāryate jagat ||

English

Its English is given with 85.69, above.

Sanskrit

देव्युवाच

devyuvāca

English

85.70The Devī said: You have spoken the truth; nothing false has been said by you here. Śumbha is the lord of the three worlds, and Niśumbha too is just like him.149

85.70

Sanskrit

सत्यमुक्तत्वया नात्र मिथ्या किञ्चित्त्वयोदितम् । त्रैलोक्याधिपतिः सुम्भो निशुम्भश्चापि तादृशः ॥

satyamuktatvayā nātra mithyā kiñcittvayoditam | trailokyādhipatiḥ sumbho niśumbhaścāpi tādṛśaḥ ||

English

Its English is given with 85.70, above.

85.71

Sanskrit

किं त्वत्र यत्प्रतिज्ञातं मिथ्या तत्क्रियते कथम् । श्रूयतामल्पबुद्धित्वात् प्रतिज्ञा या कृता पुरा ॥

kiṃ tvatra yatpratijñātaṃ mithyā tatkriyate katham | śrūyatāmalpabuddhitvāt pratijñā yā kṛtā purā ||

English

But here, how can what has been promised be made false? Hear the vow that I made long ago150, through my small understanding151.152

85.72

Sanskrit

यो मां जयति संग्रामे यो मे दर्पं व्यपोहति । यो मे प्रतिबलो लोके स मे भर्ता भविष्यति ॥

yo māṃ jayati saṃgrāme yo me darpaṃ vyapohati | yo me pratibalo loke sa me bhartā bhaviṣyati ||

English

Whoever conquers me in battle, whoever removes my pride, whoever is my match in strength in the world, he will be my husband.

85.73

Sanskrit

तदागच्छतु शुम्भो ऽत्र निशुम्भो वा महासुरः । मां जित्वा किं चिरेणात्र पाणि गृह्णातु मे लघु ॥

tadāgacchatu śumbho 'tra niśumbho vā mahāsuraḥ | māṃ jitvā kiṃ cireṇātra pāṇi gṛhṇātu me laghu ||

English

Therefore let Śumbha come here, or the great asura Niśumbha. Having conquered me, why [wait] here for long? Let him take my hand153 quickly.154

Sanskrit

दूत उवाच

dūta uvāca

85.74

Sanskrit

अवलिप्तासि मैवं त्वं देवि ब्रूहि ममाग्रतः । त्रैलोक्ये कः पुमांस्तिष्ठेदग्रे शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः ॥

avaliptāsi maivaṃ tvaṃ devi brūhi mamāgrataḥ | trailokye kaḥ pumāṃstiṣṭhedagre śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ ||

English

You are full of pride155, Devī; do not speak like this in front of me. Which man in the three worlds could stand before156 Śumbha and Niśumbha?157

85.75

Sanskrit

अन्येषामपि दैत्यानां सर्वे देवा न वै युधि । तिष्ठन्ति संमुखे देवि किं पुनः स्त्री त्वमेकिका ॥

anyeṣāmapi daityānāṃ sarve devā na vai yudhi | tiṣṭhanti saṃmukhe devi kiṃ punaḥ strī tvamekikā ||

English

Even against the other Daityas, all the gods do not stand face to face in battle, Devī; how much less you, a woman, alone?158

85.76

Sanskrit

इन्द्राद्याः सकला देवास्तस्थुर्येषां न संयुगे । शुम्भादीनां कथं तेषां स्त्री प्रयास्यसि संमुखम् ॥

indrādyāḥ sakalā devāstasthuryeṣāṃ na saṃyuge | śumbhādīnāṃ kathaṃ teṣāṃ strī prayāsyasi saṃmukham ||

English

All the gods, with Indra at their head, did not stand in battle against those Śumbha and the others; how, being a woman, will you go face to face against them?159

85.77

Sanskrit

सा त्वं गच्छ मयैवोक्ता पार्श्वं शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः । केशाकर्षणनिर्धूतगौरवा मा गमिष्यसि ॥

sā tvaṃ gaccha mayaivoktā pārśvaṃ śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ | keśākarṣaṇanirdhūtagauravā mā gamiṣyasi ||

English

You, then, having been told by me myself, go to the side of Śumbha and Niśumbha, lest you go160 with your dignity shaken off by being dragged by the hair.161

Sanskrit

देव्युवाच

devyuvāca

English

The Goddess162 said:163

85.78

Sanskrit

एवमेतद् बली शुम्भो निशुम्भश्चातिवीर्यवान् । किं करोमि प्रतिज्ञा मे यदनालोचिता पुरा ॥

evametad balī śumbho niśumbhaścātivīryavān | kiṃ karomi pratijñā me yadanālocitā purā ||

English

So it is: Śumbha is strong, and Niśumbha is exceedingly vigorous. What can I do? I made my vow long ago without reflecting164.165

85.79

Sanskrit

स त्वं गच्छ मयोक्तं ते यदेतत्सर्वमादृतः । तदाचक्ष्वासुरेन्द्राय स च युक्तं करोतु तत् ॥

sa tvaṃ gaccha mayoktaṃ te yadetatsarvamādṛtaḥ | tadācakṣvāsurendrāya sa ca yuktaṃ karotu tat ||

English

So you go. Having taken respectfully to heart all this that I have told you, report it to the lord of the asuras166, and let him do what is fitting in that matter.167

Sanskrit

इति श्रीमार्कण्डेयपुराणेठसावर्णिके मन्वन्तरेऽ देवीमाहात्म्ये पञ्चाशोतितमो ऽध्यायः

iti śrīmārkaṇḍeyapurāṇeṭhasāvarṇike manvantare' devīmāhātmye pañcāśotitamo 'dhyāyaḥ

English

Thus ends the eighty-fifth chapter168, in the Devīmāhātmya, in the Sāvarṇika manvantara, in the revered Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa.169†

  1. The reading: The line is a chapter heading, not a verse: pañcāśītitamo 'dhyāyaḥ, 'the eighty-fifth chapter', followed by the numeral 85. The numeral is not translated separately. The sandhi form 'dhyāyaḥ stands for adhyāyaḥ. The chapter numbering follows the e-text's own count, which differs from the preceding verse labels (84.x and 684.x). ↩
  2. the shares of the yajña: Yajñabhāga: the portions of the sacrificial offerings due to the devas. ↩
  3. the lord of Śacī: Śacīpati, 'husband of Śacī', is Indra, king of the devas. ↩
  4. The reading: madabalāśrayāt: (1) mada and bala as a dvandva, 'pride and strength'; (2) a tatpuruṣa, 'strength born of intoxication/pride'. I chose the dvandva as the simpler reading, with āśraya as 'reliance, resort to' (ablative of cause or means). The glossary's mada = rut does not suit here, so mada is taken as 'pride, intoxication'. śacīpateḥ: the form can be genitive or ablative; with hṛ the sense is separation, 'taken from', so I made this explicit and put the sentence in the passive, as in the Sanskrit. hṛtā: the feminine singular does not agree with trailokyam (neuter) or yajñabhāgāḥ (masculine plural). I take it as a loose or metrical form covering both subjects, so both were taken. I disagree with the reviewer that it agrees with trailokyam by attraction, since trailokyam is neuter. Śumbha is taken as one of the two asuras, not as the glossary's separate slayer of Puṣkaramālin. ↩
  5. the office of the sun: Sanskrit sūryatā and adhikāra: the sun's rank and function, that is, his authority over his sphere. The same 'office' governs the other gods named in the verse. ↩
  6. Kubera: Kubera is the god of wealth and guardian of the north. The text has the adjective kaubera, 'of Kubera'. ↩
  7. The reading: adhikāra is taken as the object of cakrāte for all the genitives and adjectives: sūryatām (the sun's state or office), aindavam (the moon's), kauberam, yāmyam, and Varuṇa's. The verb cakrāte (dual) agrees with tau, the two asuras Śumbha and Niśumbha of 85.1. Because the sentence continues in 85.3, 'took over' is a rendering of the sense of the verb 'made' (kṛ), meaning appropriated. ↩
  8. the power of the wind: pavanardhi: ṛddhi means prosperity, success or power. It is the wind-god's share of authority, parallel to the 'office' (adhikāra) of the others. ↩
  9. he himself presides: The singular sa and adhitiṣṭhati follow the dual 'those two' (tau). The singular may be a loose generalisation, or it may point to one of the brothers, presumably Śumbha. ↩
  10. The reading: sa (singular) and adhitiṣṭhati (singular) after the dual subject tau. Readings: (a) one of the two brothers, probably Śumbha, as the chief; (b) a loose singular standing for both. I kept the singular as written and did not name anyone, since the verse does not. The verse ends with participles (vinirdhūtāḥ, bhraṣṭarājyāḥ, parājitāḥ) without a finite verb. I supplied 'were', and 85.4 then continues with a new clause. ↩
  11. unconquered: Aparājitā, 'the Unconquered', is a name of the Devī. Here it qualifies the goddess the devas remember. ↩
  12. When I am remembered: The Sanskrit has no 'iti'. The devas' words pass into the Devī's own promise, with first-person nāśayiṣyāmi and smṛtā (feminine) in the promise, and asmākam and bhavatām in the report of the gift. ↩
  13. The reading: yathā in 85.5 can mean 'that' (introducing the boon), 'when', or 'in such a way that'. I took it as introducing the content of the boon, which the colon and quotation marks render, and gave smṛtā its temporal sense. The text has akhilāḥ (nominative plural feminine) agreeing with paramāpadaḥ, so 'all ... calamities'. Some editions may read the verse a little differently. ↩
  14. Himavān: The Himālaya, personified as the king of mountains and father of the Goddess in her form as Pārvatī. ↩
  15. Viṣṇumāyā: 'Viṣṇu's māyā': the Goddess as the power of illusion belonging to Viṣṇu. She is the same Devī as the unconquered one remembered in verse 4. ↩
  16. The reading: The sense of 'iti kṛtvā matiṃ' is 'having formed this resolve'. 'Iti' refers to the plan stated in the preceding verses: to remember the Devī and seek her help. 'Tatra' is taken with 'jagmuḥ' as 'there'. Himavantam is accusative of motion, so 'went to Himavān'. 'Nageśvaram' is in apposition, 'lord of mountains'. ↩
  17. Prakṛti: Here the Goddess is addressed as prakṛti, primal nature, the creative source of the world; the word is capitalised as her name, not as the technical term of the glossary. ↩
  18. the auspicious one: Bhadrā, 'auspicious, gracious', an epithet of the Goddess; the vocative-like dative bhadrāyai is kept here as an epithet. ↩
  19. Disciplined: niyatāḥ, 'restrained, self-controlled'; it qualifies the devas, who speak. The perfect sense 'have bowed down' renders praṇatāḥ sma, a participle with the auxiliary asmi in the present. ↩
  20. The reading: prakṛtyai may mean 'to Nature' (the creative principle) or just 'to the original one'; I kept Prakṛti as a name of the Goddess. bhadrāyai could be taken as a name 'Bhadrā' or an adjective 'auspicious'; I took it as an epithet. praṇatāḥ sma is the present of a participle with sma, and could be rendered 'we bow down'; I chose 'have bowed down' to keep the participle. ↩
  21. the fierce one: Raudrā, 'terrible, fierce', the Goddess in her formidable aspect, from Rudra. ↩
  22. Dhātrī: 'She who bears, nourishes and sustains'; the Goddess as supporter and nurse of all beings. ↩
  23. Kṛti: 'Action, creation, accomplishment'. The form kṛtyai may also be read as kṛtyā, 'a magical power or spirit', but 'Kṛti' suits the context of the praise. ↩
  24. The reading: nityāyau in the e-text is a misprint for nityāyai (dative singular feminine), which I follow. kṛtyai could be from kṛti ('action, creation') or from kṛtyā ('magical power, a spirit'); I chose Kṛti as the more fitting epithet in this hymn of praise. ↩
  25. Moonlight: The text reads dyotstrāyai, which is not a Sanskrit word. It is probably a misprint for jyotsnāyai, 'to Moonlight' (jyotsnā). The Goddess is addressed as moonlight and, in the next word, as having the moon's form. ↩
  26. Bowed down: The printed text has praṇatāṃ, which does not parse here. It is emended to praṇatāḥ, nominative plural, agreeing with the devas who are the subject of kurmaḥ; compare praṇatāḥ smo in 85.7. ↩
  27. Kalyāṇī: Kalyāṇī, 'the auspicious one', is a name of the Goddess. It is kept in IAST to keep it apart from Bhadrā and Śivā, which also mean 'auspicious'. Vṛddhi ('growth') and Siddhi ('accomplishment') that follow are also the Goddess, addressed as personified powers. ↩
  28. The reading: (1) dyotstrāyai is not a Sanskrit word. I read jyotsnāyai, 'to Moonlight', which suits the indurūpiṇyai that follows. (2) praṇatāṃ is a misprint or corruption. I read praṇatāḥ, nominative plural, agreeing with the devas who are the subject of kurmaḥ; the accusative singular praṇatām has nothing to agree with. I now record this emendation in a note. (3) cendurūpiṇyai is ca + indurūpiṇyai, and the 'and' is now shown. (4) The reviewer notes that the glossary has ṛddhi, but the text reads vṛddhi. I keep Vṛddhi, 'growth', because ṛddhi is a different word and is not what the text says. ↩
  29. Nairṛtī: Feminine of Nairṛta, from Nirṛti, the power of dissolution and misfortune. Here it is a name of the Goddess. It could also be read as 'she of the Rākṣasas', who are called Nairṛta. ↩
  30. Lakṣmī of kings: bhūbhṛtāṃ lakṣmyai: the Goddess as the fortune or royal splendour of kings. bhūbhṛt, literally 'earth-bearer', means both 'king' and 'mountain'. 'Lakṣmī of mountains' is the alternative reading. ↩
  31. to you: te is the enclitic dative of the second-person pronoun (equivalent to tubhyam). The devas turn from the third person to address the Goddess directly. The sense of the particular word is slightly uncertain here, since the surrounding verses keep to the third person. ↩
  32. The reading: te: the reviewer calls it a corrupt reading or stray particle and says te cannot be a dative. I disagree. te is the regular enclitic dative (and genitive) of yuṣmad, so 'to you' is grammatical, and it is retained. I have hedged the claim about a shift of person in the note, because the neighbouring verses use the third person. bhūbhṛtām: 'kings' or 'mountains'. I kept 'kings' because Lakṣmī is the fortune of kings, and I give the alternative in the note. nairṛtyai: the name Nairṛtī (from Nirṛti) or 'she of the Rākṣasas'; I kept the name. Sārā is kept as a name, with its sense of 'essence' in the glossary. The verse as printed has three pādas in its second line, which is probably a feature of the e-text. ↩
  33. Kṛtyā: A name of the Goddess, taken here as 'action' or 'the active power'. The form kṛtyai could also belong to the stem kṛtī or kṛti. The word is also the name of a female spirit raised by ritual. ↩
  34. The reading: The first word of the second line, mano, is probably a misprint for namo, which is the reading in the parallel hymn lines. Mano could be read as manas, 'mind', but that gives no construction. I read it as namo and translated 'homage'. The dative kṛtyai could come from kṛtyā, kṛtī or kṛti. I chose Kṛtyā because it is the usual form in this hymn. ↩
  35. Viṣṇumāyā: Literally 'the illusion-power (māyā) of Viṣṇu'; here the Goddess is named by this title as she is present in all living beings. ↩
  36. consciousness (cetanā): Cetanā is awareness, the sentient principle in living beings. Here it is one of the forms in which the Goddess is praised as present in all creatures. ↩
  37. intellect (Buddhi): Buddhi, the faculty of discernment and awareness, is here one of the forms in which the Goddess is present in beings. The glossary already treats Buddhi as a name of the Goddess. ↩
  38. mano homage: The text reads mano namaḥ where the refrain in the neighbouring verses has namo namaḥ. The word mano is probably a corrupt or variant form of namo; it is left untranslated here. ↩
  39. The reading: mano namaḥ (for the refrain namo namaḥ): mano is most likely a corruption or a metrical or scribal variant of namo, as in verses 85.12-14 and 85.16-17. Another reading takes manas ('mind') as an accusative or compound element, but that gives no sense in the refrain. I keep the word as printed, leave it untranslated and flag it, rather than silently emending the line to 'homage, homage'. ↩
  40. hunger: Sanskrit kṣudhā. This is the first of the forms in which the Goddess is praised that is a bodily need (hunger), rather than a mental power. ↩
  41. shadow (chāyā): chāyā means shadow, shade or reflection; it is also the name of Sūryā's double in the Sūrya myth. Here it is one of the forms in which the Goddess dwells in beings. ↩
  42. The reading: chāyā can mean 'shadow', 'shade', 'reflection' or 'lustre'. I chose 'shadow' as the usual sense; the verse gives no further context. ↩
  43. thirst (tṛṣṇā): tṛṣṇā means thirst and, by extension, craving or desire. The verse does not say which sense is meant; 'thirst' is chosen as the primary meaning, following the pairing with hunger (kṣudhā) in the preceding verses. ↩
  44. The reading: tṛṣṇā can mean literal thirst or, more broadly, craving or desire. I chose 'thirst' because it follows kṣudhā (hunger) in the sequence of the hymn; 'craving' is also possible. ↩
  45. kṣānti: Patience, forbearance, or forgiveness; the capacity to endure and to pardon. Here it is one of the forms in which the Goddess abides in beings. ↩
  46. jāti: Birth, lineage, or class: the kind or species into which a being is born, and with it the distinctive character of that kind. The word has more than one sense, and the verse does not say which is meant. ↩
  47. The reading: jāti can mean birth, species or class, lineage, or generic nature. Because no single English word covers these senses, I kept it in IAST between asterisks and explained it in a note instead of choosing one. ↩
  48. Lajjā: Lajjā is shame or modesty, here a form in which the Goddess abides in beings. The word is kept in IAST, as the glossary has it. ↩
  49. kānti: Radiance, loveliness or beauty; also read as desire or charm. Here it is a form in which the Goddess dwells in beings. ↩
  50. dhṛti: Steadiness, constancy, firmness of mind; fortitude or resolve that sustains a being. ↩
  51. homage to her: The text prints 'tamastasyai' for 'namastasyai'. This is an evident misprint, since the refrain is the same in every verse of the hymn. It is not the word tamas. ↩
  52. The reading: The e-text reads 'tamastasyai' (tamas + tasyai, 'darkness to her'), but this is a misprint for 'namastasyai', as in the identical refrain of verses 85.21-26 and 85.28-29. I have read 'namas' and translated it as the refrain 'homage to her'. The word tamas in the glossary is not intended here. ↩
  53. vṛtti: A word with several senses: activity, mode of being, livelihood or means of subsistence, function. The verse does not say which is meant, so the term is kept in IAST. ↩
  54. The reading: vṛtti can mean activity or function, livelihood or means of subsistence, or mode of being or conduct. The verse gives no context to decide among these, so I kept it as vṛtti and explained the range in a note instead of choosing one sense. ↩
  55. smṛti: Memory, remembrance; also what is remembered or recollected. Here a form in which the Goddess abides in beings. ↩
  56. nīti: nīti: guidance, prudent conduct, polity; the wisdom by which one rightly leads oneself and others. It is kept in Sanskrit because 'policy' or 'prudence' each cover only part of it. ↩
  57. The reading: The printed text reads nītirūpeṇaṃ, with an anusvāra that does not fit the instrumental singular. I have read nītirūpeṇa, as in the parallel verses, with the final -ṃ a misprint or a metrical or sandhi intrusion. ↩
  58. Tuṣṭi: Tuṣṭi means contentment or satisfaction; here it is one of the forms in which the Goddess dwells in beings. ↩
  59. The reading: tuṣṭi is a common noun, 'contentment', and the glossary also lists Tuṣṭi as a deity. I kept it as the name Tuṣṭi in line with the glossary, so as to keep the series consistent with the other forms in this hymn (Lakṣmī, etc.). ↩
  60. error: bhrānti: wandering of the mind, delusion, mistaken perception. Some editions read kānti (radiance) in this verse. ↩
  61. The reading: bhrānti can mean error, delusion, confusion or bewilderment. I chose 'error' as the plainest rendering of the word for mistaken perception. The reading is the one in this e-text; some versions have kānti, but I translate the text as given. ↩
  62. presiding power: adhiṣṭhātrī: the one who stands over a thing and governs it. Here the Goddess is the ruling power behind the senses (indriya). ↩
  63. the Goddess of Pervasion: vyāptidevyai: a compound of vyāpti ('pervasion', 'the state of filling everything') and devī. It stands in apposition to tasyai. ↩
  64. The reading: bhūtānām: my earlier draft took it as a second object of adhiṣṭhātrī and rendered it 'elements'. The reviewer is right that this left bhūta with two renderings and had no support in the glossary. I now take bhūtānām with indriyāṇām, 'the senses of beings', and keep 'beings' for bhūta throughout, so bhūteṣu is 'in beings'. A commentarial alternative reads bhūtānām as 'of the elements', governed by adhiṣṭhātrī, but I do not adopt it. On this construal ca joins the participial clause (she presides) with the relative clause of her presence in all beings (akhileṣu bhūteṣu). The verb is not in the Sanskrit and is bracketed. I chose '[pervades]' rather than 'abides', as the reviewer suggests, because the epithet vyāptidevyai points to pervasion. The parallel verses have saṃsthitā, so 'abides' would also be defensible. vyāptidevyai could be the name 'to the goddess Vyāpti', but I took it as a descriptive epithet in apposition to tasyai. ↩
  65. citi: Consciousness, intelligence; the Goddess as the conscious principle that pervades the whole world. ↩
  66. The reading: The verse has no 'sarvabhūteṣu' and no 'devī'; the relative pronoun yā is the subject. 'Kṛtstram etad ... jagat' is the object of vyāpya, taken as 'this entire world'. Citi could be rendered 'consciousness', but I keep it in IAST as a technical term with a note. ↩
  67. The reading: 1. The e-text prints 'stu tā', a misprint for stutā ('praised'). 2. 'tathāsurendreṇa' can be read as tathā + asurendreṇa ('by the lord of the asuras') or, by sandhi (tathā + surendreṇa), as 'by Surendra', i.e. Indra. I chose Indra, lord of the suras: the verse sets the suras' praise beside the service of their lord, and an asura lord serving the Goddess would be out of place here. 3. 'abhīṣṭasaṃśrayāt' (ablative) could be 'because of resorting to her for desired ends' or 'from the attainment of what was desired'. I took saṃśraya as 'taking refuge'. 4. 'śubhāni bhadrāṇi' is taken as the object of karotu, with 'āpadaḥ' as the object of abhihantu. ↩
  68. the Sovereign: Īśā, the feminine of Īśa, 'lord'; here an epithet of the Goddess, the same sense as Īśvarī in the preceding verse. ↩
  69. The reading: The printed 'bhaktivinamkamūrtibhiḥ' looks like a misprint for 'bhaktivinamramūrtibhiḥ' ('with bodies bowed in devotion'); I have read it so. The instrumental plural agrees with 'asmābhiḥ' ('by us'), and so with 'suraiḥ'. 'Īśā' with 'ca' could also be taken as 'and as sovereign'; I took it as the Goddess's title, 'as the Sovereign'. The verse is a relative clause whose main clause is 'karotu sā' in 85.38, so the English opens with 'She who' and has no main verb of its own. ↩
  70. praise and the rest: stavādiyuktānām: 'engaged in praise and so forth', that is, hymns and the accompanying acts of worship. ↩
  71. Pārvatī: 'Daughter of the mountain' (Himālaya); a name of the Goddess Umā, consort of Śiva. In the next verses a second form, Śivā, issues from her bodily sheath. ↩
  72. Jāhnavī: The Gaṅgā (Ganges), so called as the daughter of Jahnu. ↩
  73. The reading: The first line is printed as 'ṛṣirūvāca', a misprint for ṛṣir uvāca; I read it so. The verse number 689.1 is evidently an e-text artefact. In 'abhyāyayau' (a form of abhi-ā-yā) the reading is clear. 'tatra' could go with 'devānām' (there, among the devas) or with the coming; I took it as 'to that place', where the devas were. ↩
  74. Śivā: 'The auspicious one'. She is the Goddess who has come forth from Pārvatī's body, later called Ambikā and Kauśikī (85.43). ↩
  75. the sheath of her body: The Goddess emerges from the sheath (kośa) of Pārvatī's body, as 85.43 says again. Her later name Kauśikī is explained from this kośa. ↩
  76. The reading: śarīrakośataḥ: kośa can mean 'treasury' or 'sheath, casing'. The reviewer's reading 'sheath' is accepted. The glossary's 'royal treasury' suits kośa in the sense of a king's wealth but not a body, and 'sheath' is the sense on which 85.43 builds the name Kauśikī. I have therefore departed from the glossary here. asyāḥ is taken as Pārvatī, the subject of sābravīt (sā), and the fair-browed one (subhrūḥ) is the same Pārvatī. Śivā's reply is not given in this verse, so the second abravīt is rendered 'spoke' without an object. ↩
  77. the Daitya Śumbha: The compound śumbhadaityanirākṛtaiḥ can also be read as 'driven out by Śumbha and the Daityas'. Here it is taken as 'by the Daitya Śumbha', in line with the glossary's note that Śumbha's identity is unclear. ↩
  78. The reading: śumbhadaityanirākṛtaiḥ: (a) 'driven out by the Daitya Śumbha' (karmadhāraya/appositional), or (b) 'driven out by Śumbha and the Daityas' (dvandva). I chose (a) because the verse goes on to name only Niśumbha as the one who defeated them, which suggests a single named agent in the first half too. Reading (b) is also possible. ↩
  79. Ambikā: 'Little Mother', a name of the Goddess; here the goddess who has issued from Pārvatī's body and is identified with Śivā in the preceding verses. ↩
  80. sheath of the body: Sanskrit śarīrakośa; kośa is a sheath, covering or receptacle. It is a different word from kośa 'treasury' in the glossary. ↩
  81. Kauśikī: 'She of the sheath' (from kośa), in the sense given in this verse: the goddess born from Pārvatī's bodily sheath. ↩
  82. The reading: The construction is yad ... tato ('because ... therefore'). The verse says only that the Goddess came out of Pārvatī's sheath-body. The explanation of the name Kauśikī is implied by kośa and is not stated directly, so I have kept it in the note and not in the translation. ↩
  83. dark: kṛṣṇā, 'the dark one', is the colour the Goddess takes on once Kauśikī has left her body. ↩
  84. Kālikā: 'The black one', a name of the dark form of Pārvatī, derived from the same sense as kṛṣṇā. ↩
  85. Himālaya: himācala, 'the Himālaya mountain', is literally 'the mountain of snow'. ↩
  86. The reading: The verse can be read as 'she had made the Himālaya her dwelling' (kṛtāśrayā as a bahuvrīhi, 'one by whom the dwelling was made on Himācala'). I take himācala as the place of her dwelling. Also, 'tasyām vinirgatāyām' is a locative absolute referring to Ambikā/Kauśikī, whom I render simply as 'she'. ↩
  87. Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa: Two asura generals in the service of Śumbha and Niśumbha. They report Ambikā's beauty to Śumbha in the verses that follow. ↩
  88. Ambikā: 'Little mother', a name of the Goddess. Here it refers to Kauśikī, who came forth from Pārvatī's body. ↩
  89. She was reported: The feminine participle ākhyātā agrees with the woman (strī) of the quoted report, so the sense is 'she was reported'. The words after it are the report itself, and the speech is not introduced by a verb of saying. ↩
  90. O great king: Mahārāja is the form of address Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa use to Śumbha in their report. It is not addressed to Suratha, the listener of the narrative. ↩
  91. The reading: kāpi āste: the Sanskrit is ambiguous in tense and person, but 'there lives a certain woman' is the natural reading. The mixed-gender ākhyātā is taken as the feminine nominative singular, 'she was reported', agreeing with strī. Mahārāja is taken as addressed to Śumbha, within the report made by Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa. ↩
  92. form: Rūpa may mean 'form' or 'beauty'; here it is taken as the Goddess's bodily form, as in the preceding verses. ↩
  93. lord of the asuras: Asureśvara, a vocative addressed to Śumbha. ↩
  94. The reading: Kāpy asau devī: kā api asau devī, 'this goddess, whoever she is' (kā api taken as indefinite 'some'/'whoever'). I rendered 'who this goddess is' with jñāyatām, 'let it be learned'. Devī may be a mere title for a lady (or an actual goddess); I kept 'goddess' as the text has it. ↩
  95. lord of the Daityas: Daityendra, a vocative addressed to Śumbha. ↩
  96. The reading: The verse is a continuation of the speech of Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa; I keep the quotation marks, closing the quotation here although the speech continues in 85.49. Tu in 'sā tu' is treated as merely emphatic and left out. 'Aticārvaṅgī' is rendered 'with exceedingly beautiful limbs'; strīratnam is taken as predicate of the following 'sā'. ↩
  97. jewels: ratna: the finest thing of its kind, such as the best elephant or horse; the word also underlies strīratna in the preceding verse. ↩
  98. The reading: Prabho is a vocative, 'O lord', addressed to Śumbha by Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa. The glossary's gloss for prabhu ('addressed to the prince Puṣkaramālin') does not fit this context. In the Sanskrit, maṇayo may stand in apposition to ratnāni ('jewels, namely gems') or be a separate item ('jewels, gems, and elephants...'). I took it as a separate item. The particle vai is left out as merely emphatic, and tu is also left out. ↩
  99. Airāvata: The white elephant of Indra, who emerged at the churning of the ocean and is the foremost of elephants. ↩
  100. Pārijāta tree: One of the five trees of Indra's heaven, also produced at the churning of the ocean. ↩
  101. Uccaiḥśravas: Indra's horse, 'of lofty hearing' or 'of lofty fame', also produced at the churning of the ocean. ↩
  102. The reading: The verse has no finite verb in the second half; samānītaḥ is understood with pārijātataruḥ and uccaiḥśravā hayaḥ. The text reads uccaiḥ śravā as two words; it is the name Uccaiḥśravas, as in the parallel passages, and I have taken it so. The pronoun ayam may be read as pointing to the tree 'here', as I have done, since the verses go on to describe what stands in Śumbha's house. ↩
  103. vimāna: A divine aerial car, moving at will, belonging to a god. ↩
  104. Vedhas: Epithet of Brahmā ('creator'). The text says the car had been his. ↩
  105. The reading: 1. Yad āsīd vedhaso 'dbhutam: I accept the reviewer's point. Adbhutam is an adjective, 'wondrous', agreeing with yat and vimānam, and vedhasaḥ is a possessive genitive. I now translate 'which was the wondrous [car] of Vedhas', with 'car' in brackets because the Sanskrit implies it. The draft's 'wonder of Vedhas' made adbhutam a noun and is dropped. 2. Ratnabhūtam: it could be 'being a jewel' or 'as a jewel', i.e. as a treasure. I keep 'brought here as a jewel', taking the participle with vimānam. The relative clause now comes before the main clause, so the two no longer run together awkwardly. 3. Haṃsasaṃyuktam: I changed 'yoked with' to 'yoked to', as the reviewer asked for a clearer rendering. ↩
  106. Mahāpadma: One of the nine treasures (nidhi) of Kubera, the lord of wealth; the name means 'great lotus'. ↩
  107. the lord of wealth: Dhaneśvara, a name of Kubera, the god of wealth. ↩
  108. rich in filaments: Kiñjalkinī, 'having filaments', is feminine and agrees with mālā, the garland; it refers to the filaments of the lotuses. ↩
  109. The reading: kiñjalkinīm is taken as an adjective agreeing with mālām ('rich in filaments'). It could instead be a noun, 'a garland named Kiñjalkinī', but the adjectival reading fits the construction better. ↩
  110. the umbrella of Varuṇa: Varuṇa, lord of the waters, is the owner of this royal parasol; it is among the jewels taken from the gods and carried off to Śumbha's house. ↩
  111. which streams gold: kāñcanastrāvi, 'flowing with gold', i.e. it rains down a shower of gold. ↩
  112. The reading: syantanavara can be read as 'excellent chariot' (a tatpuruṣa/karmadhāraya with vara, best), which I have chosen; no alternative proper name is implied. vāruṇam could mean 'of Varuṇa' or 'Varuṇa's' (the same sense). The verse does not name the owner as the possessor 'you', but te is carried from the first half in the sense of 'in your house'. ↩
  113. Utkrāntidā: 'Giver of departure': the name of Death's power, which causes life to depart from the body. It is here treated as a weapon. ↩
  114. the king of the waters: Salilarāja, an epithet of Varuṇa, whose noose (pāśa) is his characteristic weapon. ↩
  115. your brother: Niśumbha, Śumbha's brother, who is named in the next verse. ↩
  116. The reading: īśa is taken as an ordinary vocative, 'O lord', addressed to Śumbha, not as the glossary's Īśa for Śiva. Śumbha is the one addressed throughout this passage. Mṛtyoḥ could be read as the genitive of the god Mṛtyu or of death in general. I chose 'of Death' to cover both. ↩
  117. [belong] to Niśumbha: The verse has only the genitive niśumbhasya and no verb. The sense of possession is taken from parigrahe ('in the keeping') in the previous verse, where the brother is not named. He is presumably Niśumbha, but the text does not say so. ↩
  118. two garments purified by fire: agniśauca: cloth cleansed in fire instead of by washing, a mark of divine fabric. ↩
  119. The reading: (1) The first half has no verb. I supply '[belong]' on the model of parigrahe in 85.54 and read niśumbhasya as a possessive genitive. 'Are in the keeping of' would be almost the same sense. The previous verse does not name the brother, so I no longer claim that he is Niśumbha. (2) I keep agniśauce as a dual neuter accusative agreeing with vāsasī, as an adjective or bahuvrīhi 'having purification by fire', hence 'two garments purified by fire'. The reviewer would take it as a locative ('for fire-cleansing'), which is possible, but the dual ending -e matches vāsasī, and the locative leaves ca and the construction awkward. My earlier doubt wrongly called it a locative that I was reading as a compound. (3) The ca after agniśauce joins this gift to the preceding gifts, as does api in the same verse. English does not need a second 'and', so I render the pair as 'also'. (4) I keep the first ca as 'too' so that no new clause is opened. (5) vahni is rendered 'Fire' as in the glossary, which ide ↩
  120. lord of the Daityas: Daityendra, the address to Śumbha, who is being urged by his counsellors to seize the Goddess. ↩
  121. auspicious woman: Kalyāṇī, 'the auspicious one', here used of the Goddess, whom the speakers do not yet recognise as such. ↩
  122. Sugrīva: An asura chosen by Śumbha as his envoy; he is not otherwise identified in this text. The name means 'fair-necked'. ↩
  123. the Goddess: Devī, the Goddess, who here is the woman Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa have described to Śumbha. ↩
  124. 'thus' and 'thus': iti ceti ca: the quotation-marks 'iti ... iti' stand for the message that Śumbha has just given to Sugrīva, or has yet to give; the verse does not repeat its content. ↩
  125. The reading: Verse 85.58 is the end of a longer speech whose earlier verses are not given here; 'iti ceti ca' is an abbreviated reference to its content. 'Abhyeti' (comes towards) has no object stated; 'to me' is supplied in the translation as implied by the context, since the Goddess is to come to Śumbha. 'Saṃprītyā' could also be rendered 'with affection' or 'with good will'; I chose 'gladly'. The identity of the speaker, Śumbha, follows the e-text's speaker line; the verse number 693.1 is a misnumbering for the speaker line in the e-text. ↩
  126. The reading: The verse is syntactically loose: 'sa tatra gatvā yatrāste ... tāṃ ca devīṃ tataḥ prāha' has the subject 'sa' (Sugrīva, from 85.57), and 'ca' and 'tataḥ' are somewhat redundant. I take 'śailoddeśe 'tiśobhane' as a locative in apposition to 'tatra yatra', and 'ślakṣṇam' as adverbial, together with 'madhurayā girā'. The subject of 'āste' is the Goddess, understood. I have left 'ca' and 'tataḥ' as a single 'and then'. ↩
  127. lord of the Daityas: Sanskrit daityeśvara, a variant of daityendra: Śumbha as ruler of the Daityas. ↩
  128. classes of gods: devayoni: a class or order of divine beings, such as the gandharvas and yakṣas. The plural with sarvāsu means all such orders. ↩
  129. enemies of the Daityas: daityāri: the foes of the Daityas. These are the devas and any others hostile to the Daityas, not the devas alone. ↩
  130. The reading: The reviewer's findings on sarvāsu ('all', not 'any'), on sadā ('always', now given its own word) and on the dangling construction are accepted. The sentence now opens with the main clause, 'Listen to what he has said', and the two descriptions of Śumbha follow, resumed by sa. The compound nirjitākhiladaityāriḥ is now rendered as a passive bahuvrīhi ('by whom all the enemies ... have been conquered'). Śumbha is the one who has conquered the devas, so the sense is the same as the draft's. I keep 'has said' for āha, which is a perfect, and not the present 'says' that one finding suggests. Devayoni could also be read as 'beings born of gods' or as 'divine wombs/orders'; I take it as 'classes of gods', the sense usual in such lists. ↩
  131. shares of the yajña: yajñabhāga: the portion of the sacrificial offering due to each deva; Śumbha claims to enjoy all of them himself. ↩
  132. The reading: Three of the four findings are accepted. (1) The note on yajñabhāga now says only what the Sanskrit says, that Śumbha enjoys all the shares, without 'in place of the gods'. (2) 'Separately, each one by itself' was redundant, so pṛthak pṛthak is now 'each separately'. (3) The closing quotation mark is removed. Śumbha's speech, opened here after 85.61 ('hear what he says'), runs on into 85.63, so the quotation stays open. The reviewer's point on vaśānugāḥ is a minor wording matter and I keep 'obedient to my will' (vaśa 'will, control' + anuga 'following'), which the reviewer accepts as adequate. In upāśnāmi (upa-√aś, 'partake of, enjoy') the sense of enjoying is kept. In mama, the speaker is Śumbha, the subject of 85.61, so 'mine' is his. ↩
  133. the jewel of an elephant: gajaratna: the elephant Airāvata, Indra's mount, is meant, though the verse does not name it. Ratna, 'jewel', here means the finest of its kind. ↩
  134. the lord of the devas: Devendra, that is Indra. ↩
  135. Uccaiḥśravas: The divine horse that rose from the churning of the Ocean of Milk. The text gives it as its name (saṃjñā). ↩
  136. The reading: I disagree with part of the reviewer's first finding. In praṇipatya the immortals are the agent, as they are of samarpitam (amaraiḥ), so the sense is 'the immortals bowed down and presented the horse to me', not 'the horse was bowed to'. I accept the rest: the horse-jewel is the sole subject of the passive samarpitam, with its name and origin as attributes, and the elephant belongs to a separate clause. I keep the speaker as the agent of hṛtvā because the elephant is said to be taken, whereas the horse is said to be given. The hṛtvā clause has no finite verb, so [I hold it] is supplied in brackets. The ca is rendered 'too', as it goes with the elephant clause after tathaiva. ↩
  137. things that are jewels: ratnabhūtāni bhūtāni: bhūta is taken here as 'things', whatever has become a ratna, the finest of its kind, and not only living creatures. ↩
  138. serpents: uraga, literally 'going on the chest', a common word for serpents. ↩
  139. The reading: bhūtāni: the glossary gives bhūta as 'beings, creatures'. I read ratnabhūtāni bhūtāni as one phrase, 'things that are jewels', following the reviewer, because the sense is every precious possession and not living creatures only. The reviewer's other points are accepted: the serpent note no longer says nāgas, and the locative mayi is rendered 'in my possession' because the context is of what Śumbha holds. śobhane is a vocative addressed to the Goddess. It cannot agree with mayi, since the speaker is male, so it is set off as 'O beautiful lady'. ↩
  140. the jewel of a woman: Sanskrit strīratna: the finest woman, as the elephant and the horse are the finest of their kinds. Here the Goddess is so described by Śumbha's messenger. ↩
  141. The reading: manyāmahe is the first-person plural middle, so 'we regard'; vayam is the subject, Śumbha speaking through the messenger of himself and his circle. Upāgaccha is an imperative, 'come to,' with asmān as object. yataḥ is taken as 'since.' ↩
  142. O you of restless glances: Sanskrit cañcalāpāṅgi, literally 'she whose side-glances (apāṅga, the outer corners of the eyes) are flickering'; a conventional compliment to a woman's beauty. ↩
  143. The reading: bhaja (imperative of √bhaj) can mean 'resort to', 'serve', 'love', 'take as one's own'. I now use 'resort to', which fits the following verse's matparigrahatāṃ vraja (become mine). The reviewer is right that bhaja governs both accusatives, māṃ and niśumbham, so my earlier 'and be his' was wrong; it dropped the 'me' option and added a clause not in the Sanskrit. I take vā ... vāpi as 'either ... or', and uruvikrama as qualifying Niśumbha. The speaker is Śumbha, so mama anuja is 'my younger brother'. I decline two suggestions. First, 'veritable' or 'jewel-like' for ratnabhūtā: bhūta here means 'being', not 'like', and the glossary's ratna already carries the sense 'finest of its kind', so 'a jewel' is the faithful rendering. Second, vai: it is a purely emphatic particle in a causal clause (yataḥ), and I leave it out under the rule on emphatic particles. ↩
  144. becoming my wife: Literally 'from taking by me' (matparigrahāt): parigraha means acceptance, and in marriage the taking of a wife; the same word underlies matparigrahatā, 'the state of being taken by me', rendered 'being my wife'. ↩
  145. intellect: buddhi, the faculty of discernment; the word is also the name of the Goddess in the glossary, but here it is the ordinary instrumental noun. ↩
  146. The reading: matparigrahāt could be 'by accepting me' (the woman taking Śumbha) or 'by my taking you'; I render it as becoming his wife, which covers both. Buddhyā is taken as the common noun 'with your intellect', not as the goddess Buddhi. ↩
  147. smiling deeply within herself: gambhīrāntaḥ smitā: literally 'smiling deep inside'. It means a smile that is inward, profound and unrevealing, not a broad one. ↩
  148. by whom this world is upheld: The relative clause (yayedaṃ dhāryate jagat) identifies Durgā as the Goddess who sustains the universe. It also tells the reader that the demon's offer comes to the one who upholds the world. ↩
  149. The reading: The e-text reads satyamuktatvayā, which I take as a misjoined satyam uktaṃ tvayā, 'the truth has been spoken by you'. The text also has sumbho for Śumbha; I follow the glossary spelling. ↩
  150. the vow that I made long ago: The vow is stated in the next verse (85.72): she will marry only the man who defeats her in battle. ↩
  151. through my small understanding: alpabuddhitvāt, 'from being of small understanding', is ablative. It is taken with pratijñā kṛtā: the vow was made out of the Devī's limited wit, a modest disclaimer. The other possible link, with śrūyatām, is set aside. ↩
  152. The reading: alpabuddhitvāt ('because of small understanding') could be taken with śrūyatām (hear it, given my limited wit) or with pratijñā kṛtā (a vow made through my small understanding). I now take it with kṛtā, as the reviewer advises. A request to be heard is not caused by small understanding, whereas a rash vow made through it makes good sense as the Devī's ironic self-deprecation. The word order does not settle the question, so the sense decides. Kiṃ tu is read as 'but' and katham as 'how'. ↩
  153. take my hand: pāṇigrahaṇa, the taking of the bride's hand, is the central act of the Hindu marriage rite; here it stands for marrying her. ↩
  154. The reading: kiṃ cireṇātra is elliptical: literally 'why with delay here?'. I supply 'wait' in brackets to make the English parse. 'pāṇi' is the neuter accusative form of pāṇi (normally masculine), used here for the hand. ↩
  155. full of pride: avaliptā: literally 'smeared' or 'besmeared' with pride; the sense is arrogant, conceited. ↩
  156. could stand before: tiṣṭhet (optative) with agre: 'stand in front of', that is, hold his ground against them. ↩
  157. The reading: The speaker is not named in the verse. From the context (85.75–76, which go on to praise the power of Śumbha and Niśumbha) it is the Goddess's interlocutor, the messenger of Śumbha (traditionally Sugrīva), who replies to her. I have not added the name. The text of 85.74 has maivam for mā evam, translated 'do not ... like this'. ↩
  158. The reading: The verse has no explicit "against"; anyeṣām api daityānām is taken as the genitive of the opponents with saṃmukhe ("face to face"), which is the natural sense. The reading "even of the other Daityas" as 'in front of' is the same construction. The particle vai is left untranslated as merely emphatic. ↩
  159. The reading: The verse is a relative-correlative construction (yeṣāṃ ... teṣāṃ) with a locative absolute sense of 'against'; I rendered 'yeṣāṃ ... na tasthuḥ' as 'did not stand against those', which carries the genitive of opposition. 'Śumbhādīnām' is in apposition to 'teṣām', so 'Śumbha and the others' is taken to include Niśumbha and their followers. The verb 'prayāsyasi' is a future, 'you will go forth'; it is put as a question of how she can go. ↩
  160. lest you go: mā with the future gamiṣyasi is rare; mā normally takes the injunctive or aorist. It is read here as a warning: go of your own accord, so that you are not taken there dishonoured. ↩
  161. The reading: mā gamiṣyasi: mā with the future is irregular. Readings: (a) a plain prohibition, 'do not go'; (b) a warning, 'lest you go'; (c) a prediction, 'you will not go [that way]'. I now choose (b), as the reviewer asks. A bare 'do not go' straight after 'go' reads as a contradiction and cuts the link between the halves. The warning keeps the contrast between going freely (gaccha) and going dragged by the hair. mayaiva: I now render the eva as 'by me myself', since it marks the speaker's own warning. sā tvam: rendered 'You, then', with sā taken as a resumptive 'that [being so]' and tvam kept as the subject. ↩
  162. The Goddess: Devī, 'the goddess', here the Goddess who has declared the terms of her marriage; she is the speaker of the verses that follow. ↩
  163. The reading: The verse number 703.1 and the speaker line are a misnumbering in the e-text; the line stands between 85.77 and 85.78 and introduces the Goddess's reply. ↩
  164. I made my vow long ago without reflecting: The Goddess's vow, stated elsewhere in the Devīmāhātmya, is that she will take as husband only the man who defeats her in battle. The Sanskrit pratijñā is 'vow' or 'promise'. ↩
  165. The reading: The clause 'yad anālocitā purā' can be read as 'which was made without reflection' (my choice), or as 'which was not considered beforehand'. Both come to the same sense. Elliptical 'evam etat' is rendered as an ironic concession, 'So it is', agreeing with the preceding speech. ↩
  166. lord of the asuras: Asurendra: here Śumbha, the chief of the asuras, to whom the messenger (dūta) is to carry the Goddess's reply. ↩
  167. The reading: ādṛtaḥ (nominative, 'respectful, having regarded with care') is read with the messenger (tvam) and not with the Goddess. Some might read mayā ... ādṛtaḥ differently, but 'you, having taken to heart what I said' fits best. The identification of asurendra with Śumbha is inferred from context; Niśumbha is also possible. ↩
  168. eighty-fifth chapter: The e-text reads pañcāśotitamaḥ, a misprint for pañcāśītitamaḥ, 'eighty-fifth'. This colophon stands at the end of chapter 85 in the usual numbering, though the GRETIL header gives 80. ↩
  169. The reading: The e-text is corrupt: 'purāṇeṭhasāvarṇike' (probably purāṇe 'tha sāvarṇike, with stray ṭh and an avagraha misplaced), 'manvantare'' and 'pañcāśotitamaḥ' (for pañcāśītitamaḥ). I emended to 'in the Purāṇe, in the Sāvarṇika manvantara, in the Devīmāhātmya, the eighty-fifth chapter', and the ending 'iti' is rendered as 'Thus ends'. 'Śrī' is rendered 'revered' as an honorific before the title, not the goddess Śrī. ↩

Adhyāya 86

Sanskrit

षडशीतितमो ऽध्यायः- ८६

ṣaḍaśītitamo 'dhyāyaḥ- 86

English

The eighty-sixth chapter: 86.1

Sanskrit

ऋषिरुवाच

ṛṣiruvāca

English

86.1The ṛṣi said: Having heard these words of the Goddess, that messenger, filled with indignation, went back to the king of the Daityas2 and reported them to him in full detail.3

86.1

Sanskrit

इत्याकर्ण्य वचो देव्याः स दूतो ऽमर्षपूरितः । समाचष्ट समागम्य दैत्यराजाय विस्तरात् ॥

ityākarṇya vaco devyāḥ sa dūto 'marṣapūritaḥ | samācaṣṭa samāgamya daityarājāya vistarāt ||

English

Its English is given with 86.1, above.

86.2

Sanskrit

तस्य दूतस्य तद्वाक्यमाकर्ण्यासुरराट् ततः । सक्रोधः प्राह दैत्यानामधिपं धूम्रलोचनम् ॥

tasya dūtasya tadvākyamākarṇyāsurarāṭ tataḥ | sakrodhaḥ prāha daityānāmadhipaṃ dhūmralocanam ||

English

Then, having heard that speech of the messenger, the king of the asuras4, filled with anger, spoke to Dhūmralocana5, the overlord of the Daityas.6

86.3

Sanskrit

हे धूम्रलोचनाशु त्वं स्वसैन्यपरिवारितः । तामानय बलाद् दुष्टां केशाकर्षणविह्वलाम् ॥

he dhūmralocanāśu tvaṃ svasainyaparivāritaḥ | tāmānaya balād duṣṭāṃ keśākarṣaṇavihvalām ||

English

"O Dhūmralocana, go quickly, surrounded by your own army, and bring that wicked woman here by force, distraught from being dragged by the hair7.8

86.4

Sanskrit

तत्परित्राणदः कश्चिद्यदि वोत्तिष्ठते ऽपरः । स हन्तव्यो ऽमरो वापि यक्षो गन्धर्व एव वा ॥

tatparitrāṇadaḥ kaścidyadi vottiṣṭhate 'paraḥ | sa hantavyo 'maro vāpi yakṣo gandharva eva vā ||

English

If anyone else rises up to protect her, he is to be killed, whether he is an immortal, or a yakṣa9, or a Gandharva.10

Sanskrit

ऋषिरुवाच

ṛṣiruvāca

English

86.5The ṛṣi said: Commanded by him11, the Daitya Dhūmralocana then quickly set out at speed, surrounded by sixty thousand asuras.12

86.5

Sanskrit

तेनाज्ञप्तस्ततः शीघ्रं स दैत्यो धूम्रलोचनः । वृतः पष्ट्या सहस्राणामसुराणां द्रुतं ययो ॥

tenājñaptastataḥ śīghraṃ sa daityo dhūmralocanaḥ | vṛtaḥ paṣṭyā sahasrāṇāmasurāṇāṃ drutaṃ yayo ||

English

Its English is given with 86.5, above.

86

Sanskrit

स दृष्ट्वा तां ततो देवीं तुहिनाचलसंस्थिताम् । जगादोच्चैः प्रयाहीति मूलं शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः ॥ । ६ ॥

sa dṛṣṭvā tāṃ tato devīṃ tuhinācalasaṃsthitām | jagādoccaiḥ prayāhīti mūlaṃ śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ || | 6 ||

English

Then, having seen that Goddess stationed on the snowy mountain13, he called out loudly, "Come away to the presence of14 Śumbha and Niśumbha!"15†

86.7

Sanskrit

न चेत्प्रीत्याद्य भवती मद्भर्तारमुपैष्यति । ततो बलान्नयाम्येष केशाकर्षणविह्वलाम् ॥

na cetprītyādya bhavatī madbhartāramupaiṣyati | tato balānnayāmyeṣa keśākarṣaṇavihvalām ||

English

If you, lady, do not come willingly today to my master16, then I here shall take you by force, distraught from being dragged by the hair."17

Sanskrit

श्रीदेव्युवाच

śrīdevyuvāca

English

86.8The Blessed Goddess18 said: "You have been sent by the lord of the Daityas, you are strong, and you are surrounded by an army. If you take me away by force in this way, what can I do to you then?19"20

86.8

Sanskrit

दैत्येश्वरेण प्रहितो बलवान् बलसंवृतः । बलान्नयसि मामेवं ततः किं ते करोम्यहम् ॥

daityeśvareṇa prahito balavān balasaṃvṛtaḥ | balānnayasi māmevaṃ tataḥ kiṃ te karomyaham ||

English

Its English is given with 86.8, above.

Sanskrit

ऋषिरुवाच

ṛṣiruvāca

English

86.9The ṛṣi said: Addressed in this way, the asura Dhūmralocana rushed at her; then Ambikā21 reduced him to ashes with a mere roar of "hūṃ."

86.9

Sanskrit

इत्युक्तः सो ऽभ्यधावत्तामसुरो धूम्रलोचनः । हुङ्कारेणैव तं भस्म सा चकाराम्बिका ततः ॥

ityuktaḥ so 'bhyadhāvattāmasuro dhūmralocanaḥ | huṅkāreṇaiva taṃ bhasma sā cakārāmbikā tataḥ ||

English

Its English is given with 86.9, above.

86.10

Sanskrit

अथ क्रुद्धं महासैन्यमसुराणां तथाम्बिका । ववर्ष सायकैस्तीक्ष्णैस्तथा शक्तिपरश्वधैः ॥

atha kruddhaṃ mahāsainyamasurāṇāṃ tathāmbikā | vavarṣa sāyakaistīkṣṇaistathā śaktiparaśvadhaiḥ ||

English

Then Ambikā22 rained sharp arrows, and likewise spears23 and axes, upon the enraged great army of the asuras.24

86.11

Sanskrit

ततो धुतसटः कोपात् कृत्वा नादं सुभैरवम् । पपातासुरसेनायां सिंहो देव्याः स्ववाहनः ॥

tato dhutasaṭaḥ kopāt kṛtvā nādaṃ subhairavam | papātāsurasenāyāṃ siṃho devyāḥ svavāhanaḥ ||

English

Then the lion, the Goddess's own mount25, shaking its mane in anger and letting out a most terrifying roar, leapt upon the army of the asuras.

86.12

Sanskrit

कांश्चित् करप्रहारेण दैत्यानास्येन चापरान् । आक्रम्य चाधरेणान्यान् स जघान महासुरान् ॥

kāṃścit karaprahāreṇa daityānāsyena cāparān | ākramya cādhareṇānyān sa jaghāna mahāsurān ||

English

He killed some of those great asuras, the Daityas, with blows of his paw26, and others with his mouth; and, pouncing on others, he killed them with his underpart27.28

86.13

Sanskrit

केषाञ्चित् पाटयामास नखैः कोष्ठानि केसरी । तथा तलप्रिहारेण शिरांसि कृतवान् पृथक् ॥

keṣāñcit pāṭayāmāsa nakhaiḥ koṣṭhāni kesarī | tathā talaprihāreṇa śirāṃsi kṛtavān pṛthak ||

English

With his claws the lion29 tore open the bellies of some of them; and likewise, with blows of his paw30, he severed the heads of others from their bodies.31

86.14

Sanskrit

विच्छिन्नबाहुशिरसः सृतास्तेन तथापरे । पपौ च रुधिरं कोष्ठादन्येषां धुतकेसरः ॥

vicchinnabāhuśirasaḥ sṛtāstena tathāpare | papau ca rudhiraṃ koṣṭhādanyeṣāṃ dhutakesaraḥ ||

English

By him others too were left with their arms and heads severed32; and the lion, shaking his mane, drank blood from the bellies of still others.33

86.15

Sanskrit

क्षणेन तद्बलं सर्वं क्षयं नीतं महात्मना । तेन केसरिणा देव्या वाहनेनातिकोपिना ॥

kṣaṇena tadbalaṃ sarvaṃ kṣayaṃ nītaṃ mahātmanā | tena kesariṇā devyā vāhanenātikopinā ||

English

In an instant all that force was brought to destruction by that great-souled lion, the Goddess's mount, who was exceedingly enraged.34

86.16

Sanskrit

श्रुत्वा तमसुरं देव्या निहतं धूम्रलोचनम् । बलं च क्षयितं कृत्स्त्रं देवीकेसरिणा ततः ॥

śrutvā tamasuraṃ devyā nihataṃ dhūmralocanam | balaṃ ca kṣayitaṃ kṛtstraṃ devīkesariṇā tataḥ ||

English

86.16–17Having heard that the asura Dhūmralocana had been slain by the Goddess, and that the whole force had then been destroyed by the Goddess's lion, Śumbha, lord of the Daityas, grew angry, his lip quivering, and he gave orders to those two great asuras, Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa.35

86.17

Sanskrit

चुकोप दैत्याधिपतिः शुम्भः प्रस्फुरिताधरः । आज्ञापयामास च तौ चण्डमुण्डौ महासुरौ ॥

cukopa daityādhipatiḥ śumbhaḥ prasphuritādharaḥ | ājñāpayāmāsa ca tau caṇḍamuṇḍau mahāsurau ||

English

Its English is given with 86.16–17, above.

86.18

Sanskrit

हे चण्ड हे मुण्ड बलैर्बहुभिः परिवारितौ । तत्र गच्छत गत्वा च सा समानीयतां लघु ॥

he caṇḍa he muṇḍa balairbahubhiḥ parivāritau | tatra gacchata gatvā ca sā samānīyatāṃ laghu ||

English

"O Caṇḍa, O Muṇḍa, surrounded by many forces, go there; and having gone, let her be brought here quickly.36

86.19

Sanskrit

केशेष्वाकृष्य बद्ध्वा वा यदि वः संशयो युधि । तदाशेषायुधैः सर्वैरसुरैर्विनिहन्यताम् ॥

keśeṣvākṛṣya baddhvā vā yadi vaḥ saṃśayo yudhi | tadāśeṣāyudhaiḥ sarvairasurairvinihanyatām ||

English

Drag her by the hair, or bind her37; or, if you have any doubt in the battle38, then let her be struck down with all your weapons, without exception, by all the asuras.39

86.20

Sanskrit

तस्यां हतायां दुष्टायां सिंहे च विनिपातिते । शीघ्रमागम्यतां बद्ध्वा गृहीत्वा तामथाम्बिकाम् ॥

tasyāṃ hatāyāṃ duṣṭāyāṃ siṃhe ca vinipātite | śīghramāgamyatāṃ baddhvā gṛhītvā tāmathāmbikām ||

English

When that wicked woman has been slain and the lion has been struck down, come back quickly40, having bound and seized that Ambikā41.42

Sanskrit

इति श्रीमार्कण्डेयमहापुराणे सावर्णिके मन्वन्तरे देवीमाहात्म्ये धूम्रलोचनवधो नाम षडशीतितमो ऽध्यायः

iti śrīmārkaṇḍeyamahāpurāṇe sāvarṇike manvantare devīmāhātmye dhūmralocanavadho nāma ṣaḍaśītitamo 'dhyāyaḥ

English

Thus, in the revered Mārkaṇḍeya Mahāpurāṇa, in the Sāvarṇika43 manvantara, in the Devīmāhātmya, ends the eighty-sixth chapter, named "The Slaying of Dhūmralocana."44

  1. The reading: Two points. (1) Rendering: the Sanskrit is ṣaḍaśītitamo 'dhyāyaḥ- 86, 'the eighty-sixth chapter', followed by the numeral 86. The draft's 'Chapter 86' dropped the ordinal, so the translation now gives the ordinal and then the numeral. (2) Numbering: the preceding colophon (705.1) calls the previous chapter the eighty-fifth, while the chapter field here reads 81. The heading itself and the verse numbers (86.1) agree on eighty-six, so I follow the Sanskrit and treat '81' as an artefact of the metadata. (3) Textual: the colophon at 705.1 reads pañcāśotitamo, an evident misprint for pañcāśītitamo. Its 'purāṇeṭha' (for purāṇe 'sāvarṇike) is also corrupt. Both lie outside the verse translated here, so I only record them. ↩
  2. king of the Daityas: Daityarāja, here the asura chief to whom the messenger belongs. The verse does not name him; from the context it is Śumbha, though Niśumbha is also possible. ↩
  3. The reading: samāgamya: 'having come together with' or 'having approached'. I chose 'went back to', since the messenger returns from the Goddess to his master. The identity of the Daitya king is not stated in the verse. The following verse calls him asurarāj and suggests Śumbha. ↩
  4. king of the asuras: Asurarāj, here Śumbha, as in the previous verse's 'king of the Daityas'. ↩
  5. Dhūmralocana: 'Smoke-eyed'; a Daitya commander, whom the king now sends against the Goddess. He is not the same as the Goddess's epithet Dhūmrā in the glossary. ↩
  6. The reading: Daityānām adhipam is taken as 'overlord of the Daityas', an epithet of Dhūmralocana (accusative, object of prāha), not as a separate person. Asurarāj is understood to be Śumbha, as the Daityarāja of the preceding verse. ↩
  7. distraught from being dragged by the hair: The compound keśākarṣaṇavihvalām can also be read 'bewildered by the dragging of her hair'; the sense is that she is to be seized and dragged by the hair until she is helpless. ↩
  8. The reading: keśākarṣaṇavihvalām: the compound is taken as 'distraught (vihvalā) through dragging by the hair', with her state as the result of the dragging, which is the natural sense of the instrumental tatpuruṣa. The verse leaves it open whether she is already so or is to be made so; the translation keeps the ambiguity as the Sanskrit does. The vocative 'he' is rendered 'O'. ↩
  9. yakṣa: A class of semi-divine beings, attendants of Kubera and guardians of wealth. ↩
  10. The reading: The compound 'tatparitrāṇadaḥ' is read as tat-paritrāṇa-daḥ, 'giver of protection to her', that is, one who gives her protection; 'tat' is the feminine 'her' (the Goddess), taken as the first member of the compound. 'Amaraḥ' is rendered 'immortal', meaning a god; 'eva' after 'gandharvaḥ' is treated as merely emphatic and left out. The text reads 'vottiṣṭhate' for 'vā uttiṣṭhate'; 'yadi vā' is taken as 'or if', so that 'yadi ... uttiṣṭhate' gives 'if ... rises up', and the 'vā' is absorbed into the sandhi without an additional alternative sense. ↩
  11. Commanded by him: The agent, 'him', is the king of the asuras (Śumbha), who gave the order to Dhūmralocana in the preceding verses. ↩
  12. The reading: The e-text reads yayo, which is metrically and grammatically a lengthened form of yayau ('went'); I have taken it as the third person singular perfect. Verse numbering is irregular in the e-text (708.1 stands for the speaker's line, which is in effect 86.4/5 in the text's own sequence), but the content is clear. ↩
  13. the snowy mountain: tuhinācala, 'snow mountain', here the Himalaya, where the Goddess is staying. ↩
  14. to the presence of: mūlam, literally 'root, base', is used in the sense 'proximity, presence', as the goal of prayāhi. ↩
  15. The reading: mūlam (accusative) could be read as 'root, base, source', i.e. 'the root of Śumbha and Niśumbha', as if describing the Goddess or the speaker's masters. I took it as 'vicinity, presence', the goal of prayāhi ('go forth'), since this suits the speech and the next verse, which speaks of going to 'my husband'. The speaker is Dhūmralocana, subject of sa. ↩
  16. my master: bhartṛ ('master, lord'): Śumbha, whom Dhūmralocana serves. The speaker is Dhūmralocana, so mad- ('my') fixes the sense as 'master'. ↩
  17. The reading: Reviewer's finding on madbhartāram accepted: with mad- the speaker is Dhūmralocana, so bhartṛ can only mean 'master' (Śumbha), and the draft's claim that 'husband' is also present is withdrawn. Finding on prītyā accepted: 'willingly' replaces 'of your own free will', which was stronger than the Sanskrit. This verse continues Dhūmralocana's speech begun in 86.6, so only a closing quotation mark is given. ↩
  18. The Blessed Goddess: śrīdevī, the Goddess with the honorific Śrī. She is the same Devī as in the preceding verses, who is answering Dhūmralocana. ↩
  19. what can I do to you then?: tataḥ kiṃ te karomi means 'what then shall I do to you?'. It is a rhetorical question that implies helplessness, or an ironic challenge. The sense is that, since he comes with force, she cannot resist. ↩
  20. The reading: The verse is hard to read. The sense could be that she is helpless, or that she is mocking him, and the Sanskrit does not settle which. I kept the literal question 'what can I do to you then?', as the Sanskrit has it. In the next verse (86.9) Ambikā reduces Dhūmralocana to ash, so the irony is probably meant. The word balavān agrees with the subject, 'you', so it does not apply to the Daityeśvara. The compound balasaṃvṛtaḥ agrees with it also. ↩
  21. Ambikā: 'Little mother'; a name of the Goddess (Devī), here used of her as she confronts Dhūmralocana. ↩
  22. Ambikā: 'Little mother', a name of the Goddess (Devī). ↩
  23. spears: Śakti here is the missile weapon, a spear or lance, not the divine power. ↩
  24. The reading: The compound śaktiparaśvadhaiḥ could be 'spears and axes' (dvandva) or, less likely, 'axes with spears'. I take it as a dvandva. The glossary renders śakti as 'power' for the Goddess's energy, but here it is clearly a weapon, so I render 'spears'. In the glossary 'paraśu' is rendered 'axe', which I keep for paraśvadha. Also, 'tathā ... tathā' is rendered 'and likewise' once, since the first tathā links Ambikā's action to the preceding verse ('in the same way' as with Dhūmralocana). ↩
  25. the Goddess's own mount: devyāḥ svavāhanaḥ: the lion is the Goddess's own vehicle (vāhana), here acting as her ally in the battle. ↩
  26. paw: Sanskrit kara, 'hand', here the lion's forepaw; the glossary's 'trunk' belongs to elephants and does not apply here. ↩
  27. underpart: adhareṇa is obscure; it may be read as 'with the lower part (of the body)', or as 'with the lower lip'. The translation keeps the general sense. ↩
  28. The reading: adhareṇa: 'with the lower lip' (adhara), 'with the lower part of the body', or a corrupt reading. After ākramya ('pouncing upon') the sense 'with the underpart' seems more natural, so I rendered it literally and flagged it in a note. The subject sa is the lion of 86.11. ↩
  29. the lion: Kesarī, 'the maned one', a common word for the lion; here it is the Goddess's mount, as in the preceding verses. ↩
  30. blows of his paw: The text reads talaprihāreṇa, evidently a corruption or irregular form of talapraharaṇa/talaprahāreṇa, 'with a blow of the flat of the hand (paw)'. ↩
