  129. The reading: The text has 'evameṣa kṣayaṃ daityaḥ kṣīṇarakto gamiṣyati' as a separate pāda-group; I took 'ugrāḥ' (fierce ones) as referring to the asuras being devoured, and 'apare' as other asuras born from Raktabīja's blood. 'Na ca utpatsyanti' is read with 'apare' as subject. ↩
  130. spear: Sanskrit śūla, a spear or pike, often a trident; the same weapon is called śakti and śūla in 88.50. ↩
  131. The reading: śūlena may mean 'spear' or 'trident'; I chose 'spear' as the general sense. soṇitam is the printed form for śoṇitam (blood); the sense is unaffected. ↩
  132. that one: asau points back to Raktabīja, whose blood Kālī has just taken in the previous verse. ↩
  133. The reading: Findings 1 and 2 are accepted: both 'tataḥ' and 'atha' are now rendered ('Then ... next'), and the two clauses are joined with 'and', as 'na ca' requires. On finding 3, 'asau' is kept as 'that one' rather than replaced by the name or by a bracketed gloss. The Sanskrit has only a pronoun, and an identification in brackets would be an explanation, which the rules put in the notes. The note says the referent is Raktabīja. 'Asyāḥ' (her) is read with 'vedanām' (pain), and 'alpikām api' (even the slightest) as qualifying 'vedanām'. ↩
  134. Cāmuṇḍā: The text reads vāmuṇḍā, evidently a misprint for cāmuṇḍā; the verses that follow (88.58–59) read cāmuṇḍā. ↩
  135. The reading: The printed vāmuṇḍā is not a word; I read cāmuṇḍā (Kālī), as in 88.58–59 and the context. In sampratīcchati the prefixes sam-prati with the root iṣ/ṛcch is taken as 'receives, catches', in the sense of 'takes in', following the earlier pratīccha in 88.53; the present tense is kept, as in the Sanskrit. 'Tasya' refers to Raktabīja, who struck Caṇḍikā with the mace in the verse before and is now struck. ↩
  136. in her mouth: The locative mukhe is taken with samudgatāḥ, 'sprang up in her mouth'. It may also be read with the preceding verse's mouth, which received the blood. The sense is that the asuras born of the blood-drops arose in Cāmuṇḍā's mouth. ↩
  137. The reading: mukhe samudgatā ye 'syā: asyāḥ ('her') is taken as the genitive of Cāmuṇḍā, with mukhe, 'in her mouth'. Another reading would take mukhe alone, 'in the mouth', but the genitive points to her. The form samudgatā is a metrically shortened plural, equal to samudgatāḥ, agreeing with mahāsurāḥ. ↩
  138. whose blood had been drunk by Cāmuṇḍā: cāmuṇḍāpītaśoṇitam is a compound qualifying Raktabīja: 'the one whose blood was drunk by Cāmuṇḍā', as told in 88.58. ↩
  139. O protector of the earth: mahīpāla, a vocative for a king; here the ṛṣi addresses the king to whom he tells the story (Suratha). ↩
  140. the host of the Mothers: The Mother-Śaktis who came forth earlier in the battle to assist the Goddess. 'Teṣām ... jāto' is ambiguous: see doubts. ↩
  141. The reading: The phrase 'teṣāṃ mātṛgaṇo jāto' is difficult. Possible readings: (a) 'the host of the Mothers, born from them (the devas)' or 'the host of the Mothers that had arisen among them'; (b) with jāta as 'arisen/risen up, become [exultant]'. The Mothers were born from the energies (śaktis) of the gods, so 'teṣām' may be genitive of source. I rendered 'from among them ... which had come into being', keeping the ambiguity close to the Sanskrit. Also 'asṛṅmadoddhataḥ' is taken as 'asṛj-mada-uddhata', wild with the intoxication of blood; the nasal 'ṅ' in 'asṛṅ' is sandhi for asṛk before m. ↩
  142. eighty-eighth chapter: The colophon reads aṣṭāśītitamo 'dhyāyaḥ, 'the eighty-eighth chapter', counting through the whole Purāṇa. The Devīmāhātmya occupies chapters 81–93, so this is its eighth chapter. The chapter label 83 in this e-text's verse references does not agree with the colophon. ↩
  143. The reading: (1) raktabījavadhonāma is taken as raktabījavadho nāma, 'named (The) Slaying of Raktabīja', by sandhi of -aḥ + n- to -o n-. (2) The e-text's chapter label (83) disagrees with the colophon's aṣṭāśītitama (88), and with the verse numbers 88.56–61 just before. I follow the Sanskrit of the colophon, and the label is probably a mistake in the e-text. (3) I accept the reviewer's correction that chapter 88 is the eighth chapter of the Devīmāhātmya, not the eleventh as my earlier note said. (4) I also accept the point about the supplied verb: 'ends' now stands in square brackets, since the Sanskrit has no verb. (5) The adjective śrī- in śrīmārkaṇḍeyapurāṇe is an honorific ('revered'). It is not the goddess Śrī of the glossary. ↩

Adhyāya 89

Sanskrit

एकोननवतितमो ऽध्यायः- ८९

ekonanavatitamo 'dhyāyaḥ- 89

English

The eighty-ninth chapter.1

Sanskrit

राजोवाच

rājovāca

89.1

Sanskrit

विचित्रमिदमाख्यातं भगवन् भवता मम । देव्याश्चरितमाहात्म्यं रक्तबीजवधाश्रितम् ॥

vicitramidamākhyātaṃ bhagavan bhavatā mama | devyāścaritamāhātmyaṃ raktabījavadhāśritam ||

English

This wondrous account has been told to me by you, venerable sir2: the greatness of the deeds of the Devī3, centred on the slaying of Raktabīja.4

89.2

Sanskrit

भूयश्चेच्छाम्यहं श्रोतुं रक्तबीजे निपातिते । चका शुम्भो यत्कर्म निशुम्भश्चातिकोपनः ॥

bhūyaścecchāmyahaṃ śrotuṃ raktabīje nipātite | cakā śumbho yatkarma niśumbhaścātikopanaḥ ||

English

And I wish to hear further what deed Śumbha performed, and Niśumbha too, who was exceedingly wrathful, when Raktabīja had been struck down.5

Sanskrit

ऋषिरुवाच

ṛṣiruvāca

English

The ṛṣi said:

89.3

Sanskrit

चकार कोपमतुलं रक्तबीजे निपातिते । शुम्भासुरो निशुम्भश्च हतेष्वन्येषु चाहवे ॥

cakāra kopamatulaṃ raktabīje nipātite | śumbhāsuro niśumbhaśca hateṣvanyeṣu cāhave ||

English

The asura Śumbha, and Niśumbha too, felt incomparable wrath when Raktabīja had been struck down and the others had been slain in the battle.6

89.4

Sanskrit

हन्यमानं महासैन्यं विलोक्यामर्षमुद्वहन् । अभ्यधावन्निशुम्भो ऽथ मुख्ययासुरसेनया ॥

hanyamānaṃ mahāsainyaṃ vilokyāmarṣamudvahan | abhyadhāvanniśumbho 'tha mukhyayāsurasenayā ||

English

Seeing [his] great army being slaughtered, and bearing indignation, Niśumbha then rushed forward with the foremost force of the asuras7.8

89.5

Sanskrit

तस्याग्रतस्तथा पृष्ठे पार्श्वयोश्च महासुराः । संदष्टौष्ठपुटाः क्रुद्धा हन्तुं देवीमुपाययुः ॥

tasyāgratastathā pṛṣṭhe pārśvayośca mahāsurāḥ | saṃdaṣṭauṣṭhapuṭāḥ kruddhā hantuṃ devīmupāyayuḥ ||

English

In front of him, likewise behind him, and on both sides, the great asuras, their lips clenched between their teeth9 and enraged, advanced to kill the Devī.10†

89.6

Sanskrit

आजगाम महावीर्यः शुम्भो ऽपि स्वबलैर्वृतः । निहन्तुं चण्डिकां कोपात्कृताव युद्धं तु मातृभिः ॥

ājagāma mahāvīryaḥ śumbho 'pi svabalairvṛtaḥ | nihantuṃ caṇḍikāṃ kopātkṛtāva yuddhaṃ tu mātṛbhiḥ ||

English

Śumbha too, of great vigor, surrounded by his own forces11, came in anger to kill Caṇḍikā, having fought the battle with the Mothers12.13

89.7

Sanskrit

ततो युद्धमतीवासीद्देव्या शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः । शरवर्षमतीवोग्रं मेघयोरिव वर्षतोः ॥

tato yuddhamatīvāsīddevyā śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ | śaravarṣamatīvograṃ meghayoriva varṣatoḥ ||

English

Then there was an exceedingly fierce battle between the Goddess14 and Śumbha and Niśumbha, with a shower of arrows terrible in the extreme, as of two clouds pouring down rain.15

89.8

Sanskrit

चिच्छेदास्ताञ्छरांस्ताभ्यां चण्डिका स्वशरोत्करैः । ताडयामास चाङ्गेषु शस्त्रौघैरसुरेश्वरौ ॥

cicchedāstāñcharāṃstābhyāṃ caṇḍikā svaśarotkaraiḥ | tāḍayāmāsa cāṅgeṣu śastraughairasureśvarau ||

English

Caṇḍikā cut down the arrows hurled by the two16 with her own volleys of arrows, and she struck the two lords of the asuras17 on their limbs with floods of weapons.18

89.9

Sanskrit

निशुम्भो निशितं खड्गं चर्म चादाय सुप्रभम् । अताडयन्मूर्ध्नि सिंहं देव्या वाहनमुत्तमम् ॥

niśumbho niśitaṃ khaḍgaṃ carma cādāya suprabham | atāḍayanmūrdhni siṃhaṃ devyā vāhanamuttamam ||

English

Niśumbha took up a sharp sword and a bright shield, and struck the lion, the Goddess's excellent mount, on the head.19

89.10

Sanskrit

निशुम्भो निशितं खड्गं चर्म चादाय सुप्रभम् । अताडयन्मूर्ध्नि सिंहं देव्या वाहनमुत्तमम् ॥

niśumbho niśitaṃ khaḍgaṃ carma cādāya suprabham | atāḍayanmūrdhni siṃhaṃ devyā vāhanamuttamam ||

English

Niśumbha took up a sharp sword and a bright shield, and struck the lion, the Goddess's excellent mount, on the head.20

89.11

Sanskrit

छिन्ने चर्मणि खड्गे च शक्तिं चिक्षेप सो ऽसुरः । तामप्यस्य द्विधा चक्रे चक्रेणाभिमुखागताम् ॥

chinne carmaṇi khaḍge ca śaktiṃ cikṣepa so 'suraḥ | tāmapyasya dvidhā cakre cakreṇābhimukhāgatām ||

English

When the shield and the sword had been cut to pieces, that asura21 hurled a spear22. She made that too23, as it came straight at her, into two parts with her disc.24

89.12

Sanskrit

कोपाध्मातो निशुम्भो ऽथ शूलं जग्राह दानवः । आयातं मुष्टिपातेन देवी तच्चाप्यचूर्णयत् ॥

kopādhmāto niśumbho 'tha śūlaṃ jagrāha dānavaḥ | āyātaṃ muṣṭipātena devī taccāpyacūrṇayat ||

English

Then Niśumbha, the dānava, swollen with rage, seized a spear. As it came at her, the Goddess shattered that too to powder with a blow of her fist25.26

89.13

Sanskrit

अथादाय गदां सो ऽपि चिक्षेप चण्डिकां प्रति । सापि देव्या त्रिशूलेन भिन्ना भस्मत्वमागता ॥

athādāya gadāṃ so 'pi cikṣepa caṇḍikāṃ prati | sāpi devyā triśūlena bhinnā bhasmatvamāgatā ||

English

Then he too27 took up a mace and hurled it at Caṇḍikā; and that also, split by the Goddess with her trident, was reduced to ashes.

89.14

Sanskrit

ततः परशुहस्तं तमायान्त दैत्यपुङ्गवम् । आहत्य देवी बाणौघैरपातयत भूतले ॥

tataḥ paraśuhastaṃ tamāyānta daityapuṅgavam | āhatya devī bāṇaughairapātayata bhūtale ||

English

Then, as that bull among the Daityas28 came on with an axe in his hand, the Goddess struck him with floods of arrows and brought him down on the surface of the earth.29

89.15

Sanskrit

तस्मिन्निपतिते भूमौ निशुम्भे भीमविक्रमे । भ्रातर्यतीव संक्रुद्धः प्रययौ हन्तुमम्बिकाम् ॥

tasminnipatite bhūmau niśumbhe bhīmavikrame | bhrātaryatīva saṃkruddhaḥ prayayau hantumambikām ||

English

When his brother Niśumbha, of terrible prowess, had fallen to the ground, [Śumbha]30, exceedingly enraged, set out to kill Ambikā.31

89.16

Sanskrit

स रथस्थस्तथात्युच्चैर्गृहीतपरमायुधैः । भुजैरष्टाभिरतुलैर्व्याप्याशेषं बभौ नभः ॥

sa rathasthastathātyuccairgṛhītaparamāyudhaiḥ | bhujairaṣṭābhiratulairvyāpyāśeṣaṃ babhau nabhaḥ ||

English

Standing in his chariot, with his eight matchless arms raised high and holding the finest weapons, he filled the whole sky32 and shone.33

89.17

Sanskrit

तमायान्तं समालोक्य देवी शङ्घमवादयत् । ज्याशब्दं चापि धनुषश्चकारातीव दुः सहम् ॥

tamāyāntaṃ samālokya devī śaṅghamavādayat | jyāśabdaṃ cāpi dhanuṣaścakārātīva duḥ saham ||

English

Seeing him come on, the Goddess blew her conch34, and she also made the twang of her bow-string35, which was exceedingly hard to bear.36

89.18

Sanskrit

पूरयामास ककुभो निजघण्टास्वनेन च । समस्तदैत्यसैन्यानां तेजोवधविधायिना ॥

pūrayāmāsa kakubho nijaghaṇṭāsvanena ca | samastadaityasainyānāṃ tejovadhavidhāyinā ||

English

And she filled the quarters of the sky37 with the sound of her own bell, which brought about the loss of strength38 of all the Daitya armies.39

89.19

Sanskrit

ततः सिंहो महानादैस्त्याजितेभमहामदैः । पूरयामास गगनं गां तथैव दिशो दश ॥

tataḥ siṃho mahānādaistyājitebhamahāmadaiḥ | pūrayāmāsa gaganaṃ gāṃ tathaiva diśo daśa ||

English

Then the lion, with great roars that made the elephants abandon their mighty rut40, filled the sky, the earth, and likewise the ten directions41.42

89.20

Sanskrit

ततः काली समुत्पत्य गगनं क्ष्मामताडयत् । कराभ्यां तन्निनादेन प्राक्स्वनास्ते तिरोहिताः ॥

tataḥ kālī samutpatya gaganaṃ kṣmāmatāḍayat | karābhyāṃ tanninādena prāksvanāste tirohitāḥ ||

English

Then Kālī leapt up into the sky and struck the earth with both her hands. By the din of that, the earlier sounds were drowned out43.44

89.21

Sanskrit

अट्टाट्टहासमशिवं शिवदूती चकार ह । तैः शब्दैरसुरास्त्रेसुः शुम्भः कोपं परं ययौ ॥

aṭṭāṭṭahāsamaśivaṃ śivadūtī cakāra ha | taiḥ śabdairasurāstresuḥ śumbhaḥ kopaṃ paraṃ yayau ||

English

Śivadūtī45 let out an ill-omened, thunderous peal of laughter46. At those sounds the asuras trembled, and Śumbha was seized by the utmost rage.

89.22

Sanskrit

दुरात्मंस्तिष्ठ तिष्ठेति व्याजहाराम्बिका यदा । तदा जयेत्यभिहितं देवैराकाशसंस्थितैः ॥

durātmaṃstiṣṭha tiṣṭheti vyājahārāmbikā yadā | tadā jayetyabhihitaṃ devairākāśasaṃsthitaiḥ ||

English

When Ambikā47 called out, "Stand, stand, you evil-minded one!", then the devas stationed in the sky cried out, "Victory!"

89.23

Sanskrit

शुम्भेनागत्य या शक्तिर्मुक्ता ज्वालातिभीषणा । आयान्ती वह्निकूटाभा सा निरस्ता महोल्कया ॥

śumbhenāgatya yā śaktirmuktā jvālātibhīṣaṇā | āyāntī vahnikūṭābhā sā nirastā maholkayā ||

English

The śakti48 that Śumbha hurled as he came up, terrible with its flames and gleaming like a heap of fire as it approached, was beaten back by [the Goddess]49 with a great meteor.50

89.24

Sanskrit

सिंहनादेन शुम्भस्य व्याप्तं लोकत्रयान्तरम् । निर्घातनिः स्वनो घोरो जितवानवनीपते ॥

siṃhanādena śumbhasya vyāptaṃ lokatrayāntaram | nirghātaniḥ svano ghoro jitavānavanīpate ||

English

The space between the three worlds was filled by Śumbha's lion-roar51, [but] a terrible sound like a thunderclap52 overcame [it], O king.53

89.25

Sanskrit

शुम्भमुक्ताञ्छरान्देवी शुम्भस्तत्प्रहिताञ्छरान् । चिच्छेद स्वशरैरुग्रैः शतशो ऽथ सहस्रशः ॥

śumbhamuktāñcharāndevī śumbhastatprahitāñcharān | ciccheda svaśarairugraiḥ śataśo 'tha sahasraśaḥ ||

English

The Goddess54 cut down the arrows loosed by Śumbha, and Śumbha cut down the arrows she had sent at him, with their own fierce arrows, by the hundred and then by the thousand.55

89.26

Sanskrit

ततः सा चण्डिका क्रुद्धा शूलेनाभिजघान तम् । स तदाभिहतो भूमौ मूर्च्छितो निपपात ह ॥

tataḥ sā caṇḍikā kruddhā śūlenābhijaghāna tam | sa tadābhihato bhūmau mūrcchito nipapāta ha ||

English

Then Caṇḍikā, enraged, struck him with her spear56. Struck by it then, he fell to the ground, unconscious.57

89.27

Sanskrit

ततो निशुम्भः सम्प्राप्य चेतनामात्तकार्मुकः । आजघान शरैर्देवीं कालीं केसरिणं तथा ॥

tato niśumbhaḥ samprāpya cetanāmāttakārmukaḥ | ājaghāna śarairdevīṃ kālīṃ kesariṇaṃ tathā ||

English

Then Niśumbha, having regained consciousness and taken up his bow, struck the Goddess, Kālī, and likewise the lion58 with his arrows.

89.28

Sanskrit

पुनश्च कृत्वा बाहूनामयुतं दनुजेश्वरः । चक्रायुधेन दितिजश्छादयामास चण्डिकाम् ॥

punaśca kṛtvā bāhūnāmayutaṃ danujeśvaraḥ | cakrāyudhena ditijaśchādayāmāsa caṇḍikām ||

English

And again, having made ten thousand arms, the lord of the Danu's sons59, the son of Diti60, covered Caṇḍikā with his disc-weapons.61

89.29

Sanskrit

ततो भगवती क्रुद्धा दुर्गा दुर्गार्तिनाशिनी । चिच्छेद तानि चक्राणि स्वशरैः सायकांश्च तान् ॥

tato bhagavatī kruddhā durgā durgārtināśinī | ciccheda tāni cakrāṇi svaśaraiḥ sāyakāṃśca tān ||

English

Then the Blessed One, enraged, Durgā, the destroyer of the distress of those in difficulty62, cut down those discs and those arrows with her own arrows.63

89.30

Sanskrit

ततो निशुम्भो वेगेन गदामादाय चण्डिकाम् । अभ्यधावत वै हन्तुं दैत्यसेनासमावृतः ॥

tato niśumbho vegena gadāmādāya caṇḍikām | abhyadhāvata vai hantuṃ daityasenāsamāvṛtaḥ ||

English

Then Niśumbha, taking up a mace, rushed swiftly at Caṇḍikā to kill her, surrounded by an army of Daityas.

89.31

Sanskrit

तस्यापतत एवाशु गदां चिच्छेद चण्डिका । खड्गेन शितधारेण स च शूलं समाददे ॥

tasyāpatata evāśu gadāṃ ciccheda caṇḍikā | khaḍgena śitadhāreṇa sa ca śūlaṃ samādade ||

English

As he was rushing at her, Caṇḍikā at once cut down his mace with a sword of keen edge, and he took up a spear.64

89.32

Sanskrit

शूलहस्तं समायान्तं निशुम्भममरार्दनम् । हृदि विव्याध शूलेन वेगाविद्धेन चण्डिका ॥

śūlahastaṃ samāyāntaṃ niśumbhamamarārdanam | hṛdi vivyādha śūlena vegāviddhena caṇḍikā ||

English

As Niśumbha, the tormentor of the immortals65, came on with a spear in his hand, Caṇḍikā pierced him in the heart with her spear, hurled with force.

89.33

Sanskrit

भिन्नस्य तस्य शूलेन हृदयान्निः सृतो ऽपरः । महाबलो महावीर्यस्तिष्ठेति पुरुषो वदन् ॥

bhinnasya tasya śūlena hṛdayānniḥ sṛto 'paraḥ | mahābalo mahāvīryastiṣṭheti puruṣo vadan ||

English

From him, pierced by the spear, another man66 came forth out of his heart, of great strength and great vigor, saying, “Stand!”67

89.34

Sanskrit

तस्य निष्क्रामतो देवी प्रहस्य स्वनवत्ततः । शिरश्चिच्छेद खड्गेन ततो ऽसावपतद् भुवि ॥

tasya niṣkrāmato devī prahasya svanavattataḥ | śiraściccheda khaḍgena tato 'sāvapatad bhuvi ||

English

As he was coming forth, the Goddess burst into loud laughter and then cut off his head with her sword; thereupon he fell to the ground.68

89.35

Sanskrit

ततः सिंहश्चखादोग्रं दंष्ट्राक्षुण्णशिरोधरान् । असुरांस्तांस्तथा काली शिवदूती तथापरान् ॥

tataḥ siṃhaścakhādograṃ daṃṣṭrākṣuṇṇaśirodharān | asurāṃstāṃstathā kālī śivadūtī tathāparān ||

English

Then the lion fiercely devoured those asuras whose necks had been crushed by its fangs, and Kālī and Śivadūtī69 likewise devoured others.70

89.36

Sanskrit

कौमारीशक्तिनिर्भिन्नाः केचिन्नेशुर्महासुराः । ब्रह्माणीमन्त्रपूतेन तोयेनान्ये निराकृताः ॥

kaumārīśaktinirbhinnāḥ kecinneśurmahāsurāḥ | brahmāṇīmantrapūtena toyenānye nirākṛtāḥ ||

English

Some great asuras, pierced by the śakti of Kaumārī71, perished; others were driven off72 by water purified with the mantra of Brahmāṇī.73

89.37

Sanskrit

माहेश्वरीत्रिशूलेन भिन्नाः पेतुस्तथापरे । वाराहीतुण्डघातेन केचिच्चूर्णोकृता भुवि ॥

māheśvarītriśūlena bhinnāḥ petustathāpare | vārāhītuṇḍaghātena keciccūrṇokṛtā bhuvi ||

English

Others likewise fell, pierced by the trident of Māheśvarī, and some were ground to powder on the earth by the blows of Vārāhī's snout74.75

89.38

Sanskrit

खण्डं खण्डं च चक्रेण वैष्णव्या दानवाः कृताः । वज्रेण चैन्द्रीहस्ताग्रविमुक्तेन तथापरे ॥

khaṇḍaṃ khaṇḍaṃ ca cakreṇa vaiṣṇavyā dānavāḥ kṛtāḥ | vajreṇa caindrīhastāgravimuktena tathāpare ||

English

Some dānavas were cut into pieces, bit by bit, by the disc of Vaiṣṇavī; others likewise [were slain] by the thunderbolt loosed from the tip of Aindrī's hand76.77

89.39

Sanskrit

केचिद्विनेशुरसुराः केचिन्नष्टा महाहवात् । भक्षिताश्चापरे कालीशिवदूतीमृगाधिपैः ॥

kecidvineśurasurāḥ kecinnaṣṭā mahāhavāt | bhakṣitāścāpare kālīśivadūtīmṛgādhipaiḥ ||

English

Some asuras perished; some fled from the great battle; and others were devoured by Kālī, by Śivadūtī and by the lord of beasts78.79

Sanskrit

इति श्रीमार्कण्डेयमहापुराणे सावर्णिके मन्वन्तरे देवीमाहत्म्ये निशुम्भवधोनाम एकोननवतितमो ऽध्यायः

iti śrīmārkaṇḍeyamahāpurāṇe sāvarṇike manvantare devīmāhatmye niśumbhavadhonāma ekonanavatitamo 'dhyāyaḥ

English

Thus ends the eighty-ninth chapter80, named "The Slaying of Niśumbha," in the Devīmāhātmya81 in the Sāvarṇika manvantara of the revered Mārkaṇḍeya Mahāpurāṇa.82

  1. The reading: This line is only the chapter heading (ekonanavatitamo 'dhyāyaḥ, 'the eighty-ninth chapter'), with the numeral '89' repeated in the text. The reading 'ekonanavati' is the standard form of 'ekonanavati' ('ninety less one'); the heading is rendered as a bare title with no verb, as in the Sanskrit. ↩
  2. venerable sir: Bhagavat, here addressing the sage Medhas, who narrates the Devīmāhātmya to the speaker, King Suratha. ↩
  3. the greatness of the deeds of the Devī: The compound caritamāhātmya may be read as 'the greatness of the deeds' (as here) or as 'the deeds and the greatness'. ↩
  4. The reading: The compound devyāś caritamāhātmyam can be read as a tatpuruṣa ('the greatness of the deeds of the Devī') or as a dvandva ('the deeds and the greatness of the Devī'). I chose the tatpuruṣa because the sense of the Devīmāhātmya as a whole supports it. The e-text gives no speaker's line here, though the usual 'rājovāca' would be expected: the speaker is King Suratha, addressing the sage Medhas, and 89.2 continues his speech. The 'ṛṣir uvāca' at [734.1] opens Medhas's reply. Since the Sanskrit given has no speaker's line, I have added none. ↩
  5. The reading: The printed 'cakā' is incomplete. I read it as cakāra, 'performed', with the final syllable lost in the e-text. This fits the sense and the metre, and the verse that follows has cakāra. The singular verb agrees with the nearer subject Śumbha, and Niśumbha is joined to it by ca, so I rendered the verb as governing both. ↩
  6. The reading: The verse has no subject marked for 'cakāra' beyond the singular 'śumbhāsuraḥ'; 'niśumbhaśca' is joined to it, so the singular verb is read as agreeing with the nearer subject, with Niśumbha added by 'ca'. In the preceding verse 89.2 the verb 'cakāra' is likewise singular with both brothers. Also, 'hateṣvanyeṣu' is a locative absolute ('the others having been slain'), and 'āhave' is taken with it, 'in the battle'. ↩
  7. the foremost force of the asuras: mukhyayā āsurasenayā: the instrumental feminine mukhyayā qualifies āsurasenayā, 'with the chief (or principal) army of the asuras'. It may mean the main body or the picked troops. ↩
  8. The reading: mukhyayā ("chief, foremost") may mean the principal body of the army or its best troops; I chose "foremost force" to keep both. Also, the text reads abhyadhāvan (plural) with the singular subject Niśumbha; this is probably a scribal or metrical irregularity, and I translate it as a singular, "rushed". ↩
  9. their lips clenched between their teeth: Sanskrit saṃdaṣṭauṣṭhapuṭāḥ: a compound of saṃdaṣṭa (bitten, pressed) and oṣṭhapuṭa (the cup or fold of the lip), a sign of fury. Here it qualifies the asuras. ↩
  10. The reading: tasya ("of him") is taken as referring to Niśumbha, the subject of the preceding verse. The compound saṃdaṣṭauṣṭhapuṭāḥ is rendered literally as "with lips clenched between the teeth". The verb upāyayuḥ (from upa-√yā) is rendered "advanced", with hantum ("to kill") giving the purpose. ↩
  11. his own forces: svabalaiḥ is the plural of bala. Here it means troops, not abstract strength. ↩
  12. the Mothers: The divine Mothers (Mātṛkās) are the powers of the gods, who came forth to fight beside the Goddess against the asura host. ↩
  13. The reading: kṛtāva is not a Sanskrit form and looks like a misprint. I read it as kṛtvā, the absolutive of kṛ, which agrees with Śumbha as the subject: 'having fought the battle'. The particle tu is left untranslated as merely connective. The verse is also awkward in sense: Śumbha comes to kill Caṇḍikā only after fighting the Mothers. ↩
  14. the Goddess: Devī, here Caṇḍikā, who has been fighting the asura hosts. ↩
  15. The reading: atīva is repeated in the verse (yuddham atīva, śaravarṣam atīva ugram); both are rendered, as 'exceedingly' and 'in the extreme'. The verse is compressed: 'Goddess' is in the instrumental (devyā) and Śumbha and Niśumbha in the genitive dual, so 'between the Goddess and Śumbha and Niśumbha' is the sense. The dual genitive meghayor varṣatoḥ ('of two clouds raining') is a simile for the two brothers' arrow-showers, or possibly for both sides; I take it as the arrow-shower of both sides, like two clouds raining. ↩
  16. the arrows hurled by the two: astān is the past participle of as, 'to hurl'. tābhyām, 'by the two', is the agent of the hurling and refers to Śumbha and Niśumbha. It does not go with ciccheda. ↩
  17. the two lords of the asuras: asureśvarau is dual, so it names Śumbha and Niśumbha together. The glossary's Asureśvara is the singular address to Śumbha. ↩
  18. The reading: astān could in principle be read as a form of a noun meaning 'home' (asta), but the sense 'hurled', agreeing with śarān, is the only one that fits. tābhyām is taken with astān, as the agent of the hurling, and not with ciccheda. ↩
  19. The reading: The verse is repeated word for word as 89.10 in this e-text. I translate only 89.9. Both suprabham (bright, shining) and niśitam (sharp) are accusatives; I take suprabham as qualifying carma alone, with khaḍgaṃ niśitam and carma suprabham, though it could also apply to both objects. ↩
  20. The reading: The verse is identical to 89.9, which is given above in the preceding verses. It is translated again here as the verse requested, with the same sense. The words devyā vāhanam are taken together as 'the Goddess's mount', with siṃham as the object of atāḍayat and vāhanam in apposition. The imperfect atāḍayat, written in the text as atāḍayan before mūrdhni by sandhi, is the third person singular. ↩
  21. that asura: The subject, so asuraḥ, is Niśumbha, the asura of the preceding verses. The glossary's gloss of asura as Pātālaketu does not apply here. ↩
  22. spear: śakti: a spear or javelin hurled by hand. It is a different word from Śakti, the divine power, and the glossary's 'power' does not apply here. ↩
  23. She made that too: The subject of cakre is not stated. It is the Goddess (Caṇḍikā), the agent in the surrounding verses. ↩
  24. The reading: The word 'asya' (of him) in 'tāmapyasya' can be taken with 'śaktim' as 'his spear', and I have done so, since the weapon belongs to the asura. 'Śakti' is rendered 'spear' (a weapon), not the divine 'Power' of the glossary. The text 'chinne carmaṇi khaḍge ca' is a locative absolute and its agent is the Goddess, implied from the preceding verses; 89.10 is a repeat of 89.9 and does not affect this verse. I rendered 'ābhimukhāgatām' as 'as it came straight at her'; it literally means 'come face to face', that is, approaching directly. ↩
  25. shattered that too to powder with a blow of her fist: The text says the spear was struck as it came (āyātam), the participle agreeing with śūlam. Elsewhere in this chapter the Goddess uses weapons; here she uses only her fist. ↩
  26. The reading: The verse has śūlam as the object of jagrāha and āyātam, 'come', which agrees with it, so the 'tat' that she shattered is the spear. The verb acūrṇayat means 'ground to powder'. Niśumbha is the subject of jagrāha, and the Goddess is the subject of acūrṇayat. The word kopādhmāta literally means 'puffed up with anger' and is rendered 'swollen with rage'. ↩
  27. he too: The pronoun so 'pi refers to Niśumbha, the subject of the preceding verses. ↩
  28. bull among the Daityas: Daityapuṅgava: puṅgava, 'bull', marks the chief or foremost of a class. Here it refers to Niśumbha, the same asura who has been attacking in the preceding verses. ↩
  29. The reading: The text reads āyānta, which is metrically and grammatically irregular; the expected form is the accusative āyāntam (agreeing with tam ... daityapuṅgavam), or a participle in some other case. I take it as āyāntam, 'coming on'. Likewise apātayata is a middle form (expected apātayat); I treat it as a plain active past, 'brought down'. The text does not name Niśumbha in this verse. I take the Daitya bull to be Niśumbha because 89.15 speaks of Niśumbha as fallen, after this verse. ↩
  30. [Śumbha]: The verse does not name the one who sets out; from the context he is Śumbha, Niśumbha's elder brother, the other chief asura. The subject is understood from 'brother' (bhrātari) and the following verses. ↩
  31. The reading: The subject of prayayau is not named in the verse. I supply Śumbha in brackets because 'in the brother' (bhrātari) implies the other brother and the following verses describe him; the verse itself does not say so. ↩
  32. he filled the whole sky: The subject sa is Śumbha, the brother who set out in 89.15. The absolutive vyāpya ('having pervaded') shares its agent with babhau ('shone'). ↩
  33. The reading: atyuccaiḥ ('very high') is an adverb, and the Sanskrit does not say which word it modifies. It could go with gṛhīta ('raised high and holding') or with vyāpya ('filling on high'). I took it with the arms and the weapons they hold. The subject sa is not named in the verse; I took it to be Śumbha, from 89.15. ↩
  34. conch: The text reads śaṅgham, which is probably a misprint for śaṅkham, 'conch'. ↩
  35. twang of her bow-string: jyāśabdaṃ dhanuṣaḥ: literally 'the sound of the string of the bow'. ↩
  36. The reading: śaṅgham in the e-text is probably a misprint for śaṅkham (conch); I translate it as 'conch'. The accusative object is the conch, and the verb avādayat 'made sound' is rendered 'blew'. The subject of samālokya is the Goddess, and the one seen is Śumbha, who is named in no verse here but implied by 89.15–16; the text says only 'him'. ↩
  37. the quarters of the sky: Sanskrit kakubh, here the regions of the sky; the subject of the verb is the Goddess, who blew the conch and twanged her bow in the previous verse. ↩
  38. loss of strength: The compound tejovadha may mean 'destruction of vigour' or 'slaying of splendour'; the bell's sound is said to bring about this in all the Daitya armies. ↩
  39. The reading: The verse has no stated subject; it follows 89.17, so the Goddess (Devī) is understood and I did not add her in brackets since 'she' suffices. tejovadha is ambiguous (destruction of vigour / slaying of brilliance); I chose 'loss of strength' as the plain sense. vidhāyin ('bringing about') is rendered as a relative clause. ↩
  40. abandon their mighty rut: The compound tyājitebhamahāmadaiḥ is read as 'by which the elephants were made to give up their great rut (mada)'; the roars frighten the elephants so that their musth dries up. ↩
  41. the ten directions: The eight quarters of the compass together with the zenith and the nadir. ↩
  42. The reading: The compound tyājitebhamahāmadaiḥ could be read as 'by which the great rut of the elephants was made to be abandoned' (chosen, with tyājita causative: elephants caused to give up their rut) or, less naturally, as describing roars that cause the rut itself to be shed. The sense is the same. 'Gām' is taken as 'earth' (accusative of go), not 'cow'. ↩
  43. the earlier sounds were drowned out: prāksvanāḥ is taken as 'the sounds [made] before', i.e. the conch, bow-string, bell and the lion's roars of the preceding verses; tirohitāḥ is literally 'were hidden, made to disappear'. ↩
  44. The reading: Text reads gaganaṃ kṣmām: 'sky' and 'earth' are both accusatives, and the sense is likely that Kālī leapt up into the sky and then struck the earth (as she came down). Alternatively gaganam could be read as the object of the leap (samutpatya gaganam), as translated here. Also prāksvanāḥ is read as 'earlier sounds'; a compound reading prāk-svanāḥ 'sounds before' is the one chosen, not a name or a separate word. ↩
  45. Śivadūtī: 'She who has Śiva as her messenger'; the Goddess's Śakti, who sent Śiva as an envoy to the asuras. ↩
  46. ill-omened, thunderous peal of laughter: aṭṭāṭṭahāsam is a loud, booming laugh (aṭṭa-aṭṭa-hāsa); aśivam, 'inauspicious', is a pointed contrast with her name Śivadūtī. ↩
  47. Ambikā: "Mother"; a name of the Goddess (Devī), from whose brow Kālī was born. ↩
  48. śakti: Here a śakti is a missile weapon, a spear or dart, and not the divine power. ↩
  49. [the Goddess]: The verse names no agent for the passive nirastā; the Goddess is understood from the context. maholkā is a great meteor or firebrand, taken to be a weapon of hers. ↩
  50. The reading: The agent of nirastā is not stated. I supply the Goddess, since she answers Śumbha's attack in the surrounding verses. The glossary has Śakti (power) as a term for the Goddess as the power of all things; here śakti is a weapon, so I keep it separate, in asterisks. The verse does not say what maholkā is: a meteor or a fiery missile hurled by the Goddess. I kept the literal "great meteor". ↩
  51. Śumbha's lion-roar: Śumbhasya may be taken with siṃhanādena, as here, or with the thunderclap-sound. The verse does not make plain whose roar or sound is meant. ↩
  52. a terrible sound like a thunderclap: nirghāta is a violent crash, such as thunder or a meteor's fall. The compound nirghātaniḥsvana is 'a sound like a nirghāta'. ↩
  53. The reading: The verse is awkward. (1) śumbhasya could qualify siṃhanādena (Śumbha's lion-roar) or nirghātaniḥsvano (Śumbha's thunderlike sound). I chose the first because the genitive stands beside siṃhanādena. (2) jitavān has no expressed object. I supplied 'it', meaning the filling roar, and took the thunderclap-sound as the subject. (3) The text may be slightly corrupt; the printed niḥ svano is read as one word, niḥsvanaḥ. ↩
  54. The Goddess: Devī, here the Goddess Ambikā/Caṇḍikā, who is fighting Śumbha. ↩
  55. The reading: The verse is elliptical: the verb ciccheda (singular) serves both subjects, Devī and Śumbha. 'svaśaraiḥ' (with her/his own arrows) is taken distributively, each cutting the other's arrows with his or her own. Both 'śumbhamuktān' and 'tatprahitān' are accusatives governed by ciccheda. ↩
  56. spear: śūla: a pointed weapon, spear or trident, the characteristic weapon of the Goddess (and of Śiva). ↩
  57. The reading: The verse does not say who 'him' (tam) is. The preceding verses concern Śumbha, so the referent is most likely Śumbha. However, verse 89.27 then has Niśumbha recover consciousness (sampräpya cetanām), which suggests the one struck down may be Niśumbha. The Sanskrit pronoun is kept as 'him'; the identity is left open. Also, śūlena may mean 'spear' or 'trident'; I chose 'spear'. ↩
  58. the lion: Sanskrit kesariṇam, 'the maned one', a word for the lion; this is the Goddess's mount (siṃha). ↩
  59. the lord of the Danu's sons: Danujeśvara: Niśumbha, called lord of the Danujas, the sons of Danu. He is also called Ditija, 'son of Diti', the term used for the asuras generally; the two epithets refer to the same person. ↩
  60. the son of Diti: Ditija: born of Diti, mother of the Daityas. Here it is a further epithet of the same Niśumbha, not another person. ↩
  61. The reading: The subject is not named in the verse. It follows from 89.27 and 89.30 that it is Niśumbha. Danujeśvara and ditija are taken as two epithets of that one person, not two persons. The compound bāhūnām ayutam is taken as 'ten thousand arms' (ayuta = 10,000). The glossary renders ayuta as a number, so I give the plain number. ↩
  62. Durgā, the destroyer of the distress of those in difficulty: The name Durgā is played on in durgārtināśinī, 'destroyer of the distress (ārti) that comes from difficulty (durga)'. It could also be read 'destroyer of the pain of Durga', but the sense of rescuing from hardship is the one intended. ↩
  63. The reading: durgārtināśinī: the compound can be read 'destroyer of the distress of difficulty' (durga + ārti) or 'destroyer of the distress of those in difficulty'. I chose the second as the natural sense of the epithet, with a pun on the name Durgā. The sāyakāṃś ca tān 'those arrows' presumably refers to the arrows Niśumbha shot along with the discs; it is not specified in the verse. ↩
  64. The reading: tasyāpatataḥ is a genitive absolute ("as he was rushing"); the genitive is rendered as 'as he was rushing at her', with 'her' implied. The subject of samādade is Niśumbha, the 'he' of tasya. The particle eva ('even as he was rushing') is rendered by 'at once' together with āśu, so that the sense of immediacy is kept. ↩
  65. the tormentor of the immortals: Sanskrit amarārdana, 'one who afflicts the gods'; an epithet of Niśumbha, distinct from the glossary's amarāri, used for Mahiṣa. ↩
  66. another man: The Sanskrit has aparaḥ ... puruṣaḥ, 'another man', a second being who issues from the wounded Niśumbha's heart. It is not a separate person named earlier in the verses. ↩
  67. The reading: The text prints hṛdayānniḥ sṛtaḥ, to be read hṛdayān niḥsṛtaḥ, 'came forth from the heart'. The ablative hṛdayāt is construed with niḥsṛtaḥ, and bhinnasya tasya is a genitive absolute ('of him, pierced'), rendered as 'From him, pierced'. The compounds mahābalo mahāvīryaḥ are taken as two adjectives of puruṣaḥ. ↩
  68. The reading: The word svanavat ("with a sound") is an adverb; I take it with prahasya, "laughing aloud". It could also be read as qualifying the cutting of the head, "with a ringing sound", but the position after prahasya favours the first reading. The word tataḥ ("then") is rendered as "and then". The second tato in the verse is rendered "thereupon". ↩
  69. Śivadūtī: 'She who has Śiva as her messenger'; a Śakti of the Goddess, here active beside Kālī. ↩
  70. The reading: The verb cakhāda ('devoured') is singular and its stated subject is the lion; Kālī and Śivadūtī (tathā ... tathā) are taken as further subjects, with the verb understood as repeated for them. Ugram is taken adverbially ('fiercely'), though it could be read as qualifying asurān ('fierce asuras'). I chose the adverbial reading because asurān already has its own qualifier, daṃṣṭrākṣuṇṇaśirodharān. ↩
  71. śakti of Kaumārī: Here śakti is the spear-like weapon carried by Kaumārī, the Mother-goddess of Kumāra (Skanda). Her name Kaumārī is already in the glossary; this is the weapon, not the Goddess's power. ↩
  72. driven off: nirākṛtāḥ means 'driven away, repulsed, dispelled'. It need not mean 'killed'; the verse contrasts those who perished (neśuḥ) with those merely repelled. ↩
  73. The reading: śakti is read as Kaumārī's weapon (the compound kaumārīśaktinirbhinnāḥ, 'pierced by'), not as her power in the abstract; I keep it lowercase and in IAST to distinguish it from the glossary's Śakti = power. nirākṛtāḥ is rendered 'driven off' rather than 'destroyed'. ↩
  74. Vārāhī's snout: Vārāhī is the Śakti of Viṣṇu's boar form, so she fights with the tuṇḍa, the snout or muzzle of a boar. ↩
  75. The reading: The printed cūrṇokṛtā is read as cūrṇīkṛtā, 'reduced to powder' (the e-text has -o- for -ī-, a misprint). The subject of petuḥ is 'others' (apare), parallel to 'some' (kecit) in the second half, and I translate accordingly. ↩
  76. the tip of Aindrī's hand: The compound haste-agra means literally 'the fingertip/front of the hand'; the thunderbolt is hurled from the hand of Aindrī, Indra's Śakti. ↩
  77. The reading: khaṇḍaṃ khaṇḍaṃ is a repeated (distributive) form, 'piece by piece'. The verb kṛtāḥ ('made') is understood with the second half, and I supply 'were slain' in brackets, since the Sanskrit implies but does not state a verb for the thunderbolt clause. ↩
  78. the lord of beasts: mṛgādhipa, 'lord of beasts' (mṛga, 'animal, beast'), is the lion, the Goddess's mount, mentioned in verse 35. ↩
  79. The reading: naṣṭā (from naś, 'be lost, perish, disappear') can mean 'perished' or 'fled, vanished'. After vineśuḥ ('perished') in the first clause, and with mahāhavāt ('from the great battle'), I took it as 'fled'. kālīśivadūtīmṛgādhipaiḥ is a dvandva compound in the plural, read as Kālī, Śivadūtī and the lion (the plural is natural for a three-member dvandva). ↩
  80. eighty-ninth chapter: The e-text's verse and chapter numbering is that of the GRETIL file; the colophon itself says ekonanavatitama, 'eighty-ninth', while the preceding verses are labelled 89.34–39 and the following chapter 90. ↩
  81. Devīmāhātmya: 'The Greatness of the Goddess'; the section of the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa (chapters 81–93 in the usual numbering) devoted to the Goddess's victories over the asuras. ↩
  82. The reading: The text reads devīmāhatmye (short a), a misprint for devīmāhātmye; I have read the latter. Also 'niśumbhavadhonāma' is one compound, niśumbhavadho nāma, 'named the Slaying of Niśumbha'. The numbering in the GRETIL file (verse labels '89.x' in the preceding verses) agrees with ekonanavatitama, 'eighty-ninth'. ↩

Adhyāya 90

Sanskrit

नवतितमो ऽध्यायः- ९०

navatitamo 'dhyāyaḥ- 90

English

The ninetieth chapter: 90.1

90.1

Sanskrit

ऋषिरुवाच निशुम्भं निहतं दृष्ट्वा भ्रातरं प्राणसंमितम् । हन्यमानं बलं चैव शुम्भः क्रुद्धो ऽब्रवीद्वचः ॥

ṛṣiruvāca niśumbhaṃ nihataṃ dṛṣṭvā bhrātaraṃ prāṇasaṃmitam | hanyamānaṃ balaṃ caiva śumbhaḥ kruddho 'bravīdvacaḥ ||

English

The sage said:2 Seeing Niśumbha slain, his brother, who was as dear to him as his own life-breath3, and seeing his army also being destroyed, Śumbha4 spoke these words in anger.5

90.2

Sanskrit

बलावलेपाद् दुष्टे त्वं मा दुर्गे गर्वमावह । अन्यासां बलमाश्रित्य युध्यसे यातिमानिनी ॥

balāvalepād duṣṭe tvaṃ mā durge garvamāvaha | anyāsāṃ balamāśritya yudhyase yātimāninī ||

English

"You wicked Durgā6, do not bring on yourself pride from the arrogance of [your] strength. Relying on the strength of other women7, you fight, though you are exceedingly proud."8

Sanskrit

श्रीदेव्युवाच

śrīdevyuvāca

English

The Blessed Goddess said:

90.3

Sanskrit

एकैवाहं जगत्यत्र द्वितीया का ममापरा । पश्यैता दुष्ट मय्येव विशन्त्यो मद्विभूतयः ॥

ekaivāhaṃ jagatyatra dvitīyā kā mamāparā | paśyaitā duṣṭa mayyeva viśantyo madvibhūtayaḥ ||

English

I am alone in this world. Who else is there, a second, apart from me? See, wicked one, these vibhūtis9 of mine entering into me alone.10

Sanskrit

ऋषिरुवाच

ṛṣiruvāca

English

The sage said:

90.4

Sanskrit

ततः समस्तास्ता देव्यो ब्रह्माणीप्रमुखा लयम् । तस्या देव्यास्तनौ जग्मुरेकावासीत्तदाम्बिका ॥

tataḥ samastāstā devyo brahmāṇīpramukhā layam | tasyā devyāstanau jagmurekāvāsīttadāmbikā ||

English

Then all those goddesses, with Brahmāṇī at their head, went into dissolution11 in the body of that Goddess, and at that time Ambikā12 was alone.

Sanskrit

श्रीदेव्युवाच

śrīdevyuvāca

English

The Blessed Goddess said:

90.5

Sanskrit

अहं विभूत्या बहुभिरिह रूपैर्यदास्थिता । सत्संहृतं मयैकैव तिष्ठाम्याजौ स्थिरो भव ॥

ahaṃ vibhūtyā bahubhiriha rūpairyadāsthitā | satsaṃhṛtaṃ mayaikaiva tiṣṭhāmyājau sthiro bhava ||

English

I was present here through my vibhūti in many forms; that has been withdrawn13 by me. I stand alone in the battle. Be steady.14

Sanskrit

ऋषिरुवाच

ṛṣiruvāca

English

The sage said:

90.6

Sanskrit

ततः प्रववृते युद्धं देव्याः शुम्भस्य चोभयोः । पश्यतां सर्वदेवानामसुराणां च दारुणम् ॥

tataḥ pravavṛte yuddhaṃ devyāḥ śumbhasya cobhayoḥ | paśyatāṃ sarvadevānāmasurāṇāṃ ca dāruṇam ||

English

Then a terrible battle broke out between the Goddess and Śumbha, the two of them, while all the gods and the asuras looked on.15

90.7

Sanskrit

शरवर्षैः शितैः शस्त्रैस्तथा चास्त्रैः सुदारुणैः । तयोर्युद्धमभूद् भूयः सर्वलोकभयङ्करम् ॥

śaravarṣaiḥ śitaiḥ śastraistathā cāstraiḥ sudāruṇaiḥ | tayoryuddhamabhūd bhūyaḥ sarvalokabhayaṅkaram ||

English

With showers of arrows, with sharp weapons, and with most terrible missiles, there was again a battle between the two of them, terrifying to all the worlds.16

90.8

Sanskrit

दिव्यान्यस्त्राणि शतशो मुमुचे यान्यथाम्बिका । बभञ्ज तानि दैत्येन्द्रस्तत्प्रतीघातकर्तृभिः ॥

divyānyastrāṇi śataśo mumuce yānyathāmbikā | babhañja tāni daityendrastatpratīghātakartṛbhiḥ ||

English

Then the divine missiles that Ambikā17 hurled by the hundred, the lord of the Daityas shattered with those [missiles] that served to counter them18.19

90.9

Sanskrit

मुक्तानि तेन चास्त्राणि दिव्यानि परमेश्वरी । बभञ्ज लीलयैवोग्रहुङ्कारोच्चारणादिभिः ॥

muktāni tena cāstrāṇi divyāni parameśvarī | babhañja līlayaivograhuṅkāroccāraṇādibhiḥ ||

English

And the divine missiles hurled by him the Parameśvarī shattered in sport, by uttering a fierce roar of "hūṃ" and the like20.21†

90.10

Sanskrit

ततः शरशतैर्देवीमाच्छादयत सो ऽसुरः । सा च तत्कुपिता देवी धनुश्चिच्छेद चेषुभिः ॥

tataḥ śaraśatairdevīmācchādayata so 'suraḥ | sā ca tatkupitā devī dhanuściccheda ceṣubhiḥ ||

English

Then that asura22 covered the Goddess with hundreds of arrows; and the Goddess, enraged at that, cut his bow in two with her arrows.23

90.11

Sanskrit

छिन्ने धनुषि दैत्येन्द्रस्तथा शक्तिमथाददे । चिच्छेद देवी चक्रेण तामप्यस्य करे स्थिताम् ॥

chinne dhanuṣi daityendrastathā śaktimathādade | ciccheda devī cakreṇa tāmapyasya kare sthitām ||

English

When the bow was cut, the lord of the Daityas then took up a spear (śakti)24; and the Goddess cut that too with her disc, as it lay in his hand.25

90.12

Sanskrit

ततः खड्गमुपादाय शतचन्द्रं च भानुमत् । अभ्यधावत्तदा देवीं दैत्यानामधिपेश्वरः ॥

tataḥ khaḍgamupādāya śatacandraṃ ca bhānumat | abhyadhāvattadā devīṃ daityānāmadhipeśvaraḥ ||

English

Then, taking up a sword with a hundred moons26 and shining brightly, the sovereign lord of the Daityas27 rushed at the Goddess at that moment.28

90.13

Sanskrit

तस्यापतत एवाशु खड्गं चिच्छेद चण्डिका । धनुर्मुक्तैः शितैर्बाणैश्चर्म चार्ककरामलम् ॥

tasyāpatata evāśu khaḍgaṃ ciccheda caṇḍikā | dhanurmuktaiḥ śitairbāṇaiścarma cārkakarāmalam ||

English

As he was rushing on, Caṇḍikā quickly cut his sword in two, and also his shield, bright as the rays of the sun29, with sharp arrows shot from her bow.30

90.14

Sanskrit

अश्वांश्च पातयामास रथं सारथिना सह । हताश्वः स तदा दैत्यश्छिन्नधान्वा विसारथिः । जग्राह मुद्गरं घोरमम्बिकानिधनोद्यतः ॥

aśvāṃśca pātayāmāsa rathaṃ sārathinā saha | hatāśvaḥ sa tadā daityaśchinnadhānvā visārathiḥ | jagrāha mudgaraṃ ghoramambikānidhanodyataḥ ||

English

And she brought down his horses, and his chariot together with its charioteer. Then that Daitya, his horses slain, his bow broken, and his charioteer gone, seized a fearsome mace (mudgara)31, intent on killing Ambikā.32

90.15

Sanskrit

चिच्छेदापततस्तस्य मुद्गरं निशितैः शरैः । तथापि सो ऽभ्यधावत्तां मुष्टिमुद्यम्य वेगवान् ॥

cicchedāpatatastasya mudgaraṃ niśitaiḥ śaraiḥ | tathāpi so 'bhyadhāvattāṃ muṣṭimudyamya vegavān ||

English

She cut his mace in two with sharp arrows as he came at her. Even so, he rushed at her, swift in his speed, raising his fist.33

90.16

Sanskrit

स मुष्टिं पातयामास हृदये दैत्यपुङ्गवः । देव्यास्तं चापि सा देवी तलेनोरस्यताडयत् ॥

sa muṣṭiṃ pātayāmāsa hṛdaye daityapuṅgavaḥ | devyāstaṃ cāpi sā devī talenorasyatāḍayat ||

English

That bull among the Daityas brought down his fist on the heart of the Goddess; and the Goddess in turn struck him on the chest with the palm of her hand.34

90.17

Sanskrit

तलप्रहाराभिहतो निपपात महीतले । स दैत्यराजः सहसा पुनरेव तथोत्थितः ॥

talaprahārābhihato nipapāta mahītale | sa daityarājaḥ sahasā punareva tathotthitaḥ ||

English

Struck by the blow of her palm, he fell to the surface of the earth; but that king of the Daityas suddenly rose up again in the same way.35

90.18

Sanskrit

उत्पत्य च प्रगृह्योच्चैर्देवीं गगनमास्थितः । तत्रापि सा निराधारा युयुधे तेन चण्डिका ॥

utpatya ca pragṛhyoccairdevīṃ gaganamāsthitaḥ | tatrāpi sā nirādhārā yuyudhe tena caṇḍikā ||

English

And leaping up and seizing the Goddess high aloft, he took his stand in the sky. Even there, without any support36, Caṇḍikā fought with him.37

90.19

Sanskrit

नियुद्धं खे तदा दैत्यश्चण्डिका च परस्परम् । चक्रतुः प्रथमं सिद्धमुनिविस्मयकारकम् ॥

niyuddhaṃ khe tadā daityaścaṇḍikā ca parasparam | cakratuḥ prathamaṃ siddhamunivismayakārakam ||

English

Then, in the sky, the Daitya and Caṇḍikā fought each other in close combat, the first of its kind38, which filled the siddhas39 and sages with wonder.40

90.20

Sanskrit

ततो नियुद्धं सुचिरं कृत्वा तेनाम्बिका सह । उत्पात्य भ्रामयामास चिक्षेप धरणीतले ॥

tato niyuddhaṃ suciraṃ kṛtvā tenāmbikā saha | utpātya bhrāmayāmāsa cikṣepa dharaṇītale ||

English

Then Ambikā, having fought a very long close combat with him, lifted him up, whirled him around, and hurled him down onto the surface of the earth.

90.21

Sanskrit

स क्षिप्तो धरणीं प्राप्य मुष्टिमुद्यम्य वेगितः । अभ्यधावत दुष्टात्मा चण्डिकानिधनेच्छया ॥

sa kṣipto dharaṇīṃ prāpya muṣṭimudyamya vegitaḥ | abhyadhāvata duṣṭātmā caṇḍikānidhanecchayā ||

English

Hurled down, he reached the earth, and, raising his fist, that evil-souled one rushed furiously at her, wishing to kill Caṇḍikā.41

90.22

Sanskrit

तमायान्तं ततो देवी सर्वदैत्यजनेश्वरम् । जगत्यां पातयामास भित्त्वा शूलेन वक्षसि ॥

tamāyāntaṃ tato devī sarvadaityajaneśvaram | jagatyāṃ pātayāmāsa bhittvā śūlena vakṣasi ||

English

Then the Goddess, having pierced him in the chest with her spear42 as he came at her, that lord of all the Daitya people, threw him down on the ground43.44

90.23

Sanskrit

स गतासुः पपातोर्व्यां देवीशूलाग्रविक्षतः । चालयन् सकलां पृथ्वीं साब्धिद्वीपां सपर्वताम् ॥

sa gatāsuḥ papātorvyāṃ devīśūlāgravikṣataḥ | cālayan sakalāṃ pṛthvīṃ sābdhidvīpāṃ saparvatām ||

English

Lifeless, he fell upon the earth, torn open by the point of the Goddess's spear, shaking the whole earth45 with its oceans, islands and mountains.46

90.24

Sanskrit

ततः प्रसन्नमखिलं हते तस्मिन् दुरात्मनि । जगत्स्वास्थ्यमतीवाप निर्मलं चाभवन्नभः ॥

tataḥ prasannamakhilaṃ hate tasmin durātmani | jagatsvāsthyamatīvāpa nirmalaṃ cābhavannabhaḥ ||

English

Then, when that evil-souled one had been slain, the whole [world] grew serene, the world attained great well-being, and the sky became free of stain.47

90.25

Sanskrit

उत्पातमेघाः सोल्का ये प्रागासंस्ते शमं ययुः । सरितो मार्गवाहिन्यस्तथासंस्तत्र पातिते ॥

utpātameghāḥ solkā ye prāgāsaṃste śamaṃ yayuḥ | sarito mārgavāhinyastathāsaṃstatra pātite ||

English

The portent-clouds48 with their meteors that had appeared before were stilled, and when he was struck down there, the rivers too flowed within their proper courses.49

90.26

Sanskrit

ततो देवगणाः सर्वे हर्षनिर्भमानसाः । बभूवुर्निहते तस्मिन् गन्धर्वा ललितं जगुः ॥

tato devagaṇāḥ sarve harṣanirbhamānasāḥ | babhūvurnihate tasmin gandharvā lalitaṃ jaguḥ ||

English

Then, when he had been slain, all the hosts of the devas became joyful in mind, and the Gandharvas sang sweetly.50

90.27

Sanskrit

अवादयंस्तथैवान्ये ननृतुश्चाप्सरोगणाः । ववुः पुण्यास्तथा वाताः सुप्रभो ऽभूद्दिवाकरः ॥

avādayaṃstathaivānye nanṛtuścāpsarogaṇāḥ | vavuḥ puṇyāstathā vātāḥ suprabho 'bhūddivākaraḥ ||

English

90.27Others likewise played [instruments], and the hosts of Apsarases51 danced. Auspicious winds blew, and the sun became brightly shining. The fires blazed, tranquil, and the sounds that had arisen in the quarters52 were stilled.53

Sanskrit

इति श्रीमार्कण्डेयपुराणे सावर्णिके मन्वन्तरे देवीमाहात्म्ये शुम्भवधोनाम नवतितमो ऽध्यायः

iti śrīmārkaṇḍeyapurāṇe sāvarṇike manvantare devīmāhātmye śumbhavadhonāma navatitamo 'dhyāyaḥ

English

Thus ends the ninetieth54 chapter, named "The Slaying of Śumbha", in the Devīmāhātmya, in the Sāvarṇika manvantara55, in the revered Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa.56

  1. The reading: The line is a chapter heading, not a verse: navatitamaḥ adhyāyaḥ, 'the ninetieth chapter', followed by the numeral 90. The Sanskrit has no verb, so none is supplied. The numeral is rendered once more as the text gives it. ↩
  2. The sage said:: The speaker is the ṛṣi, i.e. Medhas, who narrates the Devīmāhātmya to King Suratha and the merchant; the text here says only ṛṣi. ↩
  3. as dear to him as his own life-breath: Sanskrit prāṇasaṃmita, 'equal to life-breath', i.e. as precious as life itself. ↩
  4. Śumbha: The elder asura brother of Niśumbha and chief enemy of the Goddess in this episode. ↩
  5. The reading: The e-text reads prāṇasaṃmitam; I take it as 'equal to life-breath', qualifying bhrātaram. Balam is taken as 'army', not 'strength', since it is described as being slain (hanyamānam), which suits the army. Caiva is rendered 'also'. ↩
  6. Durgā: Śumbha addresses the Goddess by this name, 'the hard to reach'; here it is used contemptuously. The glossary has the name. ↩
  7. other women: anyāsām is feminine plural, 'of other [women]'. It refers to the Śaktis (Brahmāṇī, Māheśvarī, Aindrī and the rest) who fight beside the Goddess. ↩
  8. The reading: duṣṭe is vocative feminine singular of duṣṭā, 'wicked one', and is taken with durge. The Sanskrit has a single imperative, mā ... āvaha. yātimāninī (yā atimāninī) is read as 'you who are exceedingly proud', with yā as the relative 'who' in a sentence that is not fully relative. The alternative is to read it as an unmarked compound; I kept the first reading. The line is also read with ā-√vah as 'bring on yourself, display'. ↩
  9. vibhūtis: Manifestations or emanations of power; here the Śaktis such as Brahmāṇī who came forth from the Goddess and now re-enter her. ↩
  10. The reading: The speaker's line (śrīdevyuvāca) occurs in the preceding verse block, so it is not repeated here. 'dvitīyā kā mamāparā' is read as 'who else is a second apart from me'; 'aparā' is taken as 'other' (feminine), agreeing with kā. 'jagati atra' is taken as 'in this world'. ↩
  11. went into dissolution: layam ... jagmuḥ, literally 'went to dissolution'. tanau is a locative, 'in the body'. The goddesses, called her vibhūtis in 90.3, are absorbed back into her. ↩
  12. Ambikā: 'Mother'; the name is a diminutive-form of ambā. It is a name of the Goddess, used here for Durgā, who spoke as the Blessed Goddess in 90.3. ↩
  13. that has been withdrawn: The e-text reads satsaṃhṛtaṃ, evidently a misprint or sandhi-misdivision for tat saṃhṛtam, 'that withdrawn'. The relative yad in the first half is picked up by tat. ↩
  14. The reading: satsaṃhṛtaṃ in the e-text: 'well withdrawn' (sat + saṃhṛtam) or tat saṃhṛtam ('that has been withdrawn'). I chose tat saṃhṛtam, since yad ... tat is the expected correlative pair and it matches the usual reading of this verse. In the first half, yad āsthitā ('in which I was present/established') could also be taken as 'since I was present'; I took it as a relative clause answered by tat. ↩
  15. The reading: The sage's speaker line (ṛṣiruvāca) is at 90.5/746.1, before this verse, so it is not repeated here. 'Śumbha' is taken as the second combatant by name, as in the glossary. 'ubhayoḥ' is rendered 'the two of them' because it reinforces 'devyāḥ śumbhasya ca' as a pair. ↩
  16. The reading: bhūyaḥ can mean 'again' (the fighting resumed or continued in a new form) or 'further, more, exceedingly'. As the reviewer says, it goes with abhūt, not with the adjective, so I dropped 'grew still more fearful' and chose 'again'. This fits the sequence from 90.6, where the battle has already begun and this verse describes how it was fought. I kept the instrumentals in the Sanskrit order: śitaiḥ goes with śastraiḥ and sudāruṇaiḥ with astraiḥ, so 'sharp' and 'most terrible' each stay with their own noun. 'The two of them' renders tayoḥ, meaning the Goddess and Śumbha of 90.6. I removed the note on astra and śastra because the glossary already explains them. ↩
  17. Ambikā: 'Little Mother', a name of the Goddess (Devī) in this text. ↩
  18. those [missiles] that served to counter them: tatpratīghātakartṛbhiḥ is an instrumental plural of agent or means: 'by the producers of their counter-stroke'. The noun is not stated; astra is understood from the preceding clause. ↩
  19. The reading: tatpratīghātakartṛbhiḥ has no noun expressed. I take it as counter-missiles (astras) that neutralize the Goddess's astras. It could also mean 'by those who devise counter-strokes', that is, by his own skill or agents. The context of astra-warfare favours missiles. ↩
  20. by uttering a fierce roar of "hūṃ" and the like: ugrahuṅkāroccāraṇādibhiḥ: the fierce sound "hūṃ" and similar means, by which the Goddess breaks the missiles without using weapons. ↩
  21. The reading: līlayaiva: 'in sport' (effortlessly, as play); the emphatic eva is left untranslated except as carried by 'in sport'. ādi in the compound 'and the like' is rendered loosely; the instrumental plural refers to the means used, not only the hūṃkāra. ↩
  22. that asura: The asura is Śumbha, as the following verses (90.11–12), which call him lord of the Daityas, show. ↩
  23. The reading: tatkupitā (tat + kupitā) is read as 'enraged at that', that is, at his covering her with arrows. An alternative is 'then enraged' (tat as adverb). The first reading is chosen because tat stands in composition with kupitā. The pronoun so 'suraḥ is taken as Śumbha on the strength of the next verses, though the glossary marks his identity as unclear. ↩
  24. spear (śakti): Here śakti is a hurled weapon, a spear or lance, and not the divine power of the glossary. The word is the same. ↩
  25. The reading: śakti: the glossary entry 'power' is the divine energy; here the word means the weapon (spear, lance). I render it as 'spear' with the Sanskrit word marked. tathā: taken as 'then' (connective), not 'likewise'. apy asya: 'too' marks that the spear was cut after the bow; asya 'of him' qualifies kare 'in his hand'. ↩
  26. a sword with a hundred moons: śatacandra: the sword is marked with a hundred moon-like discs, and the same epithet is used of a shield in the next verse. ↩
  27. sovereign lord of the Daityas: daityānām adhipeśvara: Śumbha, the lord of the Daityas. The compound adhipa-īśvara puts two words for ruler together. ↩
  28. The reading: Śatacandra could mean a sword marked with a hundred moons, or a sword with a hundred moon-like bosses. I chose the first, the usual reading. Bhānumat, 'shining', is taken as a second adjective of khaḍga. The verse does not name Śumbha; the epithet refers to him by context. ↩
  29. bright as the rays of the sun: arkakarāmalam: 'spotless (or bright) like the sun's rays'; the compound can also be read 'spotless with the sun's rays', the shield being polished and gleaming. Both readings give the same sense of brightness. ↩
  30. The reading: arkakarāmalam is ambiguous: 'bright as the sun's rays' (comparison) or 'made bright by the sun's rays'. I chose the comparison. Also, 'tasyāpatata eva' is rendered 'as he was rushing on'; eva stresses that she cut it while he was still advancing. ↩
  31. mace (mudgara): A mudgara is a heavy club or mace, here the last weapon the asura takes up before he fights with his fist. ↩
  32. The reading: The subject of pātayāmāsa is not stated. It follows 90.13 and is the Goddess (Caṇḍikā), so I supply 'she'. The word chinnadhānvā is read as 'with his bow cut', from dhanvan, the bow cut in 90.10. The chariot is the Daitya's. The compound ambikānidhanodyataḥ is taken as 'intent on the slaying of Ambikā'. ↩
  33. The reading: The verse has no stated subject for 'cicchheda'; the subject is understood as the Goddess (Caṇḍikā/Ambikā), continuing from 90.13–14, and 'tasya' is the Daitya (Śumbha's follower) whose mace is cut. 'Āpatataḥ' agrees with 'tasya' (genitive absolute-like construction, 'as he was falling upon [her]'). The edition reads 'cicchedāpatatas', which I take as cicceda + āpatataḥ. ↩
  34. The reading: 1. devyāḥ hṛdaye: devyāḥ is genitive and goes with hṛdaye, so 'on the heart of the Goddess'. Daityapuṅgavaḥ is the subject of pātayāmāsa. The reviewer is right that the word order should make this clear, and I have moved the phrase accordingly. 2. taṃ cāpi: taṃ ('him') is the Daitya, since the Goddess is the subject of atāḍayat. 'Too' in my draft could be taken to mean she was also struck on the chest. I have replaced it with 'in turn', which gives the sense of api here: she answers his blow with one of her own. The chest (uras, locative urasi, by sandhi tala-enorasy-) is the Daitya's, and 90.17 confirms this when he falls from the palm-blow. ↩
  35. The reading: The verse is taken as a single sentence. The glossary lists Daityarāja as in context Śumbha, but the preceding verses (90.11–16) present the Daitya who fights as Niśumbha, 'bull among the Daityas' in 90.16. Here daityarājaḥ is rendered 'king of the Daityas' as the text has it, and it refers to the same fighter. The word tathā ('in the same way') is read with utthitaḥ and means that he rose just as he had fallen, that is, at once and with force. It could also be read as 'thus', but the sense is unchanged. ↩
  36. without any support: nirādhārā agrees with Caṇḍikā: she fights in the air with nothing to stand on. ↩
  37. The reading: The subject of utpatya, pragṛhya and āsthitaḥ is the Daitya of the preceding verse (the king of the Daityas, Niśumbha or Śumbha according to the glossary's identifications), not named in this verse. pragṛhya uccaiḥ could mean 'seizing her' and 'on high'; I took uccaiḥ as 'high aloft', modifying the action of seizing and rising. gaganam āsthitaḥ is taken as 'took his stand in the sky'. ↩
  38. the first of its kind: prathamam is taken as an adjective of niyuddham, 'first, unprecedented'. It could also be an adverb, 'at first', as the opening of the combat, which continues in 90.20. ↩
  39. siddhas: Semi-divine beings of perfected powers who dwell between earth and heaven. ↩
  40. The reading: prathamam: adjective of niyuddham ('the first, unprecedented combat') or adverb ('at first'). I chose the adjective because the compound siddhamunivismayakārakam, 'causing wonder', fits a combat without precedent. Siddhamuni- is read as a dvandva, 'siddhas and sages', not as 'sage-siddhas'. ↩
  41. The reading: The subject 'sa' is not named in the verse; from the context (90.20) it is the Daitya Niśumbha, but the verse itself gives no name, so I leave it as 'he'. 'Vegitaḥ' (furious, impelled by speed) is taken with the subject, as 'furiously'. 'Caṇḍikānidhanecchayā' is a compound: 'with the wish for the death of Caṇḍikā', rendered 'wishing to kill Caṇḍikā'. ↩
  42. spear: The Sanskrit is śūla, a pike or trident-spear, the Goddess's weapon. ↩
  43. ground: The Sanskrit has jagatyām, locative of jagatī, 'earth, ground'. Here it is a different word from the glossary's jagat, 'the world'. ↩
  44. The reading: sarvadaityajaneśvaram: it could mean 'lord of all the Daitya people' or 'lord of all the Daityas and [other] people'. I took it as 'lord of all the Daitya people', as the compound most naturally reads. The referent is Niśumbha, 'that bull among the Daityas' of 90.16 and 90.21. jagatyām is read as 'on the ground' and not as 'in the world'. ↩
  45. shaking the whole earth: The participle cālayan agrees with the subject, the fallen Daityarāja; it is his fall that shakes the earth, not the spear's. ↩
  46. The reading: gatāsuḥ (one whose breath has gone) is taken as 'lifeless', agreeing with sa. The compound devīśūlāgravikṣataḥ is read as 'wounded/torn by the point of the Goddess's spear'; the sense 'pierced' is equally possible. The e-text papātorvyām is papāta + urvyām (sandhi), 'fell upon the earth'. ↩
  47. The reading: The Sanskrit has 'prasannam akhilam' with no noun. 'The whole [world]' is supplied as the likeliest subject, since 'jagat' follows immediately. 'Akhilam' could also be taken as an adverb ('entirely clear'), and 'prasanna' could mean 'clear' or 'tranquil'. I chose 'serene' with 'world' supplied. 'Jagatsvāsthyam' is read as 'jagat' and 'svāsthyam', 'the world' and 'well-being', with 'jagat' as the subject of 'āpa': the world attained well-being. ↩
  48. portent-clouds: utpāta: an ominous natural phenomenon, such as meteors or unseasonal clouds, taken as a sign of coming disaster. The compound utpātameghāḥ can also be read as 'clouds that were portents'. ↩
  49. The reading: prāgāsaṃs is taken as prāk + āsan, 'were before'. The verse has no verb in the second half beyond āsan, so I read 'tathā āsan' as 'were likewise', with the participle mārgavāhinyaḥ as the predicate. 'Within their proper courses' renders mārgavāhinyaḥ, literally 'flowing in the path'. Pātite (locative absolute) is read as referring to the Daitya, Caṇḍa, struck down in the preceding verses, with tatra meaning 'there' (on the battlefield), though 'then' is also possible. ↩
  50. The reading: The text reads harṣanirbhamānasāḥ, which is probably a misprint or corruption. The usual reading is harṣanirbharamānasāḥ, 'with minds full of joy'. I translate it as 'joyful in mind' on that basis. Another possible reading is that nirbha stands for nirbhara, 'full', which gives the same sense. Also, lalitam may be taken as an adverb, 'sweetly' (my choice), or as the object, 'a sweet song'. ↩
  51. Apsarases: Apsaras: a celestial nymph, a dancer of the heavens who attends the Gandharvas and the devas. ↩
  52. the sounds that had arisen in the quarters: digjanitasvanāḥ: 'sounds produced in the directions', probably the ominous cries and roars that marked the portents; see the doubts. ↩
  53. The reading: The second line, śāntāḥ ... śāntā digjanitasvanāḥ, can be read two ways. (1) śāntāḥ qualifies agnayaḥ, and digjanitasvanāḥ is a separate subject, 'the sounds arisen in the quarters', with its own śāntāḥ (the visarga is dropped before d). (2) digjanitasvanāḥ is a bahuvrīhi qualifying the fires, 'the fires, which had made sounds in the quarters, were calmed'. I chose (1), because the portent-sounds are naturally a separate item, in the same list as the stilled portent-clouds of 90.25. The first śāntāḥ is rendered 'tranquil', in agreement with the fires. ↩
  54. ninetieth: The text gives navatitama, 'ninetieth'; the verse numbering in this e-text (749.2) does not match the chapter heading (85) given in the header. ↩
  55. Sāvarṇika manvantara: The age of the eighth Manu, Sāvarṇi; the Devīmāhātmya is set within the account of this age. ↩
  56. The reading: śumbhavadhonāma is read as śumbhavadho nāma, 'named the Slaying of Śumbha'. The adjective śrī- in the compound is taken as an honorific of the Purāṇa ('revered'). The chapter number is as in the text (ninetieth), though the header says chapter 85. ↩

Adhyāya 91

Sanskrit

जज्वलुश्चाग्नयः शान्ताः शान्ता दिग्जनितस्वनाः ।

jajvaluścāgnayaḥ śāntāḥ śāntā digjanitasvanāḥ |

English

Its English is given with 90.27, above.

Sanskrit

एकनवतितमो ऽध्यायः- ९१

ekanavatitamo 'dhyāyaḥ- 91

English

The ninety-first chapter.1

Sanskrit

ऋषिरुवाच

ṛṣiruvāca

English

The ṛṣi said:

91.1

Sanskrit

देव्या हते तत्र महासुरेन्द्रे सेन्द्राः सुरा वह्निपुरोगमास्ताम् । कात्यायनीं तुष्टुवुरिष्टलाभाद् विकाशिवक्त्राब्जविकाशिताशाः ॥

devyā hate tatra mahāsurendre sendrāḥ surā vahnipurogamāstām | kātyāyanīṃ tuṣṭuvuriṣṭalābhād vikāśivaktrābjavikāśitāśāḥ ||

English

When the Goddess had slain the great lord of the asuras there, the devas, together with Indra and led by Agni, praised Kātyāyanī2, since what they desired had been gained; their faces were open like blooming lotuses, and the quarters of the sky were brightened3.4

Sanskrit

देवा ऊचुः

devā ūcuḥ

English

The devas said:

91.2

Sanskrit

देवि प्रपन्नार्तिहरे प्रसीद प्रसीद मातर्जगतो ऽखिलस्य । प्रसीद विश्वेश्वरि पाहि विश्वं त्वमीश्वरी देवि चराचरस्य ॥

devi prapannārtihare prasīda prasīda mātarjagato 'khilasya | prasīda viśveśvari pāhi viśvaṃ tvamīśvarī devi carācarasya ||

English

O Goddess, remover of the distress of those who have sought refuge in you, be gracious. Be gracious, Mother of the whole world. Be gracious, Sovereign Lady of the universe5, and protect the universe. You, Goddess, are the sovereign lady of all that moves and does not move.6

91.3

Sanskrit

आधारभूता जगतस्त्वमेका महीस्वरूपेण यतः स्थितासि । अपां स्वरूपस्थितया त्वयैतद् आप्याय्यते कृत्स्त्रमलङ्घ्यवीर्ये ॥

ādhārabhūtā jagatastvamekā mahīsvarūpeṇa yataḥ sthitāsi | apāṃ svarūpasthitayā tvayaitad āpyāyyate kṛtstramalaṅghyavīrye ||

English

You alone are the support of the world, since you abide in the form of the earth. And by you, abiding in the form of the waters, all this is refreshed, O you of irresistible vigor7.8

91.4

Sanskrit

त्वं वैष्णवी शक्तिरनन्तवीर्या विश्वस्य बीजं परमासि माया । संमोहितं देवि समस्तमेतत् तेवं वै प्रसन्ना भुवि मुक्तिहेतुः ॥

tvaṃ vaiṣṇavī śaktiranantavīryā viśvasya bījaṃ paramāsi māyā | saṃmohitaṃ devi samastametat tevaṃ vai prasannā bhuvi muktihetuḥ ||

English

You are the Vaiṣṇavī9 Śakti of endless vigor; you are the supreme māyā, the seed of the universe. All this, O Goddess, has been bewildered; you alone, when you are gracious, are the cause of release on earth.10

91.5

Sanskrit

विद्याः समस्तास्तव देवि भेदाः स्त्रियः समस्ताः सकला जगत्सु । त्वयैकया पूरितमम्बयैतत् का ते स्तुतिः स्तव्यपरा परोक्तिः ॥

vidyāḥ samastāstava devi bhedāḥ striyaḥ samastāḥ sakalā jagatsu | tvayaikayā pūritamambayaitat kā te stutiḥ stavyaparā paroktiḥ ||

English

All the branches of knowledge, O Goddess, are your divisions; all women, every one, throughout the worlds, [are your divisions as well]. All this is filled by you alone, the Mother11. What praise of you is there, one that goes beyond what is to be praised12, an utterance of another13?14

91.6

Sanskrit

सर्वभूता यदा देवी स्वर्गमुक्तिप्रदायिनी । त्वं स्तुता स्तुतये का वा भवन्तु परमोक्तयः ॥

sarvabhūtā yadā devī svargamuktipradāyinī | tvaṃ stutā stutaye kā vā bhavantu paramoktayaḥ ||

English

When you, Goddess, who are all beings15 and the giver of heaven and release, have been praised, what supreme utterances16 could serve for praise?17

91.7

Sanskrit

सर्वस्य बुद्धिरूपेण जनस्य हृदि संस्थिते । स्वर्गापवर्गदे देवि नारायणि नमो ऽस्तु ते ॥

sarvasya buddhirūpeṇa janasya hṛdi saṃsthite | svargāpavargade devi nārāyaṇi namo 'stu te ||

English

O Goddess who abide in the heart of every person in the form of intellect, giver of heaven and of final release18, Nārāyaṇī19, homage to you.

91.8

Sanskrit

कलाकाष्ठादिरूपेण परिणामप्रदायिनी । विश्वस्योपरतौ शक्ते नारायणि नमो ऽस्तु ते ॥

kalākāṣṭhādirūpeṇa pariṇāmapradāyinī | viśvasyoparatau śakte nārāyaṇi namo 'stu te ||

English

O giver of change20 in the form of the kalā, the kāṣṭhā and the rest21, O you who are capable at the cessation of the universe22, Nārāyaṇī, homage to you.23

91.9

Sanskrit

सर्वमङ्गलमाङ्गल्ये शिवे सर्वार्थसाधिके । शरण्ये त्र्यम्बके गौरि नारायणि नमो ऽस्तु ते ॥

sarvamaṅgalamāṅgalye śive sarvārthasādhike | śaraṇye tryambake gauri nārāyaṇi namo 'stu te ||

English

O you who are the auspiciousness of all that is auspicious, Śivā24, accomplisher of every aim, giver of refuge, three-eyed one25, Gaurī, Nārāyaṇī, homage to you.26

91.10

Sanskrit

सृष्टिस्थितिविनाशानां शक्तिभूते सनातनि । गुणाश्रये गुणमये नारायणि नमो ऽस्तु ते ॥

sṛṣṭisthitivināśānāṃ śaktibhūte sanātani | guṇāśraye guṇamaye nārāyaṇi namo 'stu te ||

English

O you who are the Śakti of creation, preservation and destruction, eternal one, support of the guṇas27, formed of the guṇas, Nārāyaṇī, homage to you.28

91.11

Sanskrit

शरणागतदीनार्तपरित्राणपरायणे । सर्वस्यार्तिहरे देवि नारायणि नमो ऽस्तु ते ॥

śaraṇāgatadīnārtaparitrāṇaparāyaṇe | sarvasyārtihare devi nārāyaṇi namo 'stu te ||

English

O you who are wholly devoted to rescuing those who have come for refuge, the wretched and the afflicted, O remover of the suffering of all, Goddess, Nārāyaṇī, homage to you.29

91.12

Sanskrit

हंसयुक्तविमानस्थे ब्रह्माणीरूपधारिणि । कौशाम्भः क्षरिके देवि नारायणि नमो ऽस्तु ते ॥

haṃsayuktavimānasthe brahmāṇīrūpadhāriṇi | kauśāmbhaḥ kṣarike devi nārāyaṇi namo 'stu te ||

English

O you who stand in the vimāna yoked with swans, who assume the form of Brahmāṇī, who pour out water [sprinkled] with kuśa31 grass30, Goddess, Nārāyaṇī, homage to you.32

91.13

Sanskrit

त्रिशूलचन्द्राहिधरे महावृषभवाहिनि । माहेश्वरीस्वरूपेण नारायणि नमो ऽस्तु ते ॥

triśūlacandrāhidhare mahāvṛṣabhavāhini | māheśvarīsvarūpeṇa nārāyaṇi namo 'stu te ||

English

O you who bear the trident, the moon and the serpent33, who ride the great bull, Nārāyaṇī, in your own form as Māheśvarī34, homage to you.35

91.14

Sanskrit

मयूरकुक्कुटवृते महाशक्तिधरे ऽमघे । कौमारीरूपसंस्थाने नारायणि नमो ऽस्तु ते ॥

mayūrakukkuṭavṛte mahāśaktidhare 'maghe | kaumārīrūpasaṃsthāne nārāyaṇi namo 'stu te ||

English

O you who are surrounded by peacocks and cocks, who bear the great spear36, O sinless one37, who abide in the form of Kaumārī, Nārāyaṇī, homage to you.38

91.15

Sanskrit

शङ्खचक्रगदाशार्ङ्गगृहीतपरमायुधे । प्रसीद वैष्णवीरूपे नारायणि नमो ऽस्तु ते ॥

śaṅkhacakragadāśārṅgagṛhītaparamāyudhe | prasīda vaiṣṇavīrūpe nārāyaṇi namo 'stu te ||

English

O you who have taken up the supreme weapons: conch, disc, mace and the Śārṅga bow, be gracious, you who are in the form of Vaiṣṇavī, Nārāyaṇī, homage to you.39

91.16

Sanskrit

गृहीतोग्रमहाचक्रे दंष्ट्रोद्धृतवसुन्धरे । वराहरूपिणि शिवे नारायणि नमो ऽस्तु ते ॥

gṛhītogramahācakre daṃṣṭroddhṛtavasundhare | varāharūpiṇi śive nārāyaṇi namo 'stu te ||

English

O you who have taken up the fierce great disc, who lifted up the earth on your tusk, who have the form of the Boar40, auspicious one41, Nārāyaṇī, homage to you.42

91.17

Sanskrit

नृसिंहरूपेणोग्रेण हन्तुं दैत्यान् कृतोद्यमे । त्रैलोक्यत्राणसहिते नारायणि नमो ऽस्तु ते ॥

nṛsiṃharūpeṇogreṇa hantuṃ daityān kṛtodyame | trailokyatrāṇasahite nārāyaṇi namo 'stu te ||

English

O you who have undertaken the effort to slay the Daityas in the fierce form of Nṛsiṃha, you who bring protection to the three worlds43, Nārāyaṇī, homage to you.44

91.18

Sanskrit

किरीटिनि महावज्रे सहस्रनयनोज्ज्वले । वृत्रप्राणहरे चैन्द्रि नारायणि नमो ऽस्तु ते ॥

kirīṭini mahāvajre sahasranayanojjvale | vṛtraprāṇahare caindri nārāyaṇi namo 'stu te ||

English

O you who wear the diadem, who hold the great thunderbolt, who shine with a thousand eyes45, who take away the life-breaths of Vṛtra46, O Aindrī, Nārāyaṇī, homage to you.47

91.19

Sanskrit

शिवदूतीस्वरूपेण हतदैत्यमहाबले । घोररूपे महारावे नारायणि नमो ऽस्तु ते ॥

śivadūtīsvarūpeṇa hatadaityamahābale | ghorarūpe mahārāve nārāyaṇi namo 'stu te ||

English

O you who, in your own form as Śivadūtī48, have destroyed the great host49 of the Daityas, whose form is terrible, whose roar is mighty, Nārāyaṇī, homage to you.50

91.20

Sanskrit

दंष्ट्राकरालवदने शिरोमालाविभूषणे । चामुण्डे मुण्डमथने नारायणि नमो ऽस्तु ते ॥

daṃṣṭrākarālavadane śiromālāvibhūṣaṇe | cāmuṇḍe muṇḍamathane nārāyaṇi namo 'stu te ||

English

O you whose face is terrible with tusks, who are adorned with a garland of heads51, Cāmuṇḍā, crusher of Muṇḍa52, Nārāyaṇī, homage to you.

91.21

Sanskrit

लक्ष्मि लज्जे महाविद्ये श्रद्धे पुष्टे स्वधे ध्रुवे । महारात्रे महामाये नारायणि नमो ऽस्तु ते ॥

lakṣmi lajje mahāvidye śraddhe puṣṭe svadhe dhruve | mahārātre mahāmāye nārāyaṇi namo 'stu te ||

English

O Lakṣmī, Lajjā, Mahāvidyā53, Śraddhā, Puṣṭi, svadhā, Dhruvā54, Mahārātri, Mahāmāyā, Nārāyaṇī, homage to you.55

91.22

Sanskrit

मेधे सरस्वति वरे भूति बाभ्रवि तामसि । नियते त्वं प्रसीदेशे नारायणि नमो ऽस्तु ते ॥

medhe sarasvati vare bhūti bābhravi tāmasi | niyate tvaṃ prasīdeśe nārāyaṇi namo 'stu te ||

English

O Medhā, Sarasvatī, Varā56, Bhūti, Bābhravī57, Tāmasī58, Niyatā, be gracious, you, O sovereign lady; Nārāyaṇī, homage to you.59

91.23

Sanskrit

सर्वतः पाणिपादान्ते सर्वतो ऽक्षिशिरोमुखे । सर्वतः श्रवणघ्राणे नारायणि नमो ऽस्तु ते ॥

sarvataḥ pāṇipādānte sarvato 'kṣiśiromukhe | sarvataḥ śravaṇaghrāṇe nārāyaṇi namo 'stu te ||

English

You who have hands and feet reaching everywhere60, who have eyes, heads and faces everywhere, who have ears and noses everywhere, Nārāyaṇī, homage to you.61

91.24

Sanskrit

सर्वस्वरूपे सर्वेशे सर्वशक्तिसमन्विते । भयेभ्यस्त्राहि नो देवि दुर्गे देवि नमो ऽस्तु ते ॥

sarvasvarūpe sarveśe sarvaśaktisamanvite | bhayebhyastrāhi no devi durge devi namo 'stu te ||

English

O you whose form is all things, sovereign lady of all62, endowed with every power, protect us from fears, O Devī63; O Durgā, O Devī, homage to you.

91.25

Sanskrit

एतत्ते वदनं सौम्यं लोचनत्रयभूषितम् । पातु नः सर्वभीतिभ्यः कात्यायनि नमो ऽस्तु ते ॥

etatte vadanaṃ saumyaṃ locanatrayabhūṣitam | pātu naḥ sarvabhītibhyaḥ kātyāyani namo 'stu te ||

English

May this gentle face of yours, adorned with three eyes64, protect us from all fears. Kātyāyanī, homage to you.

91.26

Sanskrit

ज्वालाकरालमत्युग्रमशेषासुरशूदनम् । त्रिशूलं पातु नो भीतेर्भद्रकालि नमो ऽस्तुते ॥

jvālākarālamatyugramaśeṣāsuraśūdanam | triśūlaṃ pātu no bhīterbhadrakāli namo 'stute ||

English

May your trident, terrible with flames, exceedingly fierce, the destroyer of all the asuras65 without exception, protect us from fear. Bhadrakālī, homage to you.66

91.27

Sanskrit

हिनस्ति दैत्यतेजांसि स्वनेनापूर्य या जगत् । सा घण्टा पातु नो देवि पापेभ्यो नः सुतानिव ॥

hinasti daityatejāṃsi svanenāpūrya yā jagat | sā ghaṇṭā pātu no devi pāpebhyo naḥ sutāniva ||

English

May that bell, O Devī, which destroys the energies67 of the Daityas by filling the world with its sound, protect us from sins, as [one protects] sons68.69

91.28

Sanskrit

असुरासृग्वसापङ्कचर्चितस्ते करोज्ज्वलः । शुभाय खड्गो भवतु चण्डिके त्वां नता वयम् ॥

asurāsṛgvasāpaṅkacarcitaste karojjvalaḥ | śubhāya khaḍgo bhavatu caṇḍike tvāṃ natā vayam ||

English

May your sword, bright in your hand and smeared with the mud of the asuras' blood and fat70, be for our welfare. O Caṇḍikā, we bow to you.71

91.29

Sanskrit

रोगानशेषानपहंसि तुष्टा रुष्टा तु कामान् सकलानभीष्टान् । त्वामाश्रितानां न विपन्नराणां त्वामाश्रिता ह्याश्रयतां प्रयान्ति ॥

rogānaśeṣānapahaṃsi tuṣṭā ruṣṭā tu kāmān sakalānabhīṣṭān | tvāmāśritānāṃ na vipannarāṇāṃ tvāmāśritā hyāśrayatāṃ prayānti ||

English

Pleased, you destroy all diseases without exception; angry, you destroy all desires that are wished for. For people who have resorted to you there is [no] misfortune72; those who have resorted to you become a refuge [for others].73†

91.30

Sanskrit

एतत्कृतं यत्कदनं त्वयाद्य धर्मद्विषां देवि महासुराणाम् । रूपैरनेकैर्बहुधाऽत्ममूर्ति कृत्वाम्बिके तत्प्रकरोति कान्या ॥

etatkṛtaṃ yatkadanaṃ tvayādya dharmadviṣāṃ devi mahāsurāṇām | rūpairanekairbahudhā'tmamūrti kṛtvāmbike tatprakaroti kānyā ||

English

This slaughter of the great asuras, the haters of dharma, which has been done by you today, O Devī — who else but you, O Ambikā74, could do that, having made your own form manifold in many shapes?75

91.31

Sanskrit

विद्यासु शास्त्रेषु विवेकदीपेष्व् आद्येषु वाक्येषु च का त्वदन्या । ममत्वगर्ते ऽतिमहान्धकारे विभ्रामयत्येतदतीव विश्वम् ॥

vidyāsu śāstreṣu vivekadīpeṣv ādyeṣu vākyeṣu ca kā tvadanyā | mamatvagarte 'timahāndhakāre vibhrāmayatyetadatīva viśvam ||

English

In the branches of knowledge, in the śāstras that are lamps of discernment, and in the foremost sayings76, who is other than you? [You] make this universe whirl about77 exceedingly in the pit of mamatva, in the exceedingly great darkness.78

91.32

Sanskrit

रक्षांसि यत्रोग्रविषाश्च नागा यत्रारयो दस्युबलानि यत्र । दावानलो यत्र तथाब्धिमध्ये तत्र स्थिता त्वं परिपासि विश्वम् ॥

rakṣāṃsi yatrograviṣāśca nāgā yatrārayo dasyubalāni yatra | dāvānalo yatra tathābdhimadhye tatra sthitā tvaṃ paripāsi viśvam ||

English

Wherever there are rākṣasas79, and snakes80 of fierce venom; wherever there are enemies, and armies of bandits; wherever there is a forest fire, and likewise in the midst of the ocean — there you stand and protect the universe.81

91.33

Sanskrit

विश्वेश्वरि त्वं परिपासि विश्वं विश्वात्मिका धारयसीति विश्वम् । विश्वेशवन्द्या भवती भवन्ति विश्वाश्रया ये त्वयि भक्तिनम्राः ॥

viśveśvari tvaṃ paripāsi viśvaṃ viśvātmikā dhārayasīti viśvam | viśveśavandyā bhavatī bhavanti viśvāśrayā ye tvayi bhaktinamrāḥ ||

English

O Viśveśvarī, you protect the universe; being the Self of the universe82, you sustain the universe. You are worthy of homage from the lords of the universe83. Those who bow to you in devotion become a refuge for the universe84.85†

91.34

Sanskrit

देवि प्रसीद परिपालय नो ऽपरिभीतेर् नित्यं यथासुरवधादधुनैव सद्यः । पापानि सर्वजगतां प्रशमं नयाशु उत्पातपाकजनितांश्च महोपसर्गान् ॥

devi prasīda paripālaya no 'paribhīter nityaṃ yathāsuravadhādadhunaiva sadyaḥ | pāpāni sarvajagatāṃ praśamaṃ nayāśu utpātapākajanitāṃśca mahopasargān ||

English

O Devī, be gracious. Protect us always from fear, as [you have done] just now, at once, through the slaying of the asura. Quickly bring to quietude the evils86 of all the worlds, and the great calamities87 born of the ripening of portents88.89

91.35

Sanskrit

प्रणतानां प्रसीद त्वं देवि विश्वार्तिहारिणी । त्रैलोक्यवासिनामीड्ये लोकानां वरदा भव ॥

praṇatānāṃ prasīda tvaṃ devi viśvārtihāriṇī | trailokyavāsināmīḍye lokānāṃ varadā bhava ||

English

Be gracious to us who bow before you, O Devī, you who take away the afflictions of the whole universe. O you who are worthy of praise by the dwellers of the three worlds90, be a giver of boons to the worlds.91

Sanskrit

श्रीदेव्युवाच

śrīdevyuvāca

English

91.36The Blessed Goddess said: I am a giver of boons92, O host of suras93. Choose whatever boon you wish in your minds; I shall grant it, that which is beneficial to the worlds.

91.36

Sanskrit

वरदाहं सुरगण वरं यन्मनसेच्छथ । तं वृणुध्वं प्रयच्छामि जगतामुपकारकम् ॥

varadāhaṃ suragaṇa varaṃ yanmanasecchatha | taṃ vṛṇudhvaṃ prayacchāmi jagatāmupakārakam ||

English

Its English is given with 91.36, above.

Sanskrit

देवा ऊचुः

devā ūcuḥ

English

91.37The gods said: O Sovereign Lady of all94, you must act in just this way: the quieting of every affliction of the three worlds, and the destruction of our enemies.95

91.37

Sanskrit

सर्वबाधाप्रशमनं त्रैलोक्यस्याखिलेश्वरि । एवमेव त्वया कार्यमस्मद्वैरिविनाशनम् ॥

sarvabādhāpraśamanaṃ trailokyasyākhileśvari | evameva tvayā kāryamasmadvairivināśanam ||

English

Its English is given with 91.37, above.

Sanskrit

श्रीदेव्युवाच

śrīdevyuvāca

English
