91.38The Blessed Goddess said: When the Vaivasvata manvantara96 has arrived, in the twenty-eighth yuga97, two other98 great asuras, Śumbha and Niśumbha, will arise.99

91.38

Sanskrit

वैवस्वते ऽन्तरे प्राप्ते अष्टाविंशतिमे युगे । शुम्भो निशुम्भश्चैवान्यावुत्पत्स्येते महासुरौ ॥

vaivasvate 'ntare prāpte aṣṭāviṃśatime yuge | śumbho niśumbhaścaivānyāvutpatsyete mahāsurau ||

English

Its English is given with 91.38, above.

91.39

Sanskrit

नन्दगोपगृहे जाता यशोदागर्भसम्भवा । ततस्तौ नाशयिष्यामि विन्ध्याचलनिवासिनी ॥

nandagopagṛhe jātā yaśodāgarbhasambhavā | tatastau nāśayiṣyāmi vindhyācalanivāsinī ||

English

Born in the house of the cowherd Nanda100, arisen from the womb of Yaśodā101, I shall then destroy those two, dwelling on the Vindhya mountain102.103

91.40

Sanskrit

पुनरप्यतिरौद्रेण रूपेण पृथिवीतले । अवतीर्य हनिष्यामि वैप्रचित्तांस्तु दानवान् ॥

punarapyatiraudreṇa rūpeṇa pṛthivītale | avatīrya haniṣyāmi vaipracittāṃstu dānavān ||

English

Again, having descended to the surface of the earth in an exceedingly fierce form, I shall slay the dānavas who are descendants of Vipracitti104.105

91.41

Sanskrit

भक्षयन्त्याश्च तानुग्रान् वैप्रचित्तान् सुदानवान् । रक्ता दन्ता भविष्यन्ति दाडिमीकुसुमोपमाः ॥

bhakṣayantyāśca tānugrān vaipracittān sudānavān | raktā dantā bhaviṣyanti dāḍimīkusumopamāḥ ||

English

As I devour those fierce Vaipracitta106 dānavas, those mighty ones107, my teeth will become red, like pomegranate blossoms.108

91.42

Sanskrit

ततो मां देवताः स्वर्गे मर्त्यलोके च मानवाः । स्तुवन्तो व्याहरिष्यन्ति सततं रक्तदन्तिकाम् ॥

tato māṃ devatāḥ svarge martyaloke ca mānavāḥ | stuvanto vyāhariṣyanti satataṃ raktadantikām ||

English

Then the gods in heaven, and men in the world of mortals, praising me, will constantly call me Raktadantikā109, "the Red-Toothed."

91.43

Sanskrit

भुयश्च शतवार्षिक्यामनावृष्ट्यामनम्भसि । मुनिभिः संस्तुता भूमौ संभविष्याम्ययोनिजा ॥

bhuyaśca śatavārṣikyāmanāvṛṣṭyāmanambhasi | munibhiḥ saṃstutā bhūmau saṃbhaviṣyāmyayonijā ||

English

And again, when there is a drought of a hundred years110, with no rain and no water, I shall come into being on the earth, praised by the sages, not born from a womb111.112

91.44

Sanskrit

ततः शतेन नेत्राणां निरीक्षिष्यामि यन्मुनीन् । कीर्तयिष्यन्ति मनुजाः शताक्षीमिति मां ततः ॥

tataḥ śatena netrāṇāṃ nirīkṣiṣyāmi yanmunīn | kīrtayiṣyanti manujāḥ śatākṣīmiti māṃ tataḥ ||

English

Then, because I shall look upon the sages with a hundred eyes113, human beings will thereafter proclaim me as Śatākṣī114, "the Hundred-Eyed."115

91.45

Sanskrit

ततो ऽहमखिलं लोकमात्मदेहसमुद्भवैः । भरिष्यामि सुराः शाकैरावृष्टेः प्राणधारकैः ॥

tato 'hamakhilaṃ lokamātmadehasamudbhavaiḥ | bhariṣyāmi surāḥ śākairāvṛṣṭeḥ prāṇadhārakaiḥ ||

English

Then, O gods, I shall sustain the whole world with vegetables116 that spring from my own body and keep the breath of life going until the rains come.117

91.46

Sanskrit

शाकम्भरीति विख्यातिं तदा यास्याम्यहं भुवि । तत्रैव च वधिष्यामि दुर्गमाख्यं महासुरम् । दुर्गा देवीति विख्यातं तन्मे नाम भविष्यति ॥

śākambharīti vikhyātiṃ tadā yāsyāmyahaṃ bhuvi | tatraiva ca vadhiṣyāmi durgamākhyaṃ mahāsuram | durgā devīti vikhyātaṃ tanme nāma bhaviṣyati ||

English

Then I shall become renowned on earth as Śākambharī118. And in that very place I shall slay the great asura named Durgama119. Thus that name of mine will be known: Durgā Devī120.121

91.47

Sanskrit

पुनश्चाहं यदा भीमं रूप कृत्वा हिमाचले । रक्षांसि भक्षयिष्यामि पुनीनां त्राणकारणात् ॥

punaścāhaṃ yadā bhīmaṃ rūpa kṛtvā himācale | rakṣāṃsi bhakṣayiṣyāmi punīnāṃ trāṇakāraṇāt ||

English

91.47–48And when I again assume a terrible form on the Himācala and devour the rākṣasas122 in order to protect the sages, then all the sages, their bodies bowed low, will praise me, and that name of mine will be known: Bhīmā Devī123.124

91.48

Sanskrit

तदा मां मुनयः सर्वे स्तोष्यन्त्यानम्रमूर्तयः । भीमा देवीति विख्यातं तन्मे नाम भविष्यति ॥

tadā māṃ munayaḥ sarve stoṣyantyānamramūrtayaḥ | bhīmā devīti vikhyātaṃ tanme nāma bhaviṣyati ||

English

Its English is given with 91.47–48, above.

91.49

Sanskrit

यदारुणाख्यस्त्रैलोक्ये महाबाधां करिष्यति । तदाहं भ्रामरं रूपं कृत्वासंख्येयषट्पदम् ॥

yadāruṇākhyastrailokye mahābādhāṃ kariṣyati | tadāhaṃ bhrāmaraṃ rūpaṃ kṛtvāsaṃkhyeyaṣaṭpadam ||

English

91.49–50When the [asura] named Aruṇa125 creates great affliction in the three worlds, then I shall assume a bee-form126, surrounded by countless bees, and for the welfare of the three worlds I shall slay that great asura. And then people everywhere will praise me as Bhrāmarī127.128

91.50

Sanskrit

त्रैलोक्यस्य हितार्थाय वधिष्यामि महासुरम् । भ्रामरीति च मां चोकास्तदा स्तोष्यन्ति सर्वतः ॥

trailokyasya hitārthāya vadhiṣyāmi mahāsuram | bhrāmarīti ca māṃ cokāstadā stoṣyanti sarvataḥ ||

English

Its English is given with 91.49–50, above.

91.51

Sanskrit

इत्थं यदा यदा बाधा दानवोत्था भविष्यति । तदा तदावतीर्याहं करिष्याम्यरिसंक्षयम् ॥

itthaṃ yadā yadā bādhā dānavotthā bhaviṣyati | tadā tadāvatīryāhaṃ kariṣyāmyarisaṃkṣayam ||

English

Thus, whenever affliction arising from the dānavas comes about, then, each time, having descended, I shall bring about the destruction of enemies.129

Sanskrit

इति श्रीमार्कण्डेयमहापुराणे सावर्णिके मन्वन्तरे देवीमाहत्म्ये देवैः कृता नारायणो स्तुतिर्नामैकनवतितमो ऽध्यायः

iti śrīmārkaṇḍeyamahāpurāṇe sāvarṇike manvantare devīmāhatmye devaiḥ kṛtā nārāyaṇo stutirnāmaikanavatitamo 'dhyāyaḥ

English

Thus ends the ninety-first chapter130, named "The Praise of Nārāyaṇī131 Offered by the Devas," in the Devīmāhātmya132, in the Sāvarṇika manvantara, in the revered Mārkaṇḍeya Mahāpurāṇa.133

  1. The reading: The verse is only a chapter heading, with the number given as ९१ (91). The ordinal ekanavatitama is 'ninety-first'. The visarga in -o 'dhyāyaḥ is sandhi for the nominative singular. No predicate is expressed, so the English supplies none and no bracketed words are needed. ↩
  2. Kātyāyanī: A name of the Goddess (Durgā); already in the glossary, here the object of the devas' praise. ↩
  3. their faces were open like blooming lotuses, and the quarters of the sky were brightened: The compound vikāśivaktrābjavikāśitāśāḥ can be read as 'those whose hopes (āśā) were made to bloom by their lotus-faces blooming', or as 'by whom the quarters (āśā) were made bright'. Both senses of āśā are possible. ↩
  4. The reading: The last compound, vikāśivaktrābjavikāśitāśāḥ, has āśā as either 'quarters' or 'hopes/desires'. Chosen: 'quarters', since 'hopes' would repeat iṣṭalābhāt; 'whose faces ... brightened the quarters' is the sense. The compound may also be rendered 'with hopes made to bloom'. Also, hate with devyā is a locative absolute, 'when ... had been slain by the Goddess'. ↩
  5. Sovereign Lady of the universe: Viśveśvarī, 'sovereign lady of the universe'; a title of the Goddess. ↩
  6. The reading: Reading 'jagato 'khilasya' as genitive with mātaḥ ('Mother of the whole world'), not with prasīda. 'Viśvam' is rendered 'universe' to keep it distinct from 'jagat', 'world'. ↩
  7. irresistible vigor: alaṅghyavīrye is a vocative addressed to the Goddess: 'you whose vigor cannot be transgressed or overcome'. ↩
  8. The reading: The verse is syntactically loose. 'yataḥ' (since) is taken causally with the first line: she is the support because she abides as earth. 'Kṛtsnam' is taken as agreeing with 'etad' (all this). 'Alaṅghyavīrye' is taken as a vocative to the Goddess. ↩
  9. Vaiṣṇavī: The Goddess as the Śakti of Viṣṇu, his power in feminine form. ↩
  10. The reading: The printed 'tevaṃ vai' is evidently a misprint; I read 'tvaṃ vai' (the metre and sense require it), 'you indeed'. 'saṃmohitam' is a passive participle with no agent stated; the sense is that all this is bewildered by the Goddess's māyā, and I have left the agent unstated. 'prasannā' is taken as a condition ('when you are gracious'), as the construction implies. ↩
  11. the Mother: ambayā is instrumental, in apposition to tvayā ekayā ('by you alone'); it is not a vocative. ↩
  12. one that goes beyond what is to be praised: stavyaparā is feminine and can agree with stutiḥ ('praise') or be read as an epithet of the Goddess ('you who are beyond the praiseworthy'). Here it is taken with stutiḥ. The sense is a praise that would surpass its object. ↩
  13. an utterance of another: paroktiḥ can mean 'another's utterance' (para as 'other') or 'supreme, excellent utterance' (para as 'highest'). Verse 91.6 has paramoktayaḥ, 'supreme utterances', which supports the second sense. The translation keeps the first sense and the sentence open. ↩
  14. The reading: stavyaparā paroktiḥ is ambiguous. (a) stavyaparā as an epithet of the Goddess ('you who are beyond the praiseworthy'), with paroktiḥ as 'another's utterance'. (b) stavyaparā agreeing with stutiḥ ('a praise that surpasses what is to be praised'), with paroktiḥ in apposition. (c) paroktiḥ as 'supreme utterance'. I chose (b) with 'another's utterance' for paroktiḥ, and kept it as a bare question without 'since' or 'any', following the reviewer. I did not accept the reviewer's 'throughout the worlds' for jagatsu as a rendering of sakalā. The text reads sakalā (singular) beside plural striyaḥ samastāḥ. I take it as sakalāḥ qualifying striyaḥ and render it 'every one', with jagatsu as 'throughout the worlds'. I do not use 'in every aspect', which the Sanskrit does not support. bhedāḥ is carried over to the women in brackets, as the reviewer asks. ↩
  15. who are all beings: Sarvabhūtā is a feminine compound that can mean 'who are all beings' or 'who are in all beings'. The first reading is chosen: the Goddess is identified with every creature. ↩
  16. supreme utterances: Paramoktayaḥ may be parama-uktayaḥ, 'supreme utterances', or para-uktayaḥ, 'utterances of others', which would echo parokti in 91.5. ↩
  17. The reading: (1) Sarvabhūtā: 'who are all beings' (chosen) or 'who dwell in all beings'. (2) Paramoktayaḥ: parama-uktayaḥ, 'supreme utterances' (chosen, as the ordinary sense of the compound), or para-uktayaḥ, 'utterances of others', as in parokti in 91.5. (3) Yadā is taken as 'when', not 'since'. (4) Svargamukti is the reading of this e-text. Other versions of this hymn have bhuktimukti, 'enjoyment and release', and the sense would be little changed. ↩
  18. final release: Apavarga, 'release', is liberation from rebirth, the same goal as mukti in the preceding verses. ↩
  19. Nārāyaṇī: The feminine counterpart of Nārāyaṇa (Viṣṇu); here a name of the Goddess as the Śakti of Viṣṇu. ↩
  20. giver of change: pariṇāma means transformation, ripening or development, and also the final change of decay. Here it covers the changes that passing time brings to all things. ↩
  21. the kalā, the kāṣṭhā and the rest: Kalā and kāṣṭhā are small units of time. A kāṣṭhā is commonly reckoned as eighteen winks of the eye, and a kalā as thirty kāṣṭhās. The "and the rest" (ādi) takes in the other units of time. ↩
  22. capable at the cessation of the universe: śakte is taken as the vocative of the adjective śaktā, 'able, capable'. The Goddess is addressed as having the power to act when the universe ends. Uparati is ending or dissolution. The locative viśvasya uparatau gives the occasion of her power. ↩
  23. The reading: Śakte can be the vocative of śakti ('power') or of the adjective śaktā ('able'). I now take śaktā, as the reviewer asks. Otherwise śakte has no clear function beside the locative viśvasyoparatau, and the sense 'able at the ending of the universe' suits the preceding address to the Goddess as the agent of change. Śaktibhūte in 91.10 is a different compound and does not decide the matter. I render uparati as 'cessation'; 'dissolution' is also possible. In pariṇāmapradāyinī the e-text's -yinī looks like a nominative, while a vocative -yini would be expected, parallel to śakte and nārāyaṇi. I keep the vocative 'O giver', since the whole verse is an address to the Goddess and the sense is unchanged. ↩
  24. Śivā: Śive is the feminine vocative, 'the auspicious one'; here a name of the Goddess, not the god Śiva. ↩
  25. three-eyed one: Tryambakā, feminine of Tryambaka, the epithet of Śiva ('three-eyed', or 'having three mothers'); here applied to the Goddess as his consort. ↩
  26. The reading: Tryambake: 'three-eyed' (tri + akṣi/ambaka) or 'having three mothers'; I took the common sense 'three-eyed'. 'Sarvamaṅgalamāṅgalye' is a compound read as 'auspiciousness of all that is auspicious'; it could also be 'auspicious one among all auspicious things'. ↩
  27. support of the guṇas: guṇāśraye is a vocative compound, guṇa-āśraya. I take it as a genitive tatpuruṣa, 'she who is the support of the guṇas', in which they abide. The next epithet, guṇamaye, adds that she is also made of them. ↩
  28. The reading: guṇāśraye: āśraya can mean 'support, substratum' or 'refuge, shelter'. The compound can also be read as a bahuvrīhi, 'she whose resting place is the guṇas'. I chose 'support of the guṇas', the genitive tatpuruṣa. This avoids 'refuge', which the glossary reserves for śaraṇa, a suppliant's shelter, and it agrees with the note. śaktibhūte: I read 'who are the Śakti of', taking bhūta as 'being, consisting of', and I keep the glossary's capitalised, italicised Śakti. ↩
  29. The reading: The compound śaraṇāgatadīnārtaparitrāṇaparāyaṇe can be read as a dvandva of three groups (those who have come for refuge, the wretched, the afflicted) or with śaraṇāgata qualifying dīnārta (the wretched and afflicted who have come for refuge). I chose the three-fold reading, which is the more natural for a dvandva. In sarvasyārtihare, sarvasya is taken as the genitive of the object of the removal (suffering of all), not as a vocative. ↩
  30. who pour out water [sprinkled] with kuśa grass: The e-text divides the words as kauśāmbhaḥ kṣarike. The sense is that of the compound kauśāmbhaḥkṣarike, 'who pour out water with kuśa'. The Goddess as Brahmāṇī carries water for sprinkling. ↩
  31. kuśa: A sacred grass used in Vedic and domestic rites, here for sprinkling water in purification or consecration. ↩
  32. The reading: The text reads kauśāmbhaḥ kṣarike as two words, which is odd as a vocative construction. I take it as the compound kauśāmbhaḥkṣarike (kauśa-ambhas, 'water with kuśa', plus kṣarikā, 'one who pours or sprinkles'). The bracketed 'sprinkled' is implied by kauśa ('of kuśa') qualifying the water. ↩
  33. the moon and the serpent: The crescent moon in the matted hair and the serpent worn on the body are attributes of Śiva; the Goddess bears them as his Śakti. ↩
  34. in your own form as Māheśvarī: The instrumental svarūpeṇa expresses the capacity or manner in which she is addressed ('by means of, as, the form'); it qualifies the whole address. ↩
  35. The reading: The compound triśūlacandrāhidhare could be read as 'bearing the trident, the moon and the serpent', which I chose, or as 'bearing a trident marked with a moon and a serpent'. The first is the natural reading, since these are Śiva's emblems. The reviewer rightly asks that svarūpa keep its 'sva-', so I render 'in your own form'. The instrumental svarūpeṇa is a regular instrumental of manner or capacity, not a displaced locative, and the earlier remark to the contrary is withdrawn. The note on the serpent no longer fixes its place at the neck. ↩
  36. great spear: mahāśakti: the śakti, the spear that is the weapon of Kumāra (Skanda), here carried by his Śakti Kaumārī. The compound could also be read as 'great power'. ↩
  37. sinless one: The e-text reads 'maghe, which is probably a misprint for 'naghe, i.e. anaghe, vocative of anaghā, 'sinless, faultless'. The Devīmāhātmya has mahāśaktidhare 'naghe. ↩
  38. The reading: 'maghe: the e-text reads 'maghe, which would be amaghe, but no normal adjective amagha meaning 'faultless' exists. I read it as a corruption of 'naghe = anaghe, 'sinless', following the Devīmāhātmya reading, and have dropped the earlier amoghe ('unfailing') alternative as unnecessary conjecture. mahāśaktidhare: 'bearer of the great power' or 'bearer of the great spear'. I chose 'spear' because the verse describes Kaumārī, whose weapon is Kumāra's śakti. saṃsthāne: from saṃsthāna, 'form, configuration, standing in'. I take kaumārīrūpasaṃsthāne as 'who abide in the form of Kaumārī', which is a closer rendering than 'take your place'. ↩
  39. The reading: The compound śaṅkhacakragadāśārṅgagṛhītaparamāyudhe could be read as 'by whom the supreme weapons conch, disc, mace and Śārṅga are grasped', as I have it, or as 'having grasped the conch, disc, mace and Śārṅga as supreme weapons'. The sense is the same. ↩
  40. the form of the Boar: Varāha, the boar incarnation of Viṣṇu, who raised the earth from the cosmic waters on his tusk. The Goddess is addressed here as his Śakti, Vārāhī. ↩
  41. auspicious one: Śivā, vocative of the feminine of śiva, 'auspicious'. It is here an epithet of the Goddess, not the god Śiva. ↩
  42. The reading: Śive (vocative of śivā) could be read as a plain adjective 'auspicious' qualifying the Goddess, or as the epithet Śivā. I rendered it 'auspicious one', which keeps both senses. The compound gṛhītogramahācakre is read as 'one by whom the fierce great disc is taken up' (bahuvrīhi); ugra and mahā both qualify cakra. ↩
  43. protection to the three worlds: The compound trailokyatrāṇasahite can be read as 'accompanied by the protection of the three worlds' or 'endowed with it'. It is rendered here as 'you who bring protection to the three worlds', in the sense of 'bound up with' that protection. ↩
  44. The reading: trailokyatrāṇasahite: 'accompanied by / joined with the protection of the three worlds'. I took it as 'you who bring protection to the three worlds', since sahita here means 'endowed with' the work of protecting. Daityān is the object of hantum; kṛtodyame is a vocative bahuvrīhi, 'by whom effort has been made'. ↩
  45. a thousand eyes: Sahasranayana, 'thousand-eyed', is an epithet of Indra; the Goddess here shares his form as Aindrī. ↩
  46. Vṛtra: The asura (dragon) whom Indra killed with the thunderbolt, releasing the waters. ↩
  47. The reading: caindri is cā + aindri: 'and, O Aindrī'. I take ca as a mere connective and render the vocative Aindrī. The verse is a string of vocatives; mahāvajre is rendered 'who hold the great thunderbolt' (literally 'having the great vajra'). Ujjvale is rendered as 'who shine with a thousand eyes'. ↩
  48. Śivadūtī: The Goddess's Śakti who made Śiva her messenger, sent to the asura chief Śumbha; the name means 'she who has Śiva as messenger'. ↩
  49. great host: The compound hatadaityamahābale can also be read as 'one by whom the great strength of the Daityas was destroyed'; 'host' for bala follows the battle context. ↩
  50. The reading: hatadaityamahābale: bala may mean 'strength' or 'army'. I chose 'host' because the context is battle; the sense 'great strength of the Daityas' is also possible. ↩
  51. garland of heads: śiromālā: the garland of severed heads, a regular attribute of the fierce form of the Goddess. ↩
  52. crusher of Muṇḍa: Refers to the slaying of the asura Muṇḍa, together with Caṇḍa, by the Goddess in her form as Cāmuṇḍā. ↩
  53. Mahāvidyā: 'Great Knowledge', a name of the Goddess; the vocative of mahāvidyā, here taken as a name, not as an adjective 'of great knowledge'. ↩
  54. Dhruvā: 'the Constant, Firm one'; the Sanskrit vocative dhruve is feminine and addresses the Goddess, not the pole-star Dhruva. ↩
  55. The reading: The glossary has Dhruva as the son of Uttānapāda, but here dhruve is feminine vocative and addresses the Goddess as 'the Constant (Firm) one'; I wrote it Dhruvā to avoid confusion with the pole-star. Mahāvidyā could also be read as the adjective 'possessed of great knowledge' (mahāvidye), but as the verse is a string of divine names, I take it as a name. Likewise svadhe is rendered svadhā as in the glossary, referring to the goddess personifying the offering. ↩
  56. Varā: Vocative of the feminine varā. It can mean 'excellent, best' or 'boon-granting'; both suit the Goddess, so the name is kept in IAST. ↩
  57. Bābhravī: Feminine derivative of babhru, 'tawny, brown'; it may also mean 'she who belongs to Babhru'. The sense is uncertain. ↩
  58. Tāmasī: 'She who is of tamas', the dark quality; the Goddess in her dark aspect. ↩
  59. The reading: vare: the vocative of feminine varā, 'excellent' or 'boon-granting'. The reviewer prefers 'boon-giver' and asks for the note to say 'of varā'; I made that correction. The two senses are equally possible, so I keep the name in IAST as Varā, as the glossary does with other names of the Goddess, instead of fixing one rendering. bābhravi: uncertain, kept in IAST. niyate: probably the vocative of niyatā, 'controlled, restrained, ordained one'. It might be a name such as 'Restraint' or 'Fate', but this is not certain, so I kept it in IAST as Niyatā with no note. tāmasi: kept as Tāmasī, per the glossary, and a short note added. prasīdeśe: prasīda + īśe, 'be gracious, O sovereign lady'. I removed 'to us' because the Sanskrit has no object. tvam is now rendered as 'you', set off by commas so that 'O sovereign lady' stays with prasīda and is not read as another name in the list. The draft's list punctuation had made it look like one. ↩
  60. hands and feet reaching everywhere: The verse echoes the description of the all-pervading Brahman in Bhagavadgītā 13.13 (sarvataḥ pāṇipādaṃ tat), here applied to the Goddess. ↩
  61. The reading: pāṇipādānte is difficult. Taken literally, 'at the end of hands and feet', it could mean she whose hands and feet extend to the ends of everything. I read it as 'whose hands and feet reach everywhere', in line with the Gītā verse, and so render sarvataḥ as qualifying it. The other compounds (akṣiśiromukhe, śravaṇaghrāṇe) are read as bahuvrīhi vocatives, 'having eyes, heads and faces' and 'having ears and noses'. ↩
  62. sovereign lady of all: Sarveśe, feminine vocative of sarveśā: 'mistress of all'. ↩
  63. O Devī: Devī, 'goddess', is used here as a vocative address to the Goddess; it occurs twice in the verse. ↩
  64. three eyes: The Goddess is pictured with three eyes, like Śiva; the Sanskrit is locanatraya, 'triad of eyes'. ↩
  65. asuras: The asuras are the rivals and enemies of the gods (sura); here they are the demonic foes the Goddess has fought. ↩
  66. The reading: The printed text has namo 'stute, which I read as namo 'stu te ('homage to you'), as in the preceding verses. bhīteḥ is ablative singular ('from fear'), construed with pātu; the sense is the same as bhayebhyaḥ in 91.24. ↩
  67. energies: tejāṃsi, plural of tejas: fiery energy, brilliance, power. The bell's sound strips the Daityas of it. ↩
  68. as [one protects] sons: The Sanskrit has only naḥ sutān iva, 'us, like sons'. The verb and the protecting agent are carried over from pātu, and the English supplies them in brackets. ↩
  69. The reading: I accept the reviewer's findings: 'of yours' is removed, since the Sanskrit has no te; 'splendours' is changed to 'energies'; the added 'a mother' is dropped. The comparison naḥ sutān iva is now rendered 'as [one protects] sons', with only the implied verb and agent in brackets. I have not made the agent 'you', since the verse does not say whose sons they are. The note on pāpebhyaḥ is dropped, since 'sins' is adequate and no parallel with bhaya is needed. The word could also mean 'evils' or 'misfortunes'; I keep 'sins' as the ordinary sense. ↩
  70. mud of the asuras' blood and fat: The compound asurāsṛgvasāpaṅkacarcita is read as 'smeared with the mud (paṅka) made of the asuras' blood (asṛj) and fat (vasā)'. ↩
  71. The reading: The text reads 'asurāsṛgvasāpaṅkacarcitaste', which is 'carcitaḥ te' with the visarga lost through sandhi. The compound could also be read as 'mud of blood and fat of the asura [Mahiṣa]', but the plural asuras is more natural in this hymn, where the asuras are spoken of generally. 'karojjvalaḥ' is taken as 'bright in the hand' (kara + ujjvala), i.e. shining as it is held in your hand; 'bright with its rays' is a less likely reading. ↩
  72. For people who have resorted to you there is [no] misfortune: The text reads vipannarāṇām, which I take as vipat narāṇām by sandhi: 'for men, no misfortune'. The verb 'is' is not expressed. ↩
  73. The reading: vipannarāṇām can be read in two ways: (a) vipad + narāṇām, 'for men [who have resorted to you] there is no calamity'; (b) vipanna-narāṇām, 'men fallen into misfortune'. With na before it, (a) gives the clearer sense, and the following clause, which says that devotees become a refuge, fits it. I chose (a). The words 'no' and 'for others' are added in square brackets because the Sanskrit implies them, though it does not state them. ↩
  74. Ambikā: 'Mother'; a name of the Goddess. ↩
  75. The reading: ātmamūrti (for ātmamūrtim) is the accusative singular object of kṛtvā; the final -m is lost in the printed text, probably for the metre. kānyā is kā anyā, 'who else'. The rhetorical question implies the answer 'no one but you'. ↩
  76. the foremost sayings: ādyeṣu vākyeṣu can mean the 'primary' or 'ancient' sayings, that is, the Vedic texts and other authoritative utterances. ↩
  77. [You] make this universe whirl about: The verb vibhrāmayati is third person singular and has no stated subject. The sense is that the Goddess, addressed in the preceding clause, is the one who bewilders the world, so 'you' is supplied. ↩
  78. The reading: (1) ādyeṣu: 'first, foremost' or 'primeval'; I chose 'foremost'. (2) mamatvagarte 'timahāndhakāre: the two locatives may be separate ('in the pit of mamatva, in the great darkness') or one phrase in which the pit is full of great darkness. I kept them separate, as the Sanskrit does. (3) The subject of vibhrāmayati is unexpressed; I supplied 'you', as the clause follows directly on 'who is other than you'. ↩
  79. rākṣasas: Rākṣasas are a class of night-roaming demonic beings, hostile to sacrifice and to men; they are distinct from the asuras. ↩
  80. snakes: Sanskrit nāga: serpents, here ordinary venomous snakes, though nāgas can also be semi-divine serpent beings. ↩
  81. The reading: The verse has no 'and' after 'rakṣāṃsi'; the compound 'ugraviṣāḥ' is taken as an adjective of 'nāgāḥ' (of fierce venom). 'Dasyubalāni' is taken as 'armies of bandits' (dasyu + bala). 'Sthitā' is taken as a predicate participle with 'tvam': 'you stand, being present'. ↩
  82. the Self of the universe: viśvātmikā: having the universe as her self or essence; the Goddess is the inner being of all that exists. ↩
  83. the lords of the universe: viśveśa: the lords of the universe, such as Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva; the word could also be read as a singular, 'the Lord of the universe'. ↩
  84. become a refuge for the universe: bhavanti is the verb of the relative clause ye ... bhaktinamrāḥ; the main clause is bhavatī viśveśavandyā, where bhavatī means 'you' (feminine, honorific). ↩
  85. The reading: viśveśavandyā (bhavatī): read as 'you are worthy of homage from the lords of the universe' (tatpuruṣa, vandyā with an agent in the compound). Alternatively 'the Lord of the universe' in the singular, since viśveśa can be singular. I chose the plural because the compound is general. Also bhavatī bhavanti: bhavatī as the honorific 'you' with an elided verb 'are'; the line is taken as 'you are worthy of homage ...' and the next clause begins with bhavanti, 'become'. ↩
  86. evils: pāpa means both moral evil (sin) and misfortune. Here the context of calamities favours 'evils' in the general sense. ↩
  87. great calamities: upasarga means a calamity, affliction or disaster, often of a portentous kind. ↩
  88. the ripening of portents: pāka is the 'ripening' or maturing of the portents, when the utpāta omens bring about their consequences. ↩
  89. The reading: The phrase no 'paribhīter (naḥ + a-/paribhīteḥ) is ambiguous. Read as aparibhīteḥ it would mean 'from non-fear', which makes no sense. I take it as naḥ paribhīteḥ, 'us ... from fear', with the avagraha as a misprint or a sandhi oddity, and ablative of paribhīti, 'fear'. The elliptical yathā clause ('as [you have done] just now through the slaying of the asura') has the verb supplied from paripālaya. Alternatively, asuravadhāt could be read with the preceding words ('from fear [arising from] the asura') but that is less natural. ↩
  90. worthy of praise by the dwellers of the three worlds: trailokyavāsinām is genitive plural and is construed with īḍye ("to be praised by"); the text could also be read as dependent on lokānām, but the sense is the same. ↩
  91. The reading: trailokyavāsinām īḍye: the genitive plural can be taken with īḍye (worthy of praise by the dwellers of the three worlds) or as qualifying lokānām (the worlds' dwellers). I chose the first, as the genitive of the agent with a gerundive. lokānām varadā bhava is rendered 'giver of boons to the worlds'. ↩
  92. giver of boons: Sanskrit varadā, feminine, with ahaṃ ('I'): the Goddess speaks of herself as the boon-granting one. ↩
  93. O host of suras: Vocative singular suragaṇa, a collective address to the assembled gods who have praised her. ↩
  94. Sovereign Lady of all: Akhileśvarī, a title of the Goddess; compare Viśveśvarī, 'Sovereign Lady of the universe'. ↩
  95. The reading: The syntax of 91.37 is elliptical: 'sarvabādhāpraśamanam' and 'asmadvairivināśanam' are both neuter nominatives in the sentence 'evam eva tvayā kāryam'. I read them as two things to be done, 'the quieting of all afflictions' and 'the destruction of our enemies'. The first may instead qualify the action as 'the quieting of every affliction of the three worlds [which you have just done]', but the parallel with 'evam eva' ('just so') supports the reading chosen. ↩
  96. Vaivasvata manvantara: The seventh manvantara, the age of Manu Vaivasvata, the present one. A manvantara is the period of rule of one Manu. ↩
  97. yuga: One of the four world-ages in a cycle; the twenty-eighth yuga of the Vaivasvata manvantara is the present Kali age. ↩
  98. other: anyau, 'two other', in the dual: other than the asuras of the earlier story in this text. ↩
  99. The reading: antare is here taken as the neuter of manvantara (vaivasvate 'ntare, 'in the Vaivasvata interval'), the usual shortened form. Śumbha was glossed 'slayer of Puṣkaramālin; identity unclear', but here he is plainly one of the two asura brothers, with Niśumbha. anyau is rendered 'other' as an attributive qualifying the two asuras. ↩
  100. Nanda: The cowherd chief of Gokula, foster-father of Kṛṣṇa; in the tradition the Goddess is born as his daughter, exchanged for Kṛṣṇa. ↩
  101. Yaśodā: Nanda's wife; the Goddess is said to be born as her daughter. ↩
  102. dwelling on the Vindhya mountain: Vindhyavāsinī, 'she who dwells in the Vindhya', is a well-known name and seat of the Goddess. ↩
  103. The reading: nandagopagṛhe: 'Nanda the cowherd' (gopa in apposition) is chosen over 'house of Nanda and the cowherds'. The compound nandagopa is read as a single person. Also, 'vindhyācala' is rendered 'Vindhya mountain'. ↩
  104. descendants of Vipracitti: Vaipracitta is a patronymic from Vipracitti, a chief of the dānavas; the Goddess here speaks of his line, whom the next verses show her devouring. ↩
  105. The reading: The particle 'tu' is taken as merely emphatic and left untranslated. 'Punar api' is rendered 'Again', as a further incarnation after the Vindhya birth of verse 39. ↩
  106. Vaipracitta: Patronymic: descendant of Vipracitti, here the dānavas of his line, as in the preceding verse. ↩
  107. mighty ones: sudānavān is read as an adjective-like compound, 'excellent/mighty dānavas', in apposition to the Vaipracittas; the sense is not ironical. ↩
  108. The reading: sudānavān: su- may mean 'excellent, mighty' (chosen, as an intensive of strength) or be an ironic or merely metrical filler. bhakṣayantyāḥ is a genitive feminine present participle ('of me, while devouring'), construed with dantāḥ ('my teeth'); the 'me' is implied from the Goddess's speech, so I render it as 'As I devour'. ↩
  109. Raktadantikā: "She who has red teeth"; the name comes from the red teeth mentioned in the preceding verse. ↩
  110. a drought of a hundred years: śatavārṣikyām anāvṛṣṭyām is a locative absolute: 'when there is a drought lasting a hundred years'. anambhasi, 'when there is no water', is taken in the same construction. ↩
  111. not born from a womb: ayonijā: the Goddess appears by her own power, not by birth. The next verse (91.44) goes on to the name Śatākṣī, 'the Hundred-Eyed'. ↩
  112. The reading: The verse begins 'bhuyaś ca', a misprint for 'bhūyaś ca' ('and again'); I read it so. 'anambhasi' is taken as a locative absolute parallel to 'anāvṛṣṭyām', 'when there is no water'. 'Saṃstutā' is taken as agreeing with the speaker (the Goddess), 'praised by the sages'. ↩
  113. a hundred eyes: śatena netrāṇām: the instrumental śatena is construed with the genitive plural netrāṇām, 'with a hundred of eyes'. ↩
  114. Śatākṣī: 'Hundred-Eyed' (śata, hundred; akṣa, eye); the name is explained by the hundred eyes of the Goddess in this verse. ↩
  115. The reading: yat in yanmunīn is read as the conjunction 'because, since' (ending the first clause, giving the reason for the name), not as a relative pronoun. Hence 'because I shall look upon the sages'. munīn is accusative plural, the object of nirīkṣiṣyāmi. ↩
  116. vegetables: śāka: edible greens and vegetables. This is the origin of the name Śākambharī, "she who bears vegetables", given in the next verse. ↩
  117. The reading: āvṛṣṭeḥ is an ablative of limit, 'until rain', and I have taken it so. It could be read as 'until the rains fall again'. surāḥ is a vocative, 'O gods', addressing the devas. The sense of prāṇadhārakaiḥ is 'life-sustaining'. ↩
  118. Śākambharī: 'She who bears vegetables', from śāka and bhṛ; the name recalls the vegetables of the preceding verse. ↩
  119. Durgama: The asura whose name means 'hard to pass or reach'. The Goddess's name Durgā is explained as arising from his slaying. ↩
  120. Durgā Devī: Durgā is here the Goddess's name, linked by sound to Durgama, whom she slays. ↩
  121. The reading: tatraiva ('in that very place') has no clear antecedent; it may mean 'there on earth' (bhuvi) or 'on that occasion'. I took it as 'in that very place', following bhuvi. In the last line, vikhyātam is neuter, agreeing with nāma, so 'will be known'; devī is taken as part of the name Durgā Devī. ↩
  122. rākṣasas: The text has rakṣas (neuter plural rakṣāṃsi), a class of night-roaming demons hostile to sages and sacrifice; the usual later form is rākṣasa. ↩
  123. Bhīmā Devī: Bhīmā means 'the Terrible', from bhīma, 'fearful', echoing the 'terrible form' (bhīmaṃ rūpam) of verse 47. ↩
  124. The reading: Verse 91.47 reads punīnāṃ ('of the ... punīs'), which is not a meaningful word. I take it as a misprint for munīnām, 'of the sages', since verse 91.48 has the sages (munayaḥ) praising her. Also, rūpa is printed for rūpaṃ (accusative), which the sense and the adjective bhīmaṃ require. ↩
  125. Aruṇa: An asura who, in the Devīmāhātmya's closing prophecy, troubles the three worlds; the Goddess destroys him with swarms of bees. ↩
  126. bee-form: bhrāmaraṃ rūpam: 'form of bees', from bhramara, 'bee'. The compound asaṃkhyeyaṣaṭpadam ('having countless six-footed ones') shows that the form is made of innumerable bees. ↩
  127. Bhrāmarī: 'She of the bees'; the name of the Goddess in this form. ↩
  128. The reading: cokāḥ (for lokāḥ) in 91.50 is evidently a misprint: 'people' is read for lokāḥ. The first verse's subject 'aruṇākhyaḥ' has no noun; I supply '[asura]' from mahāsuram in 91.50. The verb 'will praise' is stoṣyanti, with māṃ as object. ↩
  129. The reading: I accepted the reviewer's findings on 'and wherever', the bracketed '[our]', and the gerund 'avatīrya' ('having descended'). I dropped the note on dānava because the glossary already lists it as explained. 'Tadā tadā' is rendered 'then, each time'. 'Arisaṃkṣayam' is rendered 'destruction of enemies'. The compound has no possessive, and I add none. ↩
  130. ninety-first chapter: The colophon counts this as chapter 91 (ekanavatitama), although the chapter is headed 86 in this e-text's numbering; the verse numbers before it are 91.46–51. ↩
  131. Nārāyaṇī: The text reads nārāyaṇo, which is metrically and grammatically irregular; it is taken as a corrupt form of nārāyaṇī (the Goddess), the subject of the praise. ↩
  132. Devīmāhātmya: 'The Greatness of the Goddess': the section of the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa devoted to the Goddess's exploits, of which this chapter is a part. The text writes it devīmāhatmya. ↩
  133. The reading: nārāyaṇo is unmetrical and ungrammatical with stutiḥ (feminine). I read it as nārāyaṇī, giving 'the praise of Nārāyaṇī', as the chapter's content (the devas' hymn to the Goddess) requires. The alternative is nārāyaṇa-stuti, 'praise of Nārāyaṇa', which does not suit the chapter. Also devīmāhatmya is a misprint for devīmāhātmya. The chapter number 91 follows the colophon, not the e-text's heading. ↩

Adhyāya 92

Sanskrit

द्विनवतितमो ऽध्यायः- ९२

dvinavatitamo 'dhyāyaḥ- 92

English

The ninety-second chapter.1

92.1

Sanskrit

देव्युवाच एभिः स्तवैश्च मां नित्यं स्तोष्यते यः समाहितः । तस्याहं सकलां बाधां नाशयिष्याम्यसंशयम् ॥

devyuvāca ebhiḥ stavaiśca māṃ nityaṃ stoṣyate yaḥ samāhitaḥ | tasyāhaṃ sakalāṃ bādhāṃ nāśayiṣyāmyasaṃśayam ||

English

The Devī said:2 Whoever, with a collected mind, will constantly praise me with these hymns of praise, for him I shall destroy every affliction, without doubt.

92.2

Sanskrit

मधुकैटभानाशं च महिषासुरघातनम् । कीर्तयिष्यिन्ति ये तद्वद् वधं शुम्भनिशुम्भयोः ॥

madhukaiṭabhānāśaṃ ca mahiṣāsuraghātanam | kīrtayiṣyinti ye tadvad vadhaṃ śumbhaniśumbhayoḥ ||

English

92.2–4Those who will recount the destruction of Madhu and Kaiṭabha, the slaying of Mahiṣa, the asura3, and likewise the slaying of Śumbha and Niśumbha, and who, single-minded, on the eighth, the fourteenth and the ninth [lunar days4], will also praise with devotion this supreme greatness of mine5: for them there will be no evil deed whatever, no calamities arising from evil deeds, no poverty, and no separation from those they love.6

92.3

Sanskrit

अष्टम्यां च चतुर्दश्यां नवम्यां चैकचेतसः । स्तोष्यन्ति चैव ये भक्त्या मम माहात्म्यमुत्तमम् ॥

aṣṭamyāṃ ca caturdaśyāṃ navamyāṃ caikacetasaḥ | stoṣyanti caiva ye bhaktyā mama māhātmyamuttamam ||

English

Its English is given with 92.2–4, above.

92.4

Sanskrit

न तेषां दुष्कृतं किञ्चिद् दुष्कृतोत्था न चापदः । भविष्यति न दारिद्रयं न चैवेष्टवियोजनम् ॥

na teṣāṃ duṣkṛtaṃ kiñcid duṣkṛtotthā na cāpadaḥ | bhaviṣyati na dāridrayaṃ na caiveṣṭaviyojanam ||

English

Its English is given with 92.2–4, above.

92.5

Sanskrit

शत्रुतो न भयं तस्य दस्युतो वा न राजतः । न शस्त्रानलतोयौघात् कदाचित् सम्भविष्यति ॥

śatruto na bhayaṃ tasya dasyuto vā na rājataḥ | na śastrānalatoyaughāt kadācit sambhaviṣyati ||

English

For him there will never be fear from an enemy, or from a bandit, or from a king, nor from weapons, fire, or a flood of water.7

92.6

Sanskrit

तस्मान्ममैतन्माहात्म्यं पठितव्यं सहाहितैः । श्रोतव्यं च सदा भक्त्या परं स्वस्त्ययनं महत् ॥

tasmānmamaitanmāhātmyaṃ paṭhitavyaṃ sahāhitaiḥ | śrotavyaṃ ca sadā bhaktyā paraṃ svastyayanaṃ mahat ||

English

Therefore this greatness of mine should be recited by the attentive8, and should always be listened to with devotion; it is a supreme and great svastyayana9.10†

92.7

Sanskrit

उपसर्गानशेषांस्तु महामारीसमुद्भवान् । तथा त्रिविधमुत्पातं माहात्म्यं शमयेन्मम ॥

upasargānaśeṣāṃstu mahāmārīsamudbhavān | tathā trividhamutpātaṃ māhātmyaṃ śamayenmama ||

English

May this greatness of mine pacify all calamities that arise from epidemic plague11, and likewise the portent of three kinds12.13

92.8

Sanskrit

यत्रैतत् पठ्यते सम्यङ्नित्यमायतने मम । सदा न तद्विमोक्ष्यामि सांनिध्यं तत्र मे स्थितम् ॥

yatraitat paṭhyate samyaṅnityamāyatane mama | sadā na tadvimokṣyāmi sāṃnidhyaṃ tatra me sthitam ||

English

In the shrine14 of mine where this is properly recited every day, I will never abandon that place; my presence remains there.15

92.9

Sanskrit

बलिप्रदाने पूजायामग्निकार्ये महोत्सवे । सर्वं ममैतच्चरितमुच्चार्यं श्राव्यमेव च ॥

balipradāne pūjāyāmagnikārye mahotsave | sarvaṃ mamaitaccaritamuccāryaṃ śrāvyameva ca ||

English

At the offering of the bali16, at worship, at the fire rite17, and at a great festival, this whole account of my deeds is to be recited aloud and also to be heard.18

92.10

Sanskrit

जानताजानता वापि बलिपूजां तथा कृताम् । प्रतीच्छिष्याम्यहं प्रीत्या वह्निहोमं तथा कृतम् ॥

jānatājānatā vāpi balipūjāṃ tathā kṛtām | pratīcchiṣyāmyahaṃ prītyā vahnihomaṃ tathā kṛtam ||

English

Whether it is done knowingly or unknowingly, I will accept with pleasure the bali offering and worship performed in this way19, and the fire oblation20 performed in this way.21

92.11

Sanskrit

शरत्काले महापूजा क्रियते या च वार्षिकी । तस्यां ममैतन्माहात्म्यं श्रुत्वा भक्तिसमन्वितः ॥

śaratkāle mahāpūjā kriyate yā ca vārṣikī | tasyāṃ mamaitanmāhātmyaṃ śrutvā bhaktisamanvitaḥ ||

English

92.11–12In the great worship that is performed in the autumn season22, the one that comes every year, a man who hears this greatness23 of mine in it, filled with devotion, will become freed from every obstacle and endowed with wealth and grain, through my grace. There is no doubt.24

92.12

Sanskrit

सर्वबाधाविनिर्मृक्तो धनधान्यसमन्वितः । मनुष्यो मत्प्रसादेन भविष्यति न संशयः ॥

sarvabādhāvinirmṛkto dhanadhānyasamanvitaḥ | manuṣyo matprasādena bhaviṣyati na saṃśayaḥ ||

English

Its English is given with 92.11–12, above.

92.13

Sanskrit

श्रुत्वा ममैतन्माहात्म्यं तथोत्पत्तीः पृथक् शुभाः । पराक्रमं च युद्धेषु जायते निर्भयः पुमान् ॥

śrutvā mamaitanmāhātmyaṃ tathotpattīḥ pṛthak śubhāḥ | parākramaṃ ca yuddheṣu jāyate nirbhayaḥ pumān ||

English

Having heard this greatness of mine, and likewise [the accounts of] my auspicious births25, each one separately, and [my] prowess in battles, a man becomes fearless.26

92.14

Sanskrit

रिपवः संक्षयं यान्ति कल्याणं चोपपद्यते । नन्दते च कुलं पुंसां माहात्म्यं मम शृण्वताम् ॥

ripavaḥ saṃkṣayaṃ yānti kalyāṇaṃ copapadyate | nandate ca kulaṃ puṃsāṃ māhātmyaṃ mama śṛṇvatām ||

English

For men who listen to this greatness of mine, enemies go to utter destruction, welfare27 comes to them, and their family flourishes.28

92.15

Sanskrit

शान्तिकर्मणि सर्वत्र तथा दुः स्वप्नदर्शने । ग्रहपीडासु चोग्रासु माहात्म्यं शृणुयान्मम ॥

śāntikarmaṇi sarvatra tathā duḥ svapnadarśane | grahapīḍāsu cogrāsu māhātmyaṃ śṛṇuyānmama ||

English

In every rite of propitiation29, and likewise on seeing a bad dream, and in times of fierce affliction by the grahas30, one should listen to this greatness of mine.31

92.16

Sanskrit

उपसर्गाः शमं यान्ति ग्रहपीडाश्च दारुणाः । दुः स्वप्नं च नृभिर्दृष्टं सुस्वप्नमुपजायते ॥

upasargāḥ śamaṃ yānti grahapīḍāśca dāruṇāḥ | duḥ svapnaṃ ca nṛbhirdṛṣṭaṃ susvapnamupajāyate ||

English

Calamities subside, and the dreadful afflictions caused by the grahas; and when a bad dream has been seen by men, a good dream arises.32

92.17

Sanskrit

बालग्रहाभिभूतानां बालानां शान्तिकारकम् । संघातभेदे च नृणां मैत्रीकरणमुत्तमम् ॥

bālagrahābhibhūtānāṃ bālānāṃ śāntikārakam | saṃghātabhede ca nṛṇāṃ maitrīkaraṇamuttamam ||

English

92.17–19This entire greatness of mine is a bringer of peace to children overcome by the child-grahas33, and, when a rift arises within a company of men, the best maker of friendship. It is the supreme cause of loss of strength to all evil-doers without exception, and by recitation alone it is the destroyer of rakṣases34, bhūtas35 and piśācas. It brings about my presence.36

92.18

Sanskrit

दुर्वृत्तानामशेषाणां बलहानिकरं परम् । रक्षोभूतपिशाचानां पठनादेव नाशनम् ॥

durvṛttānāmaśeṣāṇāṃ balahānikaraṃ param | rakṣobhūtapiśācānāṃ paṭhanādeva nāśanam ||

English

Its English is given with 92.17–19, above.

92.19

Sanskrit

सर्वं ममैतन्माहात्म्यं मम सन्निधिकारकम् ॥

sarvaṃ mamaitanmāhātmyaṃ mama sannidhikārakam ||

English

Its English is given with 92.17–19, above.

92.20

Sanskrit

पशुपुष्पार्घ्यधूपैश्च गन्धदीपैस्तथोत्तमैः । विप्राणां भोजनैर्हेमैः प्रोक्षणीयैरहर्निशम् ॥

paśupuṣpārghyadhūpaiśca gandhadīpaistathottamaiḥ | viprāṇāṃ bhojanairhemaiḥ prokṣaṇīyairaharniśam ||

English

92.20–21The pleasure that is produced in me over a year by domestic animals, flowers, arghya37 and incense, and likewise by the finest perfumes and lamps, by feedings of brahmins, by gold, by things for sprinkling38, offered day and night, and by various other enjoyments and gifts, that same pleasure is produced in me when this well-told account39 is heard even once.40

92.21

Sanskrit

अन्यैश्च विविधैर्भोगैः प्रदानैर्वत्सरेण या । प्रीतिर्मे क्रियते सास्मिन् सकृत्सुचरिते श्रुते ॥

anyaiśca vividhairbhogaiḥ pradānairvatsareṇa yā | prītirme kriyate sāsmin sakṛtsucarite śrute ||

English

Its English is given with 92.20–21, above.

92.22

Sanskrit

श्रुतं हरति पापानि तथाऽरोग्यं प्रयच्छति । रक्षां करोति भूतेभ्यो जन्मनां कीर्तनं मम ॥

śrutaṃ harati pāpāni tathā'rogyaṃ prayacchati | rakṣāṃ karoti bhūtebhyo janmanāṃ kīrtanaṃ mama ||

English

When heard, the recital of my births41 takes away sins, grants freedom from disease, and gives protection from beings42.43

92.23

Sanskrit

युद्धेषु चरितं यन्मे दुष्टदैत्यनिबर्हणम् । तस्मिन् श्रुते वैरिकृतं भयं पुंसां न जायते ॥

yuddheṣu caritaṃ yanme duṣṭadaityanibarhaṇam | tasmin śrute vairikṛtaṃ bhayaṃ puṃsāṃ na jāyate ||

English

When that deed of mine in the battles44, the destruction of the wicked Daityas, is heard, fear caused by enemies does not arise in men.45

92.24

Sanskrit

युष्माभिः स्तुतयो याश्च याश्च ब्रह्मर्षिभिः कृताः । ब्रह्मणा च कृतास्तास्तु प्रयच्छन्ति शुभां गतिम् ॥

yuṣmābhiḥ stutayo yāśca yāśca brahmarṣibhiḥ kṛtāḥ | brahmaṇā ca kṛtāstāstu prayacchanti śubhāṃ gatim ||

English

The hymns of praise that were composed by you [devas]46, and those that were composed by the brahmin sages47, and those that were composed by Brahmā, bestow an auspicious destination.48

92.25

Sanskrit

अरण्ये प्रान्तरे वापि दावाग्निपरिवारितः । दस्युभिर्वा वृतः शून्ये गृहीतो वापि शत्रुभिः ॥

araṇye prāntare vāpi dāvāgniparivāritaḥ | dasyubhirvā vṛtaḥ śūnye gṛhīto vāpi śatrubhiḥ ||

English

92.25–26Whether in a forest or in a remote region, surrounded by a forest fire, or encircled by bandits49, or seized by enemies in a deserted place, or pursued by lions and tigers, or in the forest by wild elephants, or commanded by an angry king to be put to death50, or even held in bonds—51†

92.26

Sanskrit

सिंहव्याघ्रानुयातो वा वने वा वनहस्तिभिः । राज्ञा क्रुद्धेन चाज्ञप्तो वध्यो बन्धगतो ऽपि वा ॥

siṃhavyāghrānuyāto vā vane vā vanahastibhiḥ | rājñā kruddhena cājñapto vadhyo bandhagato 'pi vā ||

English

Its English is given with 92.25–26, above.

92.27

Sanskrit

आघूर्णितो वा वातेन स्थितः पोते महार्णवे । पतत्सु चापि शस्त्रेषु संग्रामे भृशदारुणे ॥

āghūrṇito vā vātena sthitaḥ pote mahārṇave | patatsu cāpi śastreṣu saṃgrāme bhṛśadāruṇe ||

English

92.27–28Or [when] tossed about by the wind while on a boat in the great ocean, or even while weapons fall in a battle exceedingly dreadful, [or] in all dire afflictions, or even tormented by pain, a man who remembers this deed of mine52 would be freed from peril53.54

92.28

Sanskrit

सर्वाबाधासु घोरासु वेदनाभ्यर्दितो ऽपि वा । स्मरन्ममैतच्छरितं नरो मुच्येत सङ्कटात् ॥

sarvābādhāsu ghorāsu vedanābhyardito 'pi vā | smaranmamaitaccharitaṃ naro mucyeta saṅkaṭāt ||

English

Its English is given with 92.27–28, above.

92.29

Sanskrit

मम प्रभावात्सिंहाद्या दस्यवो वैरिणस्तथा । दूरादेव पलायन्ते स्मरतश्चरितं मम ॥

mama prabhāvātsiṃhādyā dasyavo vairiṇastathā | dūrādeva palāyante smarataścaritaṃ mama ||

English

By my power, lions and the rest, bandits, and likewise enemies flee even from afar from one who remembers my deed.55†

Sanskrit

ऋषिरुवाच

ṛṣiruvāca

English

92.30The ṛṣi said: Having spoken thus, that Blessed One, Caṇḍikā56, of fierce prowess, vanished right there, while the devas looked on.57

92.30

Sanskrit

इत्युक्त्वा सा भगवती चण्डिका चण्डविक्रमा । पश्यतामेव देवानां तत्रैवान्तरधीयत ॥

ityuktvā sā bhagavatī caṇḍikā caṇḍavikramā | paśyatāmeva devānāṃ tatraivāntaradhīyata ||

English

Its English is given with 92.30, above.

92.31

Sanskrit

ते ऽपि देव्या निरातङ्का स्वाधिकारान् यथा पुरा । यज्ञभागभुजः सर्वे चक्रुर्विनिहतारयः ॥

te 'pi devyā nirātaṅkā svādhikārān yathā purā | yajñabhāgabhujaḥ sarve cakrurvinihatārayaḥ ||

English

They too, all the devas, free from fear because of the Goddess, their enemies slain, took up their own offices58 as before, enjoying their shares of the sacrifice59.60

92.32

Sanskrit

दैत्याश्च देव्या निहते शुम्भे देवरिपौ युधि । जगद्विध्वंसके तस्मिन् महोग्रे ऽतुलविक्रमे । निशुम्भे च महावीर्ये शेषाः पातालमाययुः ॥

daityāśca devyā nihate śumbhe devaripau yudhi | jagadvidhvaṃsake tasmin mahogre 'tulavikrame | niśumbhe ca mahāvīrye śeṣāḥ pātālamāyayuḥ ||

English

And when Śumbha61, the enemy of the devas, the destroyer of the world, so terrible and of matchless prowess, had been slain in battle by the Goddess, and Niśumbha too, of great vigor, the remaining Daityas went to the nether worlds.62

92.33

Sanskrit

एवं भगवती देवी सा नित्यापि पुनः पुनः । सम्भूय कुरुते भूप जगतः परिपालनम् ॥

evaṃ bhagavatī devī sā nityāpi punaḥ punaḥ | sambhūya kurute bhūpa jagataḥ paripālanam ||

English

Thus the Blessed One, the Goddess, though she is the eternal one, manifests herself again and again, O king63, and carries out the protection of the world.64

92.34

Sanskrit

तयैतन्मोह्यते विश्वं सैव विश्वं प्रसूयते । सा याचिता च विज्ञानं तुष्टा ऋद्धिं प्रयच्छति ॥

tayaitanmohyate viśvaṃ saiva viśvaṃ prasūyate | sā yācitā ca vijñānaṃ tuṣṭā ṛddhiṃ prayacchati ||

English

By her this universe is bewildered65, and she herself brings the universe forth. When she is entreated, she gives knowledge66; and when she is pleased, she bestows prosperity.67

92.35

Sanskrit

व्याप्तं तयैतत्सकलं ब्रह्माण्डं मनुजेश्वर । महाकाल्या महाकाले महाकारीस्वरूपया ॥

vyāptaṃ tayaitatsakalaṃ brahmāṇḍaṃ manujeśvara | mahākālyā mahākāle mahākārīsvarūpayā ||

English

This whole brahmāṇḍa68 has been pervaded by her, O lord of men, by Mahākālī69, who in the great time of dissolution70 has the nature of the Great Destroyer71.72

92.36

Sanskrit

सैव काले महामारी सैव सृष्टिर्भवत्यजा । स्थितिं करोति भूतानां सैव काले सनातनी ॥

saiva kāle mahāmārī saiva sṛṣṭirbhavatyajā | sthitiṃ karoti bhūtānāṃ saiva kāle sanātanī ||

English

In due time she herself becomes the epidemic plague; she herself, the unborn one73, becomes creation. And she herself, the everlasting one74, in due time brings about the preservation of beings.75

92.37

Sanskrit

भवकाले नृणां सैव लक्ष्मीर्वृद्धिप्रदा गृहे । सैवाभावे तथालक्ष्मीर्विनाशायोपजायते ॥

bhavakāle nṛṇāṃ saiva lakṣmīrvṛddhipradā gṛhe | saivābhāve tathālakṣmīrvināśāyopajāyate ||

English

In the time of prosperity76 she herself, as Lakṣmī, gives increase in the houses of men; and in the time of want she herself, likewise as Alakṣmī77, arises for their ruin.78

92.38

Sanskrit

स्तुता सम्पूजिता पुष्पैर्धूपगन्धादिबिस्तथा । ददाति वित्तं पुत्रांश्च मतिं धर्मे गतिं शुभाम् ॥

stutā sampūjitā puṣpairdhūpagandhādibistathā | dadāti vittaṃ putrāṃśca matiṃ dharme gatiṃ śubhām ||

English

When she is praised and well worshipped with flowers, incense, perfumes and the like79, she gives riches and sons, a mind set on dharma, and an auspicious destination.80

Sanskrit

इति श्रीमार्कण्डेयपुराणे सावर्णिके मन्वन्तरे देवीमाहात्म्ये देवीवाक्यं नाम द्विनवतितमो ऽध्यायः

iti śrīmārkaṇḍeyapurāṇe sāvarṇike manvantare devīmāhātmye devīvākyaṃ nāma dvinavatitamo 'dhyāyaḥ

English

Thus ends the ninety-second chapter, named "The Words of the Devī,"81 in the Devīmāhātmya, in the Sāvarṇika manvantara, in the revered82 Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa.83

  1. The ninety-second chapter.: This is a chapter heading, not a verse of the narrative. The numbering in the e-text (chapter 92 here, 91 in the preceding colophon) differs from the verse numbers given in the reference. ↩
  2. The Devī said:: The speaker is the Goddess, who continues the speech begun in the preceding chapter. ↩
  3. Mahiṣa, the asura: The compound is mahiṣāsura-ghātana, 'the slaying of the asura Mahiṣa'; the glossary equates Mahiṣāsura with Mahiṣa. ↩
  4. lunar days: The Sanskrit has only the ordinal numerals (aṣṭamī, caturdaśī, navamī), feminine locatives agreeing with tithi, the lunar day, which is implied; the days are those of the fortnight, in either half of the month. ↩
  5. this supreme greatness of mine: māhātmya: greatness, glory; it also names the Devīmāhātmya itself, the text in which this speech stands. ↩
  6. The reading: kīrtayiṣyinti (92.2) is an irregular form, perhaps for kīrtayiṣyanti; read as the future 'will recount'. In 92.4, dāridrayam stands for dāridryam, 'poverty'. Madhukaiṭabhānāśam is read as madhu-kaiṭabha-nāśam, 'the destruction of Madhu and Kaiṭabha', the sandhi being irregular in the e-text. Also in 92.3 the days could be taken to fall in one fortnight or in both; the text does not say, so the translation does not specify. ↩
  7. The reading: The verse is a single sentence continuing from 92.4. 'tasya' is singular, whereas 92.4 has 'teṣām' (plural); I render 'for him' as the text has it. 'śastrānalatoyaughāt' is a compound that can be read as 'weapons, fire, and a flood of water' (dvandva of śastra, anala, toya-ogha) or as 'a flood of weapons, fire and water'; I chose the former as the more natural reading. 'na ... na rājataḥ' is read as 'or from a king' (dasyuto vā na rājataḥ), continuing the series of fears. ↩
  8. the attentive: The e-text reads sahāhitaiḥ, which does not give sense. The translation follows the emendation samāhitaiḥ, 'by those with collected minds', the word used in 92.1 (samāhitaḥ). ↩
  9. svastyayana: A rite, text or recitation that secures well-being and averts misfortune; here the Māhātmya itself is said to be such a means of protection. ↩
  10. The reading: sahāhitaiḥ, as printed, is doubtful. (1) saha + ahitaiḥ, 'together with enemies', is senseless here. (2) saha + āhitaiḥ, or sa-hitaiḥ, 'together with well-wishers', is possible but strained, because saha would then need a companion word that the line does not supply. (3) The e-text probably misprints samāhitaiḥ, 'by the collected, attentive'. This is the likeliest reading, since samāhitaḥ occurs at 92.1 and sa-/saha- is an easy scribal confusion, and it is the one I chose. My earlier draft treated saha as a loose connective, which was unfounded and is withdrawn. ↩
  11. epidemic plague: mahāmārī, literally 'great death-dealer', denotes a great pestilence or epidemic; it is also a name of the Goddess as the sender of plague. ↩
  12. portent of three kinds: The threefold utpāta is traditionally taken as portents of the sky (divya), of the air or atmosphere (āntarikṣa), and of the earth (bhauma). ↩
  13. The reading: The Sanskrit śamayet is optative, 'may it pacify' or 'it should pacify'; I chose the wishing sense, 'may', as fitting the promise of a text's efficacy. The particle tu is left untranslated as merely connective. The 'threefold' division of the portent is not given in the verse, so the note offers only the traditional reading. ↩
  14. shrine: āyatana: a sacred dwelling or temple of the Goddess, where her presence is held to abide. ↩
  15. The reading: The text reads 'sadā na tadvimokṣyāmi'. Tad can be taken as 'that [place]' (object of vimokṣyāmi) or as part of a compound; I took it as 'that place', with tatra repeating the locative. Also samyaṅ-nityam could be read as 'properly and constantly' (samyak + nityam); I took samyak as 'properly' and nityam as 'every day'. The sandhi 'tadvimokṣyāmi' is read as tad vimokṣyāmi, with the verb 'abandon' from vi-muc; the first line's yatra is correlated with tatra in the second. ↩
  16. bali: An offering of food or other oblation presented to deities and spirits, distinct from the fire oblation (homa). ↩
  17. fire rite: Literally 'work of fire' (agnikārya): a rite performed with the sacrificial fire, such as an oblation (homa). ↩
  18. The reading: The verse has no finite verb. 'Is to be' is supplied from the gerundives uccāryam and śrāvyam, so I have not bracketed it. 'Caritam' is rendered 'account of my deeds' (glossary: carita = deeds), referring to the recital of the Devī's exploits. ↩
  19. performed in this way: tathā refers back to the recitation of the Devī's deeds enjoined in the previous verse as accompanying the rites; the participle kṛtām agrees with balipūjām, and kṛtam with vahnihomam. ↩
  20. fire oblation: Translates vahnihoma, an oblation (homa) poured into the sacred fire; vahni is a name of Agni. ↩
  21. The reading: balipūjām may be read as a dvandva (bali and pūjā, 'the bali offering and worship') or as a tatpuruṣa ('worship by means of the bali offering'). I chose the dvandva, which matches the two items named in the previous verse. jānatājānatā vā is a singular instrumental of the participles with api, 'whether knowingly or unknowingly', agreeing with the implied performer. ↩
  22. the great worship that is performed in the autumn season: The annual autumn worship of the Goddess (śāradīya mahāpūjā, Durgā-pūjā), held in the month of Āśvina. ↩
  23. greatness: māhātmya, here the text of the Devī's deeds, as in the preceding verses. ↩
  24. The reading: vārṣikī literally means 'yearly, annual' (from varṣa, 'year'), not 'rainy'. I read it as 'annual', which fits the autumn festival. The alternative 'of the rains' is less likely beside śaratkāle. The text has tasyām, 'in it', referring back to the worship. I render 'in the great worship'. ↩
  25. my auspicious births: utpattīḥ: the several manifestations of the Goddess told in the Devīmāhātmya (Mahākālī/Mahāsarasvatī/Mahālakṣmī, and the forms born to slay the demons). ↩
  26. The reading: utpattīḥ could mean 'origins/births' or 'manifestations'; I kept 'births'. The bracketed 'accounts of' is implied since a man hears the narratives rather than the births themselves. pṛthak could also be taken with śubhāḥ ('separately auspicious'); I took it with utpattīḥ, 'each one separately'. ↩
  27. welfare: Sanskrit kalyāṇa, neuter: auspiciousness, well-being; it is not the goddess epithet Kalyāṇī, though it is related in sense. ↩
  28. The reading: The genitive plurals puṃsām and śṛṇvatām are taken together as a possessive-and-agent phrase ("for men who listen"). The verb upapadyate is rendered "comes to [them]" with the dative sense of puṃsām implied. It could also be read as "is attained" or "occurs". ↩
  29. rite of propitiation: śāntikarma: a rite performed to avert evil, calm danger or remove ill omens. ↩
  30. grahas: graha: literally 'seizer'; a planet or a demonic influence thought to seize and afflict people. ↩
  31. The reading: The e-text reads 'duḥ svapna-' as two separate words; it is the compound duḥsvapnadarśane, 'on seeing a bad dream'. Graha may mean planets or seizing spirits; I kept it in IAST. ↩
  32. The reading: Upajāyate and susvapnam: I now take susvapnam as the subject of upajāyate, 'a good dream arises'. Duḥsvapnaṃ ... nṛbhir dṛṣṭam is then the bad dream seen by men, and the instrumental nṛbhiḥ is the agent of dṛṣṭam. The earlier 'turns into' made susvapnam a predicate, which the verb does not require. One could still read the bad dream itself as the subject, with susvapnam as what it becomes. I rejected that reading because upajāyate means 'arises', not 'becomes', and the instrumental does not support it. Grahapīḍāḥ is taken as a tatpuruṣa, 'afflictions caused by the grahas'. It shares the verb yānti with upasargāḥ, so no repeated verb is needed. ↩
  33. child-grahas: bālagraha: the seizing spirits believed to afflict infants and children. ↩
  34. rakṣases: rakṣas: a class of hostile, often night-roaming, beings; commonly rendered 'demons'. The Sanskrit compound has the stem rakṣas, not rākṣasa. ↩
  35. bhūtas: Here bhūta is a class of hostile spirits, as against the general sense 'living beings'. The piśācas are flesh-eating demons, regularly named with them. ↩
  36. The reading: (1) saṃghātabheda: a tatpuruṣa 'split in a company/assemblage' (chosen, with nṛṇām going with the company, as the reviewer suggests) or 'a breaking of a union'. Both give much the same sense; the locative is a time or occasion for maitrīkaraṇa. (2) param in 92.18: I take it as 'supreme', agreeing with balahānikaram, and keep 'cause of loss of strength', since balahānikara means 'maker of loss of strength'; 'diminisher of strength' would also be right but the literal form is kept. (3) bhūta is kept as a spirit class here, though the glossary gives 'living beings'; rakṣas is now written rakṣases, not 'rākṣasas', following the compound's stem. (4) sarvam is rendered once, as 'entire', in the subject; the reviewer's point about its doubling is accepted. ↩
  37. arghya: The water, usually with flowers, grain and the like, offered to a deity or honoured guest as a mark of reverence. ↩
  38. things for sprinkling: prokṣaṇīya: water or other substances used for ritual sprinkling and consecration. ↩
  39. well-told account: sucarite, locative absolute with śrute: literally 'this good deed (or account) having been heard'. It refers to the Devī's exploits recited in this text. ↩
  40. The reading: sucarite can mean 'well-conducted deed' (the Devī's exploits) or 'well-told account'. I chose 'well-told account' because it is what is heard (śrute). hemaiḥ is read as 'gold' (offered as gifts), though 'golden vessels' is possible. paśu is rendered 'domestic animals', following the glossary, and presumably refers to animals offered in sacrifice. ↩
  41. my births: The Goddess's successive manifestations, as told in the preceding narratives of this Māhātmya. ↩
  42. beings: bhūtebhyaḥ is ablative: protection 'from' harmful beings, here hostile spirits and creatures; compare bhūta in 92.18. ↩
  43. The reading: janmanām: the form is irregular (expected janmanāṃ, genitive plural of janman), and it may be a corruption. I take it as the genitive plural 'of births', construed with kīrtanam. The word śrutam, 'heard', is neuter and agrees with kīrtanam, which gives 'when heard'. The verb forms are singular throughout, so the single subject is kīrtanam. ↩
  44. that deed of mine in the battles: The Devī's exploits against the asuras, told earlier in the Devī Māhātmya: the slaying of Madhu and Kaiṭabha, Mahiṣa, and Śumbha and Niśumbha with their hosts. ↩
  45. The reading: yanme is yad + me, 'which of mine' (genitive of the Goddess speaking), agreeing with caritam. tasmin śrute is a locative absolute ('when that is heard'). vairikṛtam could also be read 'made by enemies' or 'arising from enemies'; the sense is the same. ↩
  46. [devas]: yuṣmābhiḥ is the plural 'you', here the devas who praised the Goddess earlier in the text; the speaker is the Goddess. ↩
  47. brahmin sages: brahmarṣi: a sage of brahmin birth who has attained the highest rank of seers. ↩
  48. The reading: The Sanskrit does not specify who 'you' are. I take the speaker as the Goddess addressing the devas, who sang the earlier hymns, and supply 'devas' in brackets. 'Śubhāṃ gatim' may mean either a good course or a good state after death; I render it 'auspicious destination'. ↩
  49. bandits: Sanskrit dasyu; the glossary already explains it. ↩
  50. commanded by an angry king to be put to death: vadhyaḥ is a gerundive: 'one who is to be killed'. It is taken with ājñaptaḥ, 'commanded'; the man is the one condemned. ↩
  51. The reading: Prāntara is taken as 'remote region' (a lonely tract, or a road through wilderness); some read it as 'a long, lonely road'. Śūnye is taken as 'in a deserted place' and goes with the enemies' seizing, but it could also qualify the earlier 'encircled by bandits'. The verb is not given in these verses; the construction continues in 92.28 (naro mucyeta), so the man is the implied subject. Siṃhavyāghrānuyātaḥ is a nominative singular compound, 'followed by lions and tigers'. ↩
  52. this deed of mine: The text reads 'maitaccharitam' by sandhi, i.e. mama etat caritam. The singular refers to the Goddess's account just recited, not to one single exploit. ↩
  53. peril: saṅkaṭa, literally a narrow or constricted place, hence a strait, a dangerous predicament. ↩
  54. The reading: The reviewer is right on the main point. sarvābādhāsu ghorāsu are locatives standing as their own condition, 'in all dire afflictions'. They do not qualify vedanābhyardito 'pi vā, which is a further alternative: 'or even tormented by pain'. I have changed the draft accordingly, rendered 'pi as 'even', and put 'all' for sarva. Verse 92.27 also has a repeated 'even' for ca api in patatsu cāpi. For saṅkaṭāt I keep 'peril', which the reviewer accepts as adequate, and explain the literal sense of 'narrow place' in a note. 'Would be freed' renders the optative mucyeta. 'Caritam' (printed 'ccharitam') is a regular sandhi form of smaran mama etat caritam and is not corrupt. ↩
  55. The reading: smarataḥ is the genitive singular of a present participle ('of one who remembers'), with no noun expressed. I take it as the person from whom they flee, construed with palāyante. It could instead be a genitive absolute ('as one remembers my deed'). The sense is the same, and I keep the simpler construction. I accept the reviewer's points on dūrād eva (rendered 'even from afar'), on siṃhādyāḥ (rendered 'lions and the rest', with no 'beasts' added, so no note is needed) and on carita. The Sanskrit carita is singular, as in 92.28 ('this deed of mine'), so I render it 'my deed' rather than the glossary's plural 'deeds'. ↩
  56. Caṇḍikā: The Fierce One, a name of the Goddess; it is picked up in the epithet caṇḍavikramā, 'of fierce prowess'. ↩
  57. The reading: Verse number 765.1 is a misnumbering in the e-text for the speaker's line 'ṛṣiruvāca'; it is treated as the opening of the sentence. The Sanskrit has 'sā bhagavatī caṇḍikā caṇḍavikramā', and 'caṇḍa' in the epithet is not the asura Caṇḍa of the glossary but the adjective 'fierce'. ↩
  58. their own offices: Svādhikāra: the proper functions or domains of each deva (Indra's sovereignty, Sūrya's, Agni's and so on), which Śumbha and Niśumbha had seized. ↩
  59. enjoying their shares of the sacrifice: Yajñabhāgabhuj: one who partakes of the portion (bhāga) of the offering assigned to the gods in the yajña; the devas' share, which the asuras had usurped. ↩
  60. The reading: The Sanskrit reads 'nirātaṅkā' (feminine/singular form) with 'te sarve' (masculine plural); it should be nirātaṅkāḥ, a sandhi or print error. I read it as nirātaṅkāḥ, 'free from fear', agreeing with te. 'Te' is not named, but from 92.30 the devas are meant. 'Devyā' is read as ablative of cause ('because of the Goddess'); an instrumental of agency, 'by the Goddess [made fearless]', is the alternative sense. ↩
  61. Śumbha: Brother of Niśumbha; with him the principal asura foe of the Goddess in this episode. ↩
  62. The reading: The locatives nihate śumbhe ... niśumbhe form a locative absolute; the second 'nihate' is understood with niśumbhe ca. The adjectives jagadvidhvaṃsake, mahogre, atulavikrame go with śumbha (tasmin), while mahāvīrye qualifies Niśumbha. Alternatively tasmin ... atulavikrame could be read with both brothers; I take them with Śumbha because of the position of ca. 'Devī' is rendered 'the Goddess' as in the preceding verses. ↩
  63. O king: The vocative bhūpa addresses the king Suratha, to whom the ṛṣi is speaking. ↩
  64. The reading: The vocative bhūpa is taken to be addressed to King Suratha, the listener in the frame story. It is not named in this verse, and the identification rests on the frame narrative. The glossary note says that prabhu is the prince Puṣkaramālin, which suggests a different frame in the glossary's source, so this identification should be checked against the text. sambhūya is rendered as 'manifests herself'; the literal sense is 'having come into being' or 'having arisen'. ↩
  65. bewildered: mohyate is the passive of the causative of muh: the universe is made to be deluded by her, as by her power of illusion (māyā). ↩
  66. knowledge: Sanskrit vijñāna, a stronger form of jñāna: discriminating or higher knowledge, not mere perception. ↩
  67. The reading: vijñānam: 'knowledge' is chosen over 'discriminating knowledge' or 'realization'; the verse does not specify. prasūyate is a passive form in the Sanskrit (literally 'is brought forth by her'), but with saiva as agent it is taken as 'she herself brings forth', the usual sense of this Purāṇic idiom. Some read it as 'is born from her itself'; the sense is the same. ↩
  68. brahmāṇḍa: 'Egg of Brahmā'; the cosmic egg, the whole universe. ↩
  69. Mahākālī: 'Great Black One' or 'Great Time'; a form of the Goddess, here linked with the time of cosmic dissolution. ↩
  70. in the great time of dissolution: mahākāle is a locative; it can also be read as 'in Mahākāla's [time]' or 'at the great time'. Dissolution is the sense implied by the context. ↩
  71. the Great Destroyer: mahākārī is obscure; the reading is uncertain. The verse after speaks of her as Mahāmārī, 'the Great Plague' or 'Great Death'. ↩
  72. The reading: mahākāle: read as a locative, 'at the great time' (of dissolution), chosen over a reading as a vocative or as a reference to Mahākāla. mahākārīsvarūpayā: the text is probably corrupt or irregular. It may be mahākāla-ī-svarūpayā, or mahā-kārī (maker, destroyer), or a mistake for mahāmārī-svarūpayā, 'having the form of the Great Plague', as the next verse suggests (mahāmārī). I kept the transmitted reading, 'having the nature of the Great Destroyer', and flagged the problem. The unglossed 'in the great time' is rendered as [the time of dissolution]; I used no brackets and let the note explain it. ↩
  73. the unborn one: ajā: 'unborn', said of the Goddess as the eternal power who is never herself produced, though she becomes creation. ↩
  74. the everlasting one: sanātanī: 'everlasting, primeval', feminine of sanātana; a title of the Goddess. ↩
  75. The reading: kāle is taken as 'in due time' (locative of kāla), parallel to 92.35's mahākāle; 'at the proper time' for each function. Sthiti is rendered 'preservation' (maintenance of the world). The glossary's mahāmārī, 'epidemic plague', is kept. In 'saiva sṛṣṭir bhavaty ajā', ajā is a nominative epithet of the subject, not a predicate. ↩
  76. In the time of prosperity: bhavakāle is read as 'time of becoming, of well-being' (bhava); an alternative reading is 'time of existence'; the contrast with abhāve ('in want') supports 'prosperity'. ↩
  77. Alakṣmī: Lakṣmī's opposite, the personification of misfortune and want; the text reads tathālakṣmīḥ, 'likewise Alakṣmī'. ↩
  78. The reading: bhavakāle: 'time of prosperity' (bhava as well-being, contrasted with abhāva) or 'time of birth/existence'. I chose 'prosperity' because abhāve is its counterpart and the sense is of increase versus ruin. abhāve is rendered 'in the time of want', taking it as the opposite of bhava. ↩
  79. incense, perfumes and the like: The text reads dhūpagandhādibiḥ, an irregular instrumental plural for dhūpagandhādibhiḥ; the sense is 'with incense, perfumes and other [offerings]'. ↩
  80. The reading: dhūpagandhādibiḥ is metrically and grammatically irregular (for -ādibhiḥ); taken as instrumental plural 'with incense, perfumes, etc.'. The feminine subject (stutā, sampūjitā) is the Goddess, understood from the preceding verses. 'gatiṃ śubhām' could mean a good course in life or a good state after death; I keep the neutral 'auspicious destination'. 'Dharme' is taken with 'matim' as 'a mind [set] on dharma'. ↩
  81. named "The Words of the Devī,": The chapter number is 92 in the text's own count (dvinavatitama), although the reference given for this colophon is chapter 87; the verses before are numbered 92.33-38. The title devīvākyam is the colophon's name for this chapter, which gives the Goddess's own account of her manifestations. ↩
  82. revered: śrī- is the honorific prefix to the title of the work, not a separate word of praise. ↩
  83. The reading: The reference gives chapter 87, but the colophon itself and the preceding verses (92.33-38) show it is the ninety-second chapter; I translate the Sanskrit's dvinavatitama as 'ninety-second'. Devīvākyam is rendered 'The Words of the Devī' (vākya: speech, utterance), the colophon title. ↩

Adhyāya 93

Sanskrit

त्रिनवतितमो ऽध्यायः- ९३

trinavatitamo 'dhyāyaḥ- 93

English

The ninety-third chapter: 93.1

Sanskrit

ऋषिरुवाच

ṛṣiruvāca

English

93.1The ṛṣi said: This excellent Devīmāhātmya2 has been told to you, O king. Such is the power of that Devī, by whom this world is sustained.3

93.1

Sanskrit

एतत्ते कथितं भूप देवीमाहात्म्यमुत्तमम् । एवंप्रभावा सा देवी ययेदं धार्यते जगत् ॥

etatte kathitaṃ bhūpa devīmāhātmyamuttamam | evaṃprabhāvā sā devī yayedaṃ dhāryate jagat ||

English

Its English is given with 93.1, above.

93.2

Sanskrit

विद्या तथैव क्रियते भगवद्विष्णुमायया । तथा त्वमेष वैश्यश्च तथैवान्ये विवेकिनः । मोह्यन्ते मोहिताश्चैव मोहमेष्यन्ति चापरे ॥

vidyā tathaiva kriyate bhagavadviṣṇumāyayā | tathā tvameṣa vaiśyaśca tathaivānye vivekinaḥ | mohyante mohitāścaiva mohameṣyanti cāpare ||

English

Knowledge too is brought about4 by the blessed Viṣṇumāyā. In the same way you, this Vaiśya, and other men of discernment likewise are being deluded; they have been deluded, and others will fall into delusion.5

93.3

Sanskrit

तामुपैहि महाराज शरणं परमेश्वरीम् । आराधिता सैव नृणां भोगस्वर्गापवर्गदा ॥

tāmupaihi mahārāja śaraṇaṃ parameśvarīm | ārādhitā saiva nṛṇāṃ bhogasvargāpavargadā ||

English

Go to her for refuge, O Mahārāja6, to the supreme sovereign lady. When she is propitiated, she alone grants men enjoyment, heaven and final liberation7.8

Sanskrit

मार्कण्डेय उवाच

mārkaṇḍeya uvāca

English

93.4–5Mārkaṇḍeya said: Having thus heard his words, that King Suratha, the lord of men, bowed low to that greatly fortunate ṛṣi of praised vows9. Disgusted by his excessive mamatva and by the seizure of his kingdom, he went at once for the sake of tapas, and so did that Vaiśya, O great sage10.11

93.4

Sanskrit

इति तस्य वचः श्रुत्वा सुरथः स नराधिपः । प्रणिपत्य महाभागं तमृषिं शंसितव्रतम् ॥

iti tasya vacaḥ śrutvā surathaḥ sa narādhipaḥ | praṇipatya mahābhāgaṃ tamṛṣiṃ śaṃsitavratam ||

English

Its English is given with 93.4–5, above.

93.5

Sanskrit

निर्विण्णो ऽतिममत्वेन राज्यापहरणेन च । जगम सद्यस्तपसे स च वैश्यो महामुने ॥

nirviṇṇo 'timamatvena rājyāpaharaṇena ca | jagama sadyastapase sa ca vaiśyo mahāmune ||

English

Its English is given with 93.4–5, above.

93.6

Sanskrit

संदर्शनार्थमम्बाया नदीपुलिनसंस्थितः । स च वैश्यस्तपस्तेपे देवीसूक्तं परं जपन् ॥

saṃdarśanārthamambāyā nadīpulinasaṃsthitaḥ | sa ca vaiśyastapastepe devīsūktaṃ paraṃ japan ||

English

And that Vaiśya too, settled on a river bank, practised tapas in order to behold the Mother, muttering the supreme Devīsūkta12.13

93.7

Sanskrit

तौ तस्मिन् पिलिने देव्याः कृत्वा मूर्ति महीमयीम् । अर्हणां चक्रतुस्तस्याः पुष्पधूपाग्नितर्पणैः ॥

tau tasmin piline devyāḥ kṛtvā mūrti mahīmayīm | arhaṇāṃ cakratustasyāḥ puṣpadhūpāgnitarpaṇaiḥ ||

English

The two of them, on that sandbank14, made an image of the Goddess out of earth and performed worship to her with offerings of flowers, incense, fire and libations15.16

93.8

Sanskrit

निराहारौ यतात्मानौ तन्मनस्कौ समाहितौ । ददतुस्तौ बलिं चैव निजगात्रासृगुक्षितम् ॥

nirāhārau yatātmānau tanmanaskau samāhitau | dadatustau baliṃ caiva nijagātrāsṛgukṣitam ||

English

Eating no food, with their minds restrained, their thoughts fixed on her and their attention composed, the two of them gave a bali sprinkled with blood from their own bodies17.

93.9

Sanskrit

एवं समाराधयतोस्त्रिभिर्वर्षैर्यतात्मनोः । परितुष्टा जगद्धात्री प्रत्यक्षं प्राह चण्डिका ॥

evaṃ samārādhayatostribhirvarṣairyatātmanoḥ | parituṣṭā jagaddhātrī pratyakṣaṃ prāha caṇḍikā ||

English

When the two, with restrained minds, had worshipped her in this way for three years, Caṇḍikā, the sustainer of the world18, was fully pleased and spoke to them face to face.19

Sanskrit

श्रीदेव्युवाच

śrīdevyuvāca

English

93.10The Blessed Goddess said: "Whatever is asked for by you, O king, and by you, O delight of your family20, let all of that be received from me. I am fully pleased, and I give it."21

93.10

Sanskrit

यत्प्रार्थ्यते त्वया भूप त्वया च कुलनन्दन । मत्तस्तत्प्राप्यतां सर्वं परितुष्टा ददामि तत् ॥

yatprārthyate tvayā bhūpa tvayā ca kulanandana | mattastatprāpyatāṃ sarvaṃ parituṣṭā dadāmi tat ||

English

Its English is given with 93.10, above.

Sanskrit

मार्कण्डेय उवाच

mārkaṇḍeya uvāca

English

93.11Mārkaṇḍeya said: Then the king chose a kingdom that would not fall away in another birth, and, right here, his own kingdom, with the strength of its enemies destroyed, by force.22

93.11

Sanskrit

ततो वव्रे नृपो राज्यमविभ्रंश्यन्यजन्मनि । अत्रैव च निजं राज्यं हतशत्रुबलं बलात् ॥

tato vavre nṛpo rājyamavibhraṃśyanyajanmani | atraiva ca nijaṃ rājyaṃ hataśatrubalaṃ balāt ||

English

Its English is given with 93.11, above.

93.12

Sanskrit

सो ऽपि वैश्यस्ततो ज्ञानं वव्रे निर्विण्णमानसः । ममेत्यहमिति प्राज्ञः सङ्गविच्युतिकारकम् ॥

so 'pi vaiśyastato jñānaṃ vavre nirviṇṇamānasaḥ | mametyahamiti prājñaḥ saṅgavicyutikārakam ||

English

The Vaiśya too, then, wise and with a mind turned away from the world, chose knowledge23, which brings about release from attachment, that is, from the thoughts "mine" and "I."2425

Sanskrit

श्रीदेव्युवाच

śrīdevyuvāca

English

93.13The Blessed Goddess said: "In a few days, O king, you yourself will gain your own kingdom. When you have killed [your] enemies, [your rule] there will be unfailing26 for you."27

93.13

Sanskrit

स्वल्पैरहोभिर्नृपते स्वं राज्यं प्राप्स्यते भवान् । हत्वा रिपूनस्खलितं तव तत्र भविष्यति ॥

svalpairahobhirnṛpate svaṃ rājyaṃ prāpsyate bhavān | hatvā ripūnaskhalitaṃ tava tatra bhaviṣyati ||

English

Its English is given with 93.13, above.

93.14

Sanskrit

मृतश्च भूयः सम्प्राप्य जन्म देवाद्विवस्वतः । सावर्णिको नाम मनुर्भवान् भुवि भविष्यति ॥

mṛtaśca bhūyaḥ samprāpya janma devādvivasvataḥ | sāvarṇiko nāma manurbhavān bhuvi bhaviṣyati ||

English

And when you have died, and have again obtained birth from the god Vivasvat28, you will be the Manu named Sāvarṇika29 on earth.30

93.15

Sanskrit

वैश्यवर्य त्वया यश्च वरो ऽस्मत्तो ऽभिवाञ्छतः । तं प्रयच्छामि संसिद्ध्यै तव ज्ञानं भविष्यति ॥

vaiśyavarya tvayā yaśca varo 'smatto 'bhivāñchataḥ | taṃ prayacchāmi saṃsiddhyai tava jñānaṃ bhaviṣyati ||

English

O best of Vaiśyas31, I grant you the boon that you wish from me; for your full accomplishment32, knowledge will be yours.33

Sanskrit

मार्कण्डेय उवाच

mārkaṇḍeya uvāca

English

93.16Mārkaṇḍeya said: Having thus given the two of them the boon as each had desired, the Devī, praised by them with devotion, at once became invisible.34

93.16

Sanskrit

इति दत्त्वा तयोर्देवी यथाभिलषितं वरम् । बभूवान्तर्हिता सद्यो भक्त्या ताभ्यामभिष्टुता ॥

iti dattvā tayordevī yathābhilaṣitaṃ varam | babhūvāntarhitā sadyo bhaktyā tābhyāmabhiṣṭutā ||

English

Its English is given with 93.16, above.

93.17

Sanskrit

एवं देव्या वरं लब्ध्वा सुरथः क्षत्रियर्षभः । सूर्याज्जन्म समासाद्य सावर्णिर्भविता मनुः ॥

evaṃ devyā varaṃ labdhvā surathaḥ kṣatriyarṣabhaḥ | sūryājjanma samāsādya sāvarṇirbhavitā manuḥ ||

English

Thus, having obtained a boon from the Devī, Suratha, the bull among Kṣatriyas35, will obtain birth from Sūrya and become the Manu Sāvarṇi36.37

Sanskrit

इति श्रीमार्कण्डेयमहापुराणे सावर्णिके मन्वन्तरे देवीमाहात्म्ये वरप्रदानं नाम त्रिनवतितमो ऽध्यायः

iti śrīmārkaṇḍeyamahāpurāṇe sāvarṇike manvantare devīmāhātmye varapradānaṃ nāma trinavatitamo 'dhyāyaḥ

English

Thus ends the ninety-third chapter38, named "The Granting of the Boon," in the Devīmāhātmya39, in the Sāvarṇika manvantara40, in the revered Mārkaṇḍeya Mahāpurāṇa.41
